Nothing was written or run. Everything below is facts and options, and I decide nothing. Once I edit them, I will record in the handoff that origin/main holds f10943d, which contradicts “nothing is pushed”, and in known-limitations that the history was already public before the SEC-1 scan.

Step 2: backup trigger (ADR-0002)
What exists now:

The backup sidecar runs backup-loop.sh in all 3 pods, using the same image as postgres. It only acts when pg_is_in_recovery() is false.
It already ran stanza-create against the database (BK-1), so it reaches Postgres over the socket.
Its limits are 128Mi memory and 10m CPU. It mounts /pgdata read-only, and it has /tmp as an emptyDir and the S3 credentials.
Whichever option you pick, retention (BK-3) is a pgBackRest setting in the environment ConfigMap, and pgbackrest verify can run in the same place as the backup.
Option 1: scheduler in the sidecar

Adds: schedule logic in sh, inside the pgbackrest-scripts ConfigMap, plus env variables for the intervals.
The full-or-incremental decision could be read from pgbackrest info --output=json (last backup time). The state would then live in the repository, not the pod. This is a design idea, not tested.
Adds nothing else: no image, no RBAC, no certificates, no new port.
Failover: the new leader’s sidecar takes over with no extra setup (prediction).
Costs:
The backup’s CPU, I/O and memory sit in the leader pod beside Postgres. 128Mi may be too small for a backup process (prediction).
Whether the read-only /pgdata mount is enough for pgbackrest backup is untested. The handoff also lists it as untested (prediction).
Failure shows only in sidecar logs, so “backup too old” in OBS-2 needs a metric built from pgbackrest info.
If two pods briefly both think they lead, pgBackRest’s lock is, as far as I know, a file on the local host, so it would not stop two overlapping backups (prediction).
The SPEC word “CronJob” would no longer be literally true.
Test: the intervals come from env variables, so bk-schedule.sh can use short ones. Changing them restarts the pods.
Option 2: CronJob with pgBackRest TLS server mode

Adds:
A pgbackrest server process in each Postgres pod, as a new sidecar. It needs the data volume and the socket directory.
A TLS CA plus server and client certificates, generated at make up by a new script, test first.
Per-pod host entries (pg1-host, pg2-host, pg3-host) for the CronJob pod, which acts as the repository host.
A new port, which needs a NetworkPolicy allow later (SEC-3).
Version: this mode needs a pgBackRest version of about 2.37 or later (prediction). I have not seen the pinned version.
Primary selection: as far as I know, pgBackRest picks the primary among several pg hosts. Not verified here.
Gains: Kubernetes provides the schedule, concurrencyPolicy: Forbid, and Job history and status. That status is the natural source for the “backup too old” alert. The SPEC word “CronJob” stays true.
Costs: the most new parts. There is no rotation procedure for the certificates, the same gap as the object store. Backup data passes through the job pod. The file reading and compression still happen in the Postgres pod, as far as I know.
Option 3: CronJob that runs kubectl exec

Adds:
A kubectl image pinned by tag and digest in versions.env (OBJ-1 style, test first).
A ServiceAccount and Role in pg-ha with pods/exec, plus get and list on pods.
A way to find the leader pod, either by pod label or by patronictl list. I have not seen 30-services.yaml, so I do not know which label Patroni sets here.
Costs:
pods/exec on the database pods exposes everything in the container, including the secrets injected as env variables. That is in tension with SEC-4 (least privilege).
AGENTS.md rule 3 only forbids manual exec steps, and this would be automated. Whether it fits is your call.
The backup runs inside the postgres container, under its 768Mi limit shared with Postgres.
What happens to a running backup if the exec stream breaks is not known (prediction).
Gains: the same Job status, schedule and “CronJob” word as option 2, with no certificates and no new sidecar process.
Step 3: SPEC wording
The BK-2 and RS-3 entries are two lines each. The second line, the test and evidence line, stays as it is in every proposal.

BK-2, current (text only, do not run):

- BK-2: Scheduled full and incremental backups (CronJob).
  Test: `tests/bk-schedule.sh`. Evidence: `docs/evidence/BK-2.txt`
BK-2, proposed, option 2 or 3 (CronJob): no change to the line.

BK-2, proposed, option 1 (sidecar) (text only, do not run):

- BK-2: Scheduled full and incremental backups (scheduler in the backup sidecar of the Postgres pods).
  Test: `tests/bk-schedule.sh`. Evidence: `docs/evidence/BK-2.txt`
Incrementals are tagged MINE, so dropping “and incremental” is a separate choice for you.

RS-3, current (text only, do not run):

- RS-3: Scheduled restore test to a scratch instance, with a data check.
  Test: `tests/rs-scheduled.sh`. Evidence: `docs/evidence/RS-3.txt`
RS-3, proposed, on-demand (text only, do not run):

- RS-3: Restore test to a scratch instance, run on demand by one script, with a data check.
  Test: `tests/rs-restore-test.sh`. Evidence: `docs/evidence/RS-3.txt`
RS-3, proposed, scheduled (text only, do not run):

- RS-3: Scheduled restore test to a scratch instance (CronJob), with a data check.
  Test: `tests/rs-scheduled.sh`. Evidence: `docs/evidence/RS-3.txt`
On-demand versus scheduled RS-3:

Brief: it allows either.
BK-4 and OBS-2: BK-4 says backups are verified “automatically”, and OBS-2 lists a “restore test failed” alert. Both presuppose a result that is recorded periodically. On-demand would need make test to run it, or a stored last-result to alert on.
Scheduled costs:
An identity that can create pods and PVCs. It either creates scratch namespaces (a cluster-scoped permission) or uses one fixed scratch namespace.
A second full Postgres at the same time on one laptop.
On-demand costs: fewer parts, with no RBAC and no job. Its evidence is a single run.
Decisions I need from you:

The trigger: option 1, 2 or 3.
The BK-2 line: keep or change, and whether “full and incremental” stays.
RS-3: on-demand or scheduled, and which line.
I will then ask only for the read-only output that the chosen option needs (for example cat versions.env, k8s/pg/30-services.yaml, k8s/pg/10-rbac.yaml). Once you decide, I will propose the ADR-0002 edit with exact before and after lines. You approve it before any change.

Optional, SEC-1, your call: a read-only history scan, for example gitleaks, is now more relevant because the repo is public.

Not verified: everything marked prediction, the pinned pgBackRest version, the image contents, and the services and RBAC manifests. I marked 7 predictions in this reply.

okay tell me in short which option is the best practice why? than  i will tell u

Best practice: option 2, the CronJob with pgBackRest TLS server mode. This is my view, and the decision is yours.

Why:

Design: it matches how pgBackRest is designed to run with a separate backup host. The Postgres pods only serve data, and scheduling and failure tracking sit in Kubernetes.
Scheduling and status: concurrencyPolicy: Forbid stops overlapping runs. The Job history gives you a clear signal for the “backup too old” alert in OBS-2.
Security: it avoids the broad pods/exec permission of option 3, which conflicts with least privilege (SEC-4).
SPEC wording: the word “CronJob” stays true, so BK-2 needs no edit.
What it costs:

It has the most new parts: a certificate set generated at make up, one new sidecar process, and a new port.
It is the riskiest to get working. It depends on the pinned pgBackRest version supporting this mode, which I have not checked (prediction).
It is the slowest to build.
When I would pick differently:

Option 1 (sidecar) is the pragmatic choice if you weigh simplicity and time more than separation. It has no new image, no certificates and no RBAC, but it puts the backup load in the leader pod and gives weaker failure visibility.
I would not pick option 3 here, because of the exec permission.
If you choose option 2, my first read-only request will be cat versions.env, so I can check the pinned pgBackRest version before any design work. I have not verified that version.

Nothing was written or run. Option 2 looks feasible. The ADR-0002 edit is below for your approval, and no file changes before that.

What the output shows
Version: pgBackRest is 2.60.0 and pgbackrest help server works, so TLS server mode exists in this image. My earlier prediction of 2.37 or later holds. The image needs no change and no new pinned image.
Backup state: pgbackrest info for stanza pg-ha reports no valid backups (no base backup exists yet). The WAL archive runs from segment 1 to 6.
Per-pod DNS: the headless Service pg-ha-headless gives per-pod names such as pg-ha-0.pg-ha-headless.pg-ha.svc.cluster.local. Its ports are 5432 and 8008 only, so a pgBackRest server port has to be added.
Pod identity: the postgres container runs as uid 999. The SQL connection over the socket path already works for the BK-1 sidecar. The database connects as postgres over the socket, so pg_hba needs no change (prediction).
RBAC: the patroni Role covers configmaps and pods only. A CronJob using TLS server mode needs no Kubernetes permission, so the new RBAC is none.
NetworkPolicies: the cluster has none. Port 8432 is therefore open for now. SEC-3 must add an allow from the job to port 8432 later. I will record that in known-limitations.
Secrets script: gen-objstore-secrets.sh signs the server certificate with a temporary CA key and deletes the key before writing the Secret. A new script can use the same pattern, so the cluster never holds a CA private key.
Design if you approve (prediction; new files named, all untested)
pgserver sidecar in the pg-ha pods, running pgbackrest server on port 8432. It uses the same image, mounts the data volume, the socket directory and /tmp, and has its own certificate Secret.
Certificates: a new scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh creates a CA, a server certificate with SANs for the three per-pod names, and one client certificate. The Secrets hold certificates and keys, not the CA key.
CronJob: it runs pgbackrest backup from the same image. Its environment names the three pg hosts, with TLS settings. It also holds the S3 credentials and the CA for the repository. A Job ends when the backup ends, so its status shows success or failure.
Retention: BK-3 settings go into the pgbackrest-env ConfigMap.
Test first: tests/bk-schedule.sh is written and run red, before any of the above exists.
Costs and risks
Rolling restart: applying the changed StatefulSet restarts all 3 pods one at a time, and the leader’s restart may cause a failover (prediction). It is a destructive-style step, so it gets a list of what it affects and needs your explicit go.
Lock behaviour: whether pgBackRest stops two overlapping backups run from different job pods is unverified (prediction). That affects the next decision.
Standby backups: whether pgbackrest backup from the job picks the leader correctly with three pg hosts is unverified (prediction). I have not planned standby backups.
First backup: an incremental backup with no earlier full backup should turn into a full one (prediction, not verified).
Scope of data flow: data passes through the job pod to S3, as far as I know.
Certificate expiry: certificates expire with no rotation procedure, like the object store. I would add that to known-limitations.
Proposed ADR-0002 edit (text only, do not run)
Current line 3:

Status: Accepted for the tool. Open for how scheduled backups reach the data (decided in Phase 3).
Proposed line 3:

Status: Accepted. Scheduled backups run as CronJobs that reach the Postgres pods through pgBackRest TLS server mode (decided in Phase 3).
Current section:

## Open question
How the scheduled backup job reaches the cluster: a CronJob using pgBackRest TLS server mode, or a sidecar. We decide this when we build Phase 3 and update this ADR.
Proposed section:

## Decision on the backup trigger
CronJobs run pgBackRest from the same pinned image and reach the Postgres pods through pgBackRest TLS server mode, with a `pgserver` sidecar in each pod and certificates generated at `make up`. Options not chosen: a scheduler in a sidecar (backup load in the leader pod, weak failure visibility) and a CronJob using kubectl exec (broad pods/exec permission, conflicts with least privilege). Not verified yet: leader selection with three pg hosts, overlapping backups, certificate rotation.
Current and proposed Consequences stay the same, except that I would add this line at its end:

- A new port (8432) and a certificate set exist; both must be covered by the NetworkPolicies of SEC-3.
Decisions I need from you
Approve, change or reject the ADR-0002 edit above.
One CronJob or two. Two (full weekly, incremental daily) are simpler but may overlap. One CronJob can choose full or incremental from pgbackrest info in a small script, which uses the repository as its state and avoids overlap, but adds script logic.
BK-2 SPEC line: under option 2 it stays unchanged. Please confirm that, and whether “full and incremental” stays (incrementals are your MINE item).
RS-3 stays open until you decide. It does not block the BK-2 test.
Not verified
The server mode behaviour, leader selection, lock behaviour, the rolling restart, and the info and incremental-to-full predictions. I marked 7 predictions in this reply.

This item changes one file: docs/decisions/0002-backup-tool.md (a phase 0 file, approved by you). The block writes the new text to a temporary file and checks it against the repo file, then copies it only if the check passes.

Run this once and paste the output:
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab && f=docs/decisions/0002-backup-tool.md && t=$(mktemp) && {
[ -z "$(git status --short)" ] || { echo "STOP: working tree not clean"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^Status: Accepted for the tool. Open for how scheduled backups reach the data (decided in Phase 3).$' "$f")" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: old status line not found exactly once"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^## Open question$' "$f")" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: open-question heading not found exactly once"; exit 1; }
cat >"$t" <<'EOF'
# ADR-0002: pgBackRest for backups, WAL archiving and PITR

Status: Accepted. Scheduled backups run as CronJobs that reach the Postgres pods through pgBackRest TLS server mode (decided in Phase 3).

## Context
We need base backups, continuous WAL archiving to object storage, retention, verification and point-in-time recovery.

## Options
1. pgBackRest. Full, differential and incremental backups, retention settings, `verify`, PITR and S3 support in one tool.
2. WAL-G. Simpler to configure and fast, but retention and verification are less complete.
3. pg_basebackup plus a custom archive script. Most work, and we would own every edge case.

## Decision
pgBackRest. `archive_command` in the Postgres pods pushes WAL to the S3-compatible repository. Retention is declared in the pgBackRest configuration.

## Decision on the backup trigger
CronJobs run pgBackRest from the same pinned image and reach the Postgres pods through pgBackRest TLS server mode, with a `pgserver` sidecar in each pod and certificates generated at `make up`. Options not chosen: a scheduler in a sidecar (backup load in the leader pod, weak failure visibility) and a CronJob using kubectl exec (broad pods/exec permission, conflicts with least privilege). Not verified yet: leader selection with three pg hosts, overlapping backups, certificate rotation.

## Consequences
- The image includes a pinned pgBackRest version.
- Backup, verify and restore scripts all use the same tool.
- Repository credentials are Kubernetes Secrets generated at `make up`.
- A new port (8432) and a certificate set exist; both must be covered by the NetworkPolicies of SEC-3.
EOF
d=$(diff "$f" "$t" || true)
echo "removed lines: $(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d")  added lines: $(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d")  (expected 3 and 4)"
[ "$(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d")" = 3 ] && [ "$(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d")" = 4 ] || { echo "STOP: the repo file differs from what I expected, nothing changed"; echo "$d"; exit 1; }
cp "$t" "$f" && echo "--- written, git diff --stat:" && git diff --stat && git status --short | wc -l
}; rm -f "$t"
What to expect (prediction): removed lines: 3  added lines: 4, then a diff stat for one file, then 1. If it prints a STOP line, nothing was changed. Paste the output, including the diff, and I will adjust. If the repo file has no trailing newline, the count will be off and the block will stop safely.

It prints counts, never key content.

Run this once and paste the whole output:
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/bk-tls.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-FAILED.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/bk-tls.sh or the FAILED evidence already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee tests/bk-tls.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# BK-2 (certificate part, ADR-0002): scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh creates the TLS material for pgBackRest server mode.
# Contract: two Secrets, pgbackrest-tls-server and pgbackrest-tls-client, each holding exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key
# (no CA private key in the cluster). The server certificate names the three pod DNS names behind pg-ha-headless and is
# valid for server use only. The client certificate has the fixed name pgbackrest-backup and is valid for client use only.
# A rerun keeps both Secrets. Nothing printed holds key content and no key material stays on disk.
# Runs in a scratch namespace, never in pg-ha. Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."

NS=pgtls-test
SRV=pgbackrest-tls-server
CLI=pgbackrest-tls-client
CLIENT_CN=pgbackrest-backup
pass() { echo "PASS: $*"; }
fail() { echo "FAIL: $*"; exit 1; }
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }
field() { k get secret "$1" -o json | jq -r --arg key "$2" '.data[$key] // empty' | base64 -d; }
keys_of() { k get secret "$1" -o json 2>/dev/null | jq -c '[.data | keys[]] | sort'; }
snapshot() {
  kubectl -n "$NS" get secret "$SRV" "$CLI" -o json \
    | jq -c '[.items[] | {n: .metadata.name, d: .data}] | sort_by(.n)' | sha256sum | cut -d' ' -f1
}
host() { echo "pg-ha-$1.pg-ha-headless.$NS.svc.cluster.local"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
gentmp=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  rm -rf "$work" "$gentmp"
  kubectl delete ns "$NS" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
[ -x scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh ] || fail "scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh does not exist or is not executable"
pass "generator script is present and executable"

kubectl delete ns "$NS" --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null
kubectl create ns "$NS" >/dev/null

if ! out1=$(NS="$NS" TMPDIR="$gentmp" scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh 2>&1); then
  echo "$out1" | tail -n 5
  fail "generator failed on the first run"
fi
[ -n "$out1" ] || fail "the generator printed nothing on the first run, so the output checks would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -c ' created$' <<<"$out1" || true)" -eq 2 ] || fail "first run must report exactly two Secrets created"
pass "first run created two Secrets"

[ "$(keys_of "$SRV")" = '["ca.crt","tls.crt","tls.key"]' ] || fail "$SRV must hold exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key"
[ "$(keys_of "$CLI")" = '["ca.crt","tls.crt","tls.key"]' ] || fail "$CLI must hold exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key"
pass "both Secrets hold exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key (no CA private key in the cluster)"

field "$SRV" ca.crt >"$work/s-ca.crt"
field "$SRV" tls.crt >"$work/s-tls.crt"
field "$SRV" tls.key >"$work/s-tls.key"
field "$CLI" ca.crt >"$work/c-ca.crt"
field "$CLI" tls.crt >"$work/c-tls.crt"
field "$CLI" tls.key >"$work/c-tls.key"
for f in s-ca.crt s-tls.crt s-tls.key c-ca.crt c-tls.crt c-tls.key; do
  [ -s "$work/$f" ] || fail "$f is empty"
done
cmp -s "$work/s-ca.crt" "$work/c-ca.crt" || fail "the two Secrets hold different CA certificates"
if cmp -s "$work/s-tls.key" "$work/c-tls.key"; then fail "server and client use the same private key"; fi
pass "six files are non-empty, one shared CA, two different private keys"

openssl verify -CAfile "$work/s-ca.crt" -purpose sslserver "$work/s-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "server certificate does not verify for server use"
openssl verify -CAfile "$work/c-ca.crt" -purpose sslclient "$work/c-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "client certificate does not verify for client use"
pass "server certificate verifies for server use and client certificate for client use"

openssl req -x509 -newkey rsa:2048 -nodes -days 1 -subj "/CN=unrelated-ca" \
  -keyout "$work/other.key" -out "$work/other.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1
if openssl verify -CAfile "$work/other.crt" -purpose sslserver "$work/s-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "negative control: the server certificate verified against an unrelated CA"
fi
if openssl verify -CAfile "$work/other.crt" -purpose sslclient "$work/c-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "negative control: the client certificate verified against an unrelated CA"
fi
pass "negative control: an unrelated CA verifies neither certificate"

if openssl verify -CAfile "$work/s-ca.crt" -purpose sslclient "$work/s-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "negative control: the server certificate was accepted for client use"
fi
if openssl verify -CAfile "$work/c-ca.crt" -purpose sslserver "$work/c-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "negative control: the client certificate was accepted for server use"
fi
pass "negative control: each certificate is rejected for the other purpose"

names=$(openssl x509 -in "$work/s-tls.crt" -noout -ext subjectAltName | grep -o 'DNS:[^, ]*' | sed 's/^DNS://' | sort || true)
expected=$(printf '%s\n%s\n%s\n' "$(host 0)" "$(host 1)" "$(host 2)" | sort)
[ -n "$names" ] || fail "the server certificate has no DNS names"
[ "$names" = "$expected" ] || fail "server certificate names are not exactly the three pod DNS names"
for i in 0 1 2; do
  openssl verify -CAfile "$work/s-ca.crt" -purpose sslserver -verify_hostname "$(host "$i")" "$work/s-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    || fail "hostname check failed for $(host "$i")"
done
if openssl verify -CAfile "$work/s-ca.crt" -purpose sslserver -verify_hostname "pg-ha-0.pg-ha-headless.other.svc.cluster.local" "$work/s-tls.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  fail "negative control: a hostname outside the certificate was accepted"
fi
pass "server certificate names exactly the three pod DNS names; a foreign hostname is rejected"

[ "$(openssl x509 -in "$work/s-tls.crt" -noout -pubkey | sha256sum)" = "$(openssl pkey -in "$work/s-tls.key" -pubout | sha256sum)" ] || fail "server key does not match server certificate"
[ "$(openssl x509 -in "$work/c-tls.crt" -noout -pubkey | sha256sum)" = "$(openssl pkey -in "$work/c-tls.key" -pubout | sha256sum)" ] || fail "client key does not match client certificate"
if [ "$(openssl x509 -in "$work/s-tls.crt" -noout -pubkey | sha256sum)" = "$(openssl pkey -in "$work/c-tls.key" -pubout | sha256sum)" ]; then
  fail "negative control: the server certificate matched the client key"
fi
pass "keys match their certificates; a mismatched pair is detected"

cn=$(openssl x509 -in "$work/c-tls.crt" -noout -subject -nameopt RFC2253 | sed 's/^subject= *//')
[ -n "$cn" ] || fail "could not read the client certificate name"
[ "$cn" = "CN=$CLIENT_CN" ] || fail "client certificate name is not CN=$CLIENT_CN"
pass "client certificate name is CN=$CLIENT_CN"

t=$(openssl x509 -in "$work/s-ca.crt" -noout -text)
[ "$(grep -F -c 'CA:TRUE' <<<"$t" || true)" -ge 1 ] || fail "ca.crt is not marked as a CA"
t=$(openssl x509 -in "$work/s-tls.crt" -noout -text)
[ "$(grep -F -c 'CA:FALSE' <<<"$t" || true)" -ge 1 ] || fail "the server certificate is marked as a CA"
t=$(openssl x509 -in "$work/c-tls.crt" -noout -text)
[ "$(grep -F -c 'CA:FALSE' <<<"$t" || true)" -ge 1 ] || fail "the client certificate is marked as a CA"
pass "ca.crt is a CA; server and client certificates are not"

for f in s-ca.crt s-tls.crt c-tls.crt; do
  openssl x509 -in "$work/$f" -noout -checkend 2592000 >/dev/null || fail "$f expires within 30 days"
done
pass "certificates are valid for more than 30 days"

[ -z "$(ls -A "$gentmp")" ] || fail "the generator left files in its temporary directory"
pass "no key material is left on disk by the generator"

cat "$work/s-tls.key" "$work/c-tls.key" >"$work/keypat"
[ -s "$work/keypat" ] || fail "the key pattern file is empty"
line=$(sed -n 2p "$work/s-tls.key")
[ -n "$line" ] || fail "could not take a key line for the leak control"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/keypat" <<<"control line with $line inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "leak control: the key scanner did not fire on a planted key line"
[REDACTED:private-key-line]
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/keypat" <<<"$out1" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "key content appears in the generator output"
[ "$(grep -F -c 'PRIVATE KEY' <<<"$out1" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a private key marker appears in the generator output"
pass "no key content in the generator output (both scanners fired on planted input)"

before=$(snapshot)
if ! out2=$(NS="$NS" TMPDIR="$gentmp" scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh 2>&1); then
  echo "$out2" | tail -n 5
  fail "generator failed on the second run"
fi
[ "$(grep -c 'already exists, keeping it' <<<"$out2" || true)" -eq 2 ] || fail "second run must keep both Secrets"
[ "$(snapshot)" = "$before" ] || fail "a Secret changed on the second run"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/keypat" <<<"$out2" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "key content appears in the second run output"
[ -z "$(ls -A "$gentmp")" ] || fail "the second run left files in the temporary directory"
pass "second run keeps both Secrets unchanged and prints no key content"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/bk-tls.sh
[ "$(tail -n 1 tests/bk-tls.sh)" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the test file is incomplete"; exit 1; }
echo "lines: $(wc -l < tests/bk-tls.sh)"
shellcheck tests/bk-tls.sh || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, no run"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean"
tests/bk-tls.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-FAILED.txt
echo "exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
)

Step 2: the generator, then the test, behind one shellcheck gate. It reuses the pattern of gen-objstore-secrets.sh: the CA key exists only in a temp dir and is removed before the Secrets are written.

The result is saved by exit code, to docs/evidence/BK-2-tls.txt on a pass and BK-2-tls-run2-FAILED.txt on a fail, so no failed run is overwritten. If the second run fails, stop and report. Do not rerun with changes on your own.

(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-2-tls.txt ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-run2-FAILED.txt ]; then echo "STOP: the generator or an evidence file already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Generate the TLS material for pgBackRest server mode (ADR-0002) once: Secrets pgbackrest-tls-server and
# pgbackrest-tls-client, each holding exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key. Never printed, never committed.
# The CA private key exists only in a temporary directory while the two certificates are signed and is removed before
# the Secrets are written, so the cluster holds no CA private key. (rm is not a secure erase.)
# The server certificate names the three pod DNS names behind pg-ha-headless and is valid for server use only.
# The client certificate is CN=pgbackrest-backup and is valid for client use only.
# An existing pair is kept, so a rerun rotates nothing. If only one of the two exists the script stops.
set -euo pipefail
NS="${NS:-pg-ha}"
HEADLESS=pg-ha-headless
DAYS=825
SRV=pgbackrest-tls-server
CLI=pgbackrest-tls-client
umask 077

exists() { kubectl -n "$NS" get secret "$1" >/dev/null 2>&1; }

if exists "$SRV" && exists "$CLI"; then
  echo "secret $SRV already exists, keeping it"
  echo "secret $CLI already exists, keeping it"
  exit 0
fi
if exists "$SRV" || exists "$CLI"; then
  echo "only one of $SRV and $CLI exists; delete both Secrets to regenerate them together" >&2
  exit 1
fi

work=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$work"' EXIT
mkdir "$work/ca"
openssl req -x509 -newkey rsa:2048 -nodes -days "$DAYS" -subj "/CN=pgbackrest-ca" \
  -addext "basicConstraints=critical,CA:TRUE" -addext "keyUsage=critical,keyCertSign,cRLSign" \
  -keyout "$work/ca/ca.key" -out "$work/ca.crt" 2>/dev/null

openssl req -newkey rsa:2048 -nodes -subj "/CN=pgbackrest-server" \
  -keyout "$work/server.key" -out "$work/server.csr" 2>/dev/null
san=""
for i in 0 1 2; do
  san="${san}${san:+,}DNS:pg-ha-$i.$HEADLESS.$NS.svc.cluster.local"
done
cat >"$work/server.ext" <<EXT
subjectAltName=$san
extendedKeyUsage=serverAuth
keyUsage=critical,digitalSignature,keyEncipherment
basicConstraints=CA:FALSE
EXT
openssl x509 -req -in "$work/server.csr" -CA "$work/ca.crt" -CAkey "$work/ca/ca.key" -CAcreateserial \
  -days "$DAYS" -extfile "$work/server.ext" -out "$work/server.crt" 2>/dev/null

openssl req -newkey rsa:2048 -nodes -subj "/CN=pgbackrest-backup" \
  -keyout "$work/client.key" -out "$work/client.csr" 2>/dev/null
cat >"$work/client.ext" <<EXT
extendedKeyUsage=clientAuth
keyUsage=critical,digitalSignature,keyEncipherment
basicConstraints=CA:FALSE
EXT
openssl x509 -req -in "$work/client.csr" -CA "$work/ca.crt" -CAkey "$work/ca/ca.key" -CAcreateserial \
  -days "$DAYS" -extfile "$work/client.ext" -out "$work/client.crt" 2>/dev/null
rm -rf "$work/ca"

kubectl -n "$NS" create secret generic "$SRV" \
  --from-file=ca.crt="$work/ca.crt" --from-file=tls.crt="$work/server.crt" --from-file=tls.key="$work/server.key" \
  --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl -n "$NS" apply -f - >/dev/null
echo "secret $SRV created"
kubectl -n "$NS" create secret generic "$CLI" \
  --from-file=ca.crt="$work/ca.crt" --from-file=tls.crt="$work/client.crt" --from-file=tls.key="$work/client.key" \
  --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl -n "$NS" apply -f - >/dev/null
echo "secret $CLI created"
rm -rf "$work"
trap - EXIT
EOF
chmod +x scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh
[ "$(tail -n 1 scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh)" = 'trap - EXIT' ] || { echo "STOP: the generator file is incomplete"; exit 1; }
echo "generator lines: $(wc -l < scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh)"
shellcheck scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh tests/bk-tls.sh || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, no test run"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean"
tmp=$(mktemp)
rc=0
tests/bk-tls.sh >"$tmp" 2>&1 || rc=$?
cat "$tmp"
echo "exit=$rc"
if [ "$rc" = 0 ]; then dest=docs/evidence/BK-2-tls.txt; else dest=docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-run2-FAILED.txt; fi
cp "$tmp" "$dest" && rm -f "$tmp"
echo "saved: $dest"
echo "--- git status"
git status --short
)

pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'server' command - 'tls-server-address' option help

TLS server address.

IP address the server will listen on for client requests.

default: localhost
=== option tls-server-port
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'server' command - 'tls-server-port' option help

TLS server port.

Port the server will listen on for client requests.

default: 8432
=== option tls-server-ca-file
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'server' command - 'tls-server-ca-file' option help

TLS server certificate authorities.

Checks that client certificates are signed by a trusted certificate authority.
=== option tls-server-cert-file
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'server' command - 'tls-server-cert-file' option help

TLS server certificate file.

Sent to the client to show the server identity.
=== option tls-server-key-file
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'server' command - 'tls-server-key-file' option help

TLS server key file.

Proves server certificate was sent by the owner.
=== option tls-server-auth
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'server' command - 'tls-server-auth' option help

TLS server authorized clients.

Clients are authorized on the server by verifying their certificate and
checking their certificate CN (Common Name) against a list on the server
configured with the tls-server-auth option.

A client CN can be authorized for as many stanzas as needed by providing a
comma-separated list to the tls-server-auth option or for all stanzas by
specifying tls-server-auth=client-cn=*. Wildcards may not be specified for the
client CN.

deprecated name: db-host

PostgreSQL host protocol type.

The following protocol types are supported:

ssh - Secure Shell.

PostgreSQL host certificate file.

PostgreSQL host key file.

Proves client certificate was sent by owner.

PostgreSQL host port when pg-host is set.

Use this option to specify a non-default port for the PostgreSQL host protocol.

NOTE: When pg-host-type=ssh there is no default for pg-host-port. In this case

Run every command below in your terminal, in ~/postgres-ha-lab, every time. I never run anything. Paste each output back to me.

What your output shows
Server options: every server option I predicted exists in pgBackRest 2.60.0: address, port, CA file, certificate file, key file and tls-server-auth.
Server address: the default is localhost. The sidecar must set it to 0.0.0.0, or the other pods cannot reach it.
Authorization: tls-server-auth maps a client certificate name to stanzas, for example pgbackrest-backup=pg-ha. A wildcard is allowed for the stanza but not for the client name. That matches the fixed name pgbackrest-backup in the generated client certificate.
Client options: pg-host, pg-host-type=tls, pg-host-ca-file, pg-host-cert-file, pg-host-key-file and pg-host-port all exist. The port default is 8432.
Container user: the runAsUser column was empty because the user is set at pod level (999, from the manifest). That is a fact, not a gap.
Contract this item fixes (my choices, tell me if you want any changed)
A third container, pgserver, in each pg-ha pod. It uses the same image and runs pgbackrest server.
Its environment holds PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_ADDRESS=0.0.0.0 and PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_AUTH=pgbackrest-backup=pg-ha. The certificate paths come from the pgbackrest-tls-server Secret, mounted read-only.
The test connects like the future CronJob will. A scratch pod uses the pgbackrest-tls-client Secret and runs pgbackrest check against all three pods as pg1, pg2 and pg3. Success proves all three servers answer with a valid certificate. It also exercises the leader selection with three pg hosts that ADR-0002 lists as not verified, but it does not prove that backups choose the leader correctly. That stays open.
Negative controls: the same command with no client certificate must fail. The same command with a self-signed certificate that has the same name (a different CA) must fail.
Known gap that I will record in known-limitations after this item: the authorization by certificate name and stanza is configured but not tested. The cluster holds no CA key, so the test cannot create a certificate with another name that the cluster CA would accept.
Red run
The test runs behind a clean shellcheck, as one gate. It stops at its first check, because no pod has a pgserver container yet, so it creates nothing in the cluster. Its exit cleanup sends deletes for a scratch pod and a scratch Secret that do not exist, with --ignore-not-found.

Run this once and paste the whole output:
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/bk-pgserver.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-2-pgserver-FAILED.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/bk-pgserver.sh or the FAILED evidence already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee tests/bk-pgserver.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# BK-2 (pgserver sidecar, ADR-0002): every pg-ha pod runs a "pgserver" container (pgbackrest server, TLS on port 8432,
# listening on 0.0.0.0, client authorised as pgbackrest-backup for stanza pg-ha). A scratch pod with the client Secret
# runs "pgbackrest check" against the three pods as pg1, pg2 and pg3. It must succeed with the client certificate and fail
# with no client certificate and with a self-signed certificate of the same name (negative controls).
# Not covered here: authorisation by certificate name and stanza (the cluster holds no CA key to sign another name),
# and which pod a backup chooses.
# Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q". Nothing printed holds key content.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

SRVSEC=pgbackrest-tls-server
CLISEC=pgbackrest-tls-client
OTHER=bk-pgserver-test-other
TP=bk-pgserver-test
IMG="${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}"
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }
keys_of() { k get secret "$1" -o json 2>/dev/null | jq -c '[.data | keys[]] | sort'; }
host() { echo "pg-ha-$1.pg-ha-headless.$NS.svc.cluster.local"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  k delete pod "$TP" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  k delete secret "$OTHER" --ignore-not-found >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  rm -rf "$work"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

rc=0
out=""
run_check() {
  local certdir=$1 i
  local a=(--stanza=pg-ha --log-level-console=warn)
  for i in 1 2 3; do
    a+=("--pg$i-host=$(host $((i - 1)))" "--pg$i-host-type=tls" "--pg$i-host-ca-file=/client/ca.crt" "--pg$i-path=/pgdata/data" "--pg$i-socket-path=/run/postgresql")
    if [ "$certdir" != none ]; then
      a+=("--pg$i-host-cert-file=$certdir/tls.crt" "--pg$i-host-key-file=$certdir/tls.key")
    fi
  done
  rc=0
  out=$(timeout 240 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$TP" -c client -- pgbackrest "${a[@]}" check </dev/null 2>&1) || rc=$?
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
pods=$(k get pods -l application=pg-ha,cluster-name=pg-ha -o json)

n=$(jq '[.items[] | select(any(.spec.containers[]; .name == "pgserver"))] | length' <<<"$pods")
[ "$n" = 3 ] || fail "expected a pgserver container in 3 pods, found it in $n"
n=$(jq '[.items[].status.containerStatuses[] | select(.name == "pgserver" and .ready == true)] | length' <<<"$pods")
[ "$n" = 3 ] || fail "expected 3 ready pgserver containers, found $n"
n=$(jq '[.items[].status.containerStatuses[] | select(.name == "pgserver") | .restartCount] | add' <<<"$pods")
[ "$n" = 0 ] || fail "pgserver containers restarted $n times"
n=$(jq '[.items[].spec.containers[] | select(.name == "pgserver" and .command == ["pgbackrest","server"])] | length' <<<"$pods")
[ "$n" = 3 ] || fail "pgserver must run 'pgbackrest server' in all 3 pods, found $n"
n=$(jq '[.items[].spec.containers[] | select(.name == "pgserver") | .env[]? | select(.name == "PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_ADDRESS" and .value == "0.0.0.0")] | length' <<<"$pods")
[ "$n" = 3 ] || fail "PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_ADDRESS must be 0.0.0.0 in all 3 pods, found $n"
n=$(jq '[.items[].spec.containers[] | select(.name == "pgserver") | .env[]? | select(.name == "PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_AUTH" and .value == "pgbackrest-backup=pg-ha")] | length' <<<"$pods")
[ "$n" = 3 ] || fail "PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_AUTH must be pgbackrest-backup=pg-ha in all 3 pods, found $n"
pass "3 ready pgserver containers with no restarts, the right command, address and authorised client"

[ "$(keys_of "$SRVSEC")" = '["ca.crt","tls.crt","tls.key"]' ] || fail "$SRVSEC must hold exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key"
[ "$(keys_of "$CLISEC")" = '["ca.crt","tls.crt","tls.key"]' ] || fail "$CLISEC must hold exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key"
pass "both TLS Secrets exist with exactly ca.crt, tls.crt and tls.key"

wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
pass "Patroni reports 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"

openssl req -x509 -newkey rsa:2048 -nodes -days 1 -subj "/CN=pgbackrest-backup" \
  -keyout "$work/o.key" -out "$work/o.crt" >/dev/null 2>&1
[ -s "$work/o.crt" ] && [ -s "$work/o.key" ] || fail "could not create the self-signed control certificate"
k create secret generic "$OTHER" --from-file=tls.crt="$work/o.crt" --from-file=tls.key="$work/o.key" >/dev/null

k delete pod "$TP" --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null
# defaultMode 288 is octal 0440: readable by group 999 through fsGroup.
kubectl -n "$NS" apply -f - >/dev/null <<YAML
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: $TP
  namespace: $NS
  labels:
    app: $TP
spec:
  restartPolicy: Never
  securityContext:
    runAsNonRoot: true
    runAsUser: 999
    runAsGroup: 999
    fsGroup: 999
    seccompProfile:
      type: RuntimeDefault
  containers:
    - name: client
      image: $IMG
      imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
      command: ["sleep", "3600"]
      envFrom:
        - configMapRef:
            name: pgbackrest-env
      env:
        - name: PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: objstore-credentials
              key: access-key-id
        - name: PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY_SECRET
          valueFrom:
            secretKeyRef:
              name: objstore-credentials
              key: secret-access-key
      securityContext:
        allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
        capabilities:
          drop: ["ALL"]
      resources:
        requests:
          cpu: 10m
          memory: 64Mi
        limits:
          memory: 256Mi
      volumeMounts:
        - name: client
          mountPath: /client
          readOnly: true
        - name: other
          mountPath: /other
          readOnly: true
        - name: objstore-ca
          mountPath: /certs
          readOnly: true
        - name: tmp
          mountPath: /tmp
  volumes:
    - name: client
      secret:
        secretName: $CLISEC
        defaultMode: 288
    - name: other
      secret:
        secretName: $OTHER
        defaultMode: 288
    - name: objstore-ca
      secret:
        secretName: objstore-tls
        items:
          - key: ca.crt
            path: ca.crt
    - name: tmp
      emptyDir: {}
YAML
k wait --for=condition=Ready "pod/$TP" --timeout=120s >/dev/null || fail "the scratch client pod did not become ready"
pass "scratch client pod is ready"

run_check /client
if [ "$rc" != 0 ]; then
  echo "$out" | tail -n 8
  fail "pgbackrest check through the three pgserver endpoints failed (exit $rc)"
fi
pass "pgbackrest check succeeds through the three pgserver endpoints with the client certificate"

run_check none
[ "$rc" != 0 ] || fail "negative control: the check succeeded with no client certificate"
[ -n "$out" ] || fail "negative control: the failed check printed nothing, so the control proves nothing"
pass "negative control: with no client certificate the check fails"

run_check /other
[ "$rc" != 0 ] || fail "negative control: the check succeeded with a self-signed certificate of the same name"
[ -n "$out" ] || fail "negative control: the failed check printed nothing, so the control proves nothing"
pass "negative control: a self-signed certificate of the same name is refused"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/bk-pgserver.sh
[ "$(tail -n 1 tests/bk-pgserver.sh)" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the test file is incomplete"; exit 1; }
echo "lines: $(wc -l < tests/bk-pgserver.sh)"

Design choices to check:

No S3 keys: the pgserver container gets no object-store credentials. A backup sends data through the protocol and the repository is written by the client side, as far as I know (prediction).
Replicas allowed: it mounts /pgdata read-only, and the same socket and /tmp as the sidecar.
Auth: only one client name is authorised, and only for stanza pg-ha.
Probe: the TCP readiness probe opens a plain connection to a TLS port. It may log a handshake warning every 10 s (prediction). If it does, I will propose an exec probe.
Headless Service: I did not add port 8432 to it. Pod DNS names resolve without it. Tell me if you want it listed for documentation.

Apply the StatefulSet. This restarts all 3 pods, one at a time, in reverse ordinal order, as far as I know (prediction). The affects list I will give first:
Each pod restarts once, and all 3 gain a third container.
The data volumes are kept, and no volume or Secret is deleted.

The PodDisruptionBudget does not stop a StatefulSet update, as far as I know (prediction).

What the block does:

It stops if the files are not as expected, or if an edit is already there.
It builds both new files in a temp location and checks the diff counts: 0 lines removed, and exactly the lines added.
It checks the result is valid, and copies the files into the repo only if every check passes.
It does not touch the cluster.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
up=scripts/pg-up.sh
sts=k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml
[ "$(git status --short | wc -l)" = 3 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly the 3 known uncommitted entries, nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^bash scripts/gen-objstore-secrets.sh$' "$up" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the objstore secrets line in $up is not there exactly once"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c 'gen-pgbackrest-tls' "$up" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: $up already calls the TLS generator"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^      volumes:$' "$sts" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the volumes line in $sts is not there exactly once"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^  volumeClaimTemplates:$' "$sts" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the volumeClaimTemplates line in $sts is not there exactly once"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c 'pgserver' "$sts" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: $sts already mentions pgserver"; exit 1; }
w=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$w"' EXIT
cat >"$w/container.txt" <<'EOF'
        - name: pgserver
          image: __IMAGE__
          imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
          command: ["pgbackrest", "server"]
          ports:
            - name: pgbackrest
              containerPort: 8432
          envFrom:
            - configMapRef:
                name: pgbackrest-env
          env:
            - name: PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_ADDRESS
              value: "0.0.0.0"
            - name: PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_CA_FILE
              value: /tls/ca.crt
            - name: PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_CERT_FILE
              value: /tls/tls.crt
            - name: PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_KEY_FILE
              value: /tls/tls.key
            - name: PGBACKREST_TLS_SERVER_AUTH
              value: "pgbackrest-backup=pg-ha"
          readinessProbe:
            tcpSocket:
              port: 8432
            periodSeconds: 10
          resources:
            requests:
              cpu: 10m
              memory: 32Mi
            limits:
              memory: 256Mi
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
          volumeMounts:
            - name: data
              mountPath: /pgdata
              readOnly: true
            - name: run
              mountPath: /run/postgresql
            - name: tmp
              mountPath: /tmp
            - name: pgserver-tls
              mountPath: /tls
              readOnly: true
EOF
cat >"$w/volume.txt" <<'EOF'
        - name: pgserver-tls
          secret:
            secretName: pgbackrest-tls-server
            defaultMode: 288
EOF
add=$(( $(wc -l <"$w/container.txt") + $(wc -l <"$w/volume.txt") ))
awk -v cf="$w/container.txt" -v vf="$w/volume.txt" '
$0=="      volumes:" { while ((getline l < cf) > 0) print l; close(cf) }
$0=="  volumeClaimTemplates:" { while ((getline l < vf) > 0) print l; close(vf) }
{ print }' "$sts" >"$w/sts.new"
awk '$0=="bash scripts/gen-objstore-secrets.sh" { print; print "bash scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh"; next } { print }' "$up" >"$w/up.new"
d=$(diff "$sts" "$w/sts.new" || true)
echo "statefulset: removed $(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true), added $(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true) (expected 0 and $add)"
[ "$(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true)" = 0 ] && [ "$(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true)" = "$add" ] || { echo "STOP: statefulset diff is not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
d=$(diff "$up" "$w/up.new" || true)
echo "pg-up.sh: removed $(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true), added $(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true) (expected 0 and 1)"
[ "$(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true)" = 0 ] && [ "$(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: pg-up.sh diff is not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}|g" "$w/sts.new" >"$w/sts.img.yaml"
kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$w/sts.img.yaml" -o json >"$w/sts.json" || { echo "STOP: the new StatefulSet is not valid, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
names=$(jq -r '[.spec.template.spec.containers[].name] | join(",")' "$w/sts.json")
echo "containers: $names (expected postgres,backup,pgserver)"
[ "$names" = "postgres,backup,pgserver" ] || { echo "STOP: container list is wrong, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
vols=$(jq -r '[.spec.template.spec.volumes[].name] | join(",")' "$w/sts.json")
echo "volumes: $vols"
[ "$(grep -c 'pgserver-tls' <<<"$vols" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the pgserver-tls volume is missing, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
if command -v kubeconform >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubeconform -strict -summary "$w/sts.img.yaml" || { echo "STOP: kubeconform rejected the StatefulSet, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
else
  echo "NOTE: kubeconform is not on the PATH, that check was skipped"
fi
cp "$w/up.new" "$up.tmp" && chmod --reference="$up" "$up.tmp" && shellcheck "$up.tmp" && echo "shellcheck clean for the new pg-up.sh" || { rm -f "$up.tmp"; echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
mv "$up.tmp" "$up"
cp "$w/sts.new" "$sts"
echo "--- git diff --stat"
git diff --stat
echo "--- git status"
git status --short
)

Next: a pre-check for the cluster steps (needs your go)
The two cluster steps are applying the StatefulSet, which restarts pods, and running the generator in pg-ha. Before them I want to see who leads and whether the new pod template passes the cluster’s admission rules. This block creates and deletes nothing. It reads, and it asks the API server to validate with --dry-run=server, as in the BK-1 rebuild:

Patroni state: patronictl list, and where the pods run.
The PDB: its allowed disruptions.
The two TLS Secrets: whether they exist yet (prediction: they do not).
Admission dry run: the new pod template as a bare Pod under restricted admission, as in the BK-1 rebuild.
StatefulSet dry run: the changed StatefulSet, to check that the update is allowed and what would change.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
r=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$r"' EXIT
echo "--- patroni state"
kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list </dev/null
echo "--- pods and nodes"
kubectl -n pg-ha get pods -l application=pg-ha -o wide --no-headers
echo "--- pdb"
kubectl -n pg-ha get pdb pg-ha --no-headers
echo "--- tls secrets (expected NotFound for both)"
kubectl -n pg-ha get secret pgbackrest-tls-server pgbackrest-tls-client --no-headers 2>&1
echo "--- new pod template under restricted admission (server-side dry run, nothing is created)"
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}|g" k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml >"$r/sts.yaml"
kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$r/sts.yaml" -o json >"$r/sts.json"
jq '{apiVersion:"v1",kind:"Pod",metadata:{name:"pg-ha-psa-check",namespace:"pg-ha",labels:.spec.template.metadata.labels},spec:(.spec.template.spec | .volumes += [{"name":"data","emptyDir":{}}])}' "$r/sts.json" >"$r/pod.json"
kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f "$r/pod.json" 2>&1
echo "--- changed StatefulSet (server-side dry run, nothing is changed)"
kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f "$r/sts.yaml" 2>&1
echo "--- no leftovers (expected NotFound)"
kubectl -n pg-ha get pod pg-ha-psa-check --no-headers 2>&1
)

The StatefulSet dry run says configured (server dry run), which means the update is accepted.

Run every command in the bash block below in your terminal, in ~/postgres-ha-lab. I never run anything. This is step A only. Do not apply the StatefulSet yet.

What the block does:

It checks that both Secrets are absent and that Patroni is healthy. If either is wrong, it stops and changes nothing.
It runs scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh with NS=pg-ha, which creates the 2 Secrets and deletes nothing.
It prints key names by count and the public certificate facts (names, validity). It never prints key content.
It runs the generator a second time and checks that both Secrets are kept.
It saves the output to docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-up.txt. That file is new, so there is one more file to commit and one header entry to add later.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
[ ! -e docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-up.txt ] || { echo "STOP: docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-up.txt already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
export NS=pg-ha
echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
n=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get secret pgbackrest-tls-server pgbackrest-tls-client --no-headers 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
[ "$n" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: $n of the two TLS Secrets already exist, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "precheck: neither TLS Secret exists"
state=$(kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list -f json </dev/null)
l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state")
s=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state")
[ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$s" = 2 ] || { echo "STOP: Patroni is not 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "precheck: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
tmp=$(mktemp -d)
echo "--- generator, first run"
TMPDIR="$tmp" scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh || { rm -rf "$tmp"; echo "STOP: the generator failed"; exit 1; }
echo "leftover files in the generator temp dir: $(find "$tmp" -type f | wc -l) (expected 0)"
for sname in pgbackrest-tls-server pgbackrest-tls-client; do
  echo "$sname keys: $(kubectl -n "$NS" get secret "$sname" -o json | jq -c '[.data | keys[]] | sort') (expected [\"ca.crt\",\"tls.crt\",\"tls.key\"])"
done
w=$(mktemp -d)
kubectl -n "$NS" get secret pgbackrest-tls-server -o json | jq -r '.data["tls.crt"]' | base64 -d >"$w/s.crt"
kubectl -n "$NS" get secret pgbackrest-tls-client -o json | jq -r '.data["tls.crt"]' | base64 -d >"$w/c.crt"
echo "--- public certificate facts (no keys)"
echo "server names:"
openssl x509 -in "$w/s.crt" -noout -ext subjectAltName | grep -o 'DNS:[^, ]*'
echo "client subject: $(openssl x509 -in "$w/c.crt" -noout -subject -nameopt RFC2253)"
echo "server expires: $(openssl x509 -in "$w/s.crt" -noout -enddate)"
echo "client expires: $(openssl x509 -in "$w/c.crt" -noout -enddate)"
rm -rf "$w"
echo "--- generator, second run (expected: both kept)"
TMPDIR="$tmp" scripts/gen-pgbackrest-tls.sh
echo "leftover files in the generator temp dir: $(find "$tmp" -type f | wc -l) (expected 0)"
rm -rf "$tmp"
echo "--- pods untouched (expected 3 pods, 2/2, 0 restarts)"
kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha --no-headers
echo "--- git status"
git status --short
) 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/BK-2-tls-up.txt

GO B

Run every command in the bash block below in your terminal, in ~/postgres-ha-lab. I never run anything. This block restarts the pods.

What it affects (as I listed before):

All 3 pods restart one at a time and gain the pgserver container.
The data volumes, Secrets and ConfigMaps are kept, and nothing is deleted.
Likely order, a prediction: pg-ha-2, then pg-ha-1, then pg-ha-0. If pg-ha-0 (the current leader) restarts, a failover happens, with a short write stall.
What the block does:

It records the state before: Patroni, the archiver counters, and the timeline. It stops if the cluster is not healthy or a Secret is missing.
It applies only k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml, with the image substituted as pg-up.sh does.
It waits up to 600 s for the rollout, then up to 180 s for 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas.
It prints the state after: Patroni, pod restart counts, the pgserver container status and its last log lines, and the archiver counters on the new leader.
It makes no other change. If the rollout times out, it stops and prints the state, and I will not suggest more changes until you paste it.
It saves the output to docs/evidence/BK-2-pgserver-up.txt, a new file.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
[ ! -e docs/evidence/BK-2-pgserver-up.txt ] || { echo "STOP: docs/evidence/BK-2-pgserver-up.txt already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
NS=pg-ha
pl() { kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list "$@" </dev/null; }
anypl() { local p; for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list -f json </dev/null 2>/dev/null && return 0; done; return 1; }
arch() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atqc "select 'archived_count=' || archived_count || ' failed_count=' || failed_count || ' last_archived_wal=' || coalesce(last_archived_wal,'none') from pg_stat_archiver" </dev/null 2>&1; }
echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
n=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get secret pgbackrest-tls-server pgbackrest-tls-client --no-headers 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
[ "$n" = 2 ] || { echo "STOP: expected both TLS Secrets, found $n, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
state=$(pl -f json)
l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state")
s=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state")
[ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$s" = 2 ] || { echo "STOP: Patroni is not 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
leader_before=$(jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .Member' <<<"$state")
echo "--- before: patroni"
pl
echo "--- before: archiver on $leader_before"
arch "$leader_before"
echo "--- apply the StatefulSet (only this file)"
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}|g" k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml | kubectl apply -f -
echo "--- rollout (timeout 600 s)"
rc=0
kubectl -n "$NS" rollout status sts/pg-ha --timeout=600s || rc=$?
echo "rollout exit=$rc"
ok=no
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do
  st=$(anypl || true)
  l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  f=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  if [ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$f" = 2 ]; then ok=yes; break; fi
  sleep 3
done
echo "healthy again within 180 s: $ok"
echo "--- after: patroni"
for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list </dev/null 2>/dev/null && break; done
echo "--- after: pods (container names, ready, restarts)"
kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha -o json | jq -r '.items[] | .metadata.name as $n | .status.containerStatuses[] | "\($n) \(.name) ready=\(.ready) restarts=\(.restartCount)"'
echo "--- after: pgserver last log lines per pod (no secrets are logged at warn level, prediction)"
for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do echo "[$p]"; kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$p" -c pgserver --tail=5 2>&1; done
leader_after=$(anypl | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .Member' || true)
echo "leader before: $leader_before  leader after: ${leader_after:-unknown}"
echo "--- after: archiver on ${leader_after:-unknown}"
[ -n "${leader_after:-}" ] && arch "$leader_after"
echo "--- after: stanza listing on ${leader_after:-unknown} (expects backup.info)"
[ -n "${leader_after:-}" ] && kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader_after" -c postgres -- pgbackrest repo-ls "backup/pg-ha" </dev/null 2>&1
echo "--- git status"
git status --short
) 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/BK-2-pgserver-up.txt

Block 1: fix the manifest (no cluster change)
It adds to the pgserver container the two S3 variables from objstore-credentials (10 lines) and a read-only mount of the existing objstore-ca volume at /certs (3 lines). It computes the diff counts, checks the result with a client-side dry run, kubeconform and a server-side dry run, and copies the file only if all checks pass.

RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
sts=k8s/pg/50-statefulset.yaml
[ "$(git status --short | wc -l)" = 8 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly the 8 known uncommitted entries, nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^              value: "pgbackrest-backup=pg-ha"$' "$sts" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the auth value line is not there exactly once"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^              mountPath: /tls$' "$sts" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the /tls mount line is not there exactly once"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c 'PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY' "$sts" || true)" = 4 ] || { echo "STOP: expected the S3 key variables 4 times (2 existing containers), nothing changed"; exit 1; }
w=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$w"' EXIT
cat >"$w/env.txt" <<'EOF'
            - name: PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: objstore-credentials
                  key: access-key-id
            - name: PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY_SECRET
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: objstore-credentials
                  key: secret-access-key
EOF
cat >"$w/mount.txt" <<'EOF'
            - name: objstore-ca
              mountPath: /certs
              readOnly: true
EOF
add=$(( $(wc -l <"$w/env.txt") + $(wc -l <"$w/mount.txt") ))
awk -v ef="$w/env.txt" -v mf="$w/mount.txt" '
{ print }
$0=="              value: \"pgbackrest-backup=pg-ha\"" { while ((getline l < ef) > 0) print l; close(ef) }
prev=="              mountPath: /tls" && $0=="              readOnly: true" { while ((getline l < mf) > 0) print l; close(mf) }
{ prev=$0 }' "$sts" >"$w/sts.new"
d=$(diff "$sts" "$w/sts.new" || true)
echo "statefulset: removed $(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true), added $(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true) (expected 0 and $add)"
[ "$(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true)" = 0 ] && [ "$(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true)" = "$add" ] || { echo "STOP: diff is not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}|g" "$w/sts.new" >"$w/sts.img.yaml"
kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$w/sts.img.yaml" -o json >"$w/sts.json" || { echo "STOP: the new StatefulSet is not valid, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "pgserver env names: $(jq -r '.spec.template.spec.containers[] | select(.name == "pgserver") | [.env[].name] | join(",")' "$w/sts.json")"
echo "pgserver mounts: $(jq -r '.spec.template.spec.containers[] | select(.name == "pgserver") | [.volumeMounts[].name] | join(",")' "$w/sts.json")"
[ "$(jq '[.spec.template.spec.containers[] | select(.name == "pgserver") | .env[] | select(.name == "PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY" or .name == "PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY_SECRET")] | length' "$w/sts.json")" = 2 ] || { echo "STOP: the S3 variables are not in pgserver, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(jq '[.spec.template.spec.containers[] | select(.name == "pgserver") | .volumeMounts[] | select(.name == "objstore-ca")] | length' "$w/sts.json")" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the objstore-ca mount is not in pgserver, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
if command -v kubeconform >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubeconform -strict -summary "$w/sts.img.yaml" || { echo "STOP: kubeconform rejected it, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
fi
echo "--- server-side dry run (nothing is changed)"
kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f "$w/sts.img.yaml" 2>&1
cp "$w/sts.new" "$sts"
echo "--- git diff --stat"
git diff --stat
)

Facts from block 2 for BK-3 and the CronJob
Retention: the retention-full warning from the spike evidence (line 32) is still the only retention signal. The repo has no retention setting yet. The default type is count, so repo1-retention-full=2 keeps 2 full backups. The help says a backup counts only after it completes, so the expiry test needs 3 full backups before the oldest goes. Each full backup copies the whole database, so this is cheap only because our data is small.
WAL retention: by default WAL expires together with the full backups. I would leave that default, because the help recommends it.
Backup type: the default is incr. As far as I know, an incremental backup with no earlier full backup is promoted to full with a warning (prediction, unverified here).
verify: pgbackrest verify takes --set, --verbose and --output.
Archive progress: the archive runs from segment 1 to 7, and info still says no valid backups.
Wording: SPEC.md lines 58 and 73 are unchanged. BK-2 stays as “CronJob” under option 2.
Still open: the CronJob question
It blocks the BK-2 test and the CronJob. It does not block step D.

One CronJob: daily, with a small script that chooses full or incr from the age of the last full backup in pgbackrest info. With concurrencyPolicy: Forbid there is no overlap. The cost is the script logic, which has to be tested.
Two CronJobs: weekly full and daily incremental. No script logic, but the two can overlap, and Forbid only applies within one CronJob. As far as I know, pgBackRest’s backup lock lives on the host that runs the backup, so two job pods would not see each other’s lock (prediction).
Testing: either way, the test can only trigger a Job from the CronJob by hand. Whether the schedule fires on time stays unverified unless the test sets an every-minute schedule.
I recommend nothing unless you ask.

Not verified
That the S3 fix is enough. The next error may be the CA path, or the key file mode.
The negative controls have still never run.
The incremental-to-full promotion.
The location of pgBackRest’s lock.
I marked 3 predictions in this reply.

# shellcheck source=/dev/null

NS=pg-ha
anypl() { local p; for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list "$@" </dev/null 2>/dev/null && return 0; done; return 1; }
arch() { timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -Atqc "select 'archived_count=' || archived_count || ' failed_count=' || failed_count || ' last_archived_wal=' || coalesce(last_archived_wal,'none') from pg_stat_archiver" </dev/null 2>&1; }
echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
n=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get secret pgbackrest-tls-server pgbackrest-tls-client objstore-credentials objstore-tls --no-headers 2>/dev/null | wc -l)
[ "$n" = 4 ] || { echo "STOP: expected 4 Secrets, found $n, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
state=$(anypl -f json || true)
l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
s=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
[ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$s" = 2 ] || { echo "STOP: Patroni is not 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas, nothing changed"; exit 1; }

anypl

echo "--- rollout (timeout 600 s)"

kubectl -n "$NS" rollout status sts/pg-ha --timeout=600s || rc=$?
echo "rollout exit=$rc"
ok=no
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do
  st=$(anypl -f json || true)
  l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  f=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)

anypl

for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do echo "[$p]"; kubectl -n "$NS" logs "$p" -c pgserver --tail=5 2>&1; done
leader_after=$(anypl -f json | jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .Member' || true)
echo "leader before: $leader_before  leader after: ${leader_after:-unknown}"
echo "--- after: archiver on ${leader_after:-unknown}"
[ -n "${leader_after:-}" ] && arch "$leader_after"
echo "--- after: stanza listing on ${leader_after:-unknown}"
[ -n "${leader_after:-}" ] && kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$leader_after" -c postgres -- pgbackrest repo-ls "backup/pg-ha" </dev/null 2>&1
echo "--- git status"
git status --short

No command to run in this reply. I read your two questions as follows. Tell me if I misread the second one.

Why a BK-2 test is needed
The rules: SPEC.md names tests/bk-schedule.sh and docs/evidence/BK-2.txt for BK-2. AGENTS.md rule 2 says the test exists and fails before the feature code, and rule 7 says a phase is done only when its test passes and the output is saved. Without it BK-2 is not accepted, and RS-3 and BK-4 depend on BK-2 having produced backups.
What it would check: the CronJob exists, and a Job started from it ends successfully. The repository then shows a full backup, and after that an incremental backup. A broken run must make the test fail, so I would add a negative control.
What it cannot show without extra work: that the real schedule fires on time. The test can start a Job by hand, or use an every-minute schedule just for the test.
What blocks it, and what does not
The only blocker is your CronJob decision. The test checks different things for each choice.
Not blockers: the sidecar (done and committed), the RS-3 choice, and the document edits.
If you meant “which option avoids overlapping runs”: one CronJob with concurrencyPolicy: Forbid cannot start a second run while the first still runs. Two CronJobs cannot see each other, so a weekly full and a daily incremental could overlap. That reading of pgBackRest’s lock is a prediction.
RS-3 scheduled: I take it as your decision
SPEC wording: the current RS-3 line already says “Scheduled restore test to a scratch instance, with a data check”, with test tests/rs-scheduled.sh. So I propose no SPEC edit. I read your choice as approving that line unchanged, so confirm if you want something different.
“In short time”: a restore test needs a restore script (RS-1) and at least one backup (BK-2) first. I can only make it fast by ordering. One option is 4a, then RS-1, then RS-3. That would put 4b, BK-3 and PITR after it. You decide the order, and I do not reorder anything on my own.
Where the scratch instance runs, facts for you:
Scratch pod in pg-ha: a CronJob runs a restore into an emptyDir and a throwaway Postgres in the same namespace. It needs no namespace creation and no cluster-wide RBAC. It is the smallest, but it is not a separate namespace, and RS-1 asks for an empty namespace, which is a different test.
A new namespace per run: the CronJob needs permission to create and delete namespaces, a cluster-scoped right. That conflicts with least privilege (SEC-4).
A fixed scratch namespace: created by manifests, with a Role only there. It is smaller than the previous option, but it is a standing namespace outside pg-ha, and your rule says tests use a new empty namespace the plan names.
Costs: a second Postgres runs next to the 3 pods on one laptop, so memory matters (item 40, .wslconfig). The CronJob’s schedule, daily or hourly, is a demonstration choice. Whether a fixed 1-minute test schedule is acceptable for the evidence is yours to decide.
Alerts: OBS-2 needs a “restore test failed” signal. A CronJob provides a Job status for it, which an on-demand script would not.
Questions for you
One CronJob or two for BK-2. It is the only thing blocking the BK-2 test. I recommend nothing unless you ask.
Which RS-3 location (the three above), or tell me to propose a design when we reach it.
Order: keep your list as it is, or the shorter order 4a, RS-1, RS-3.

Contract the BK-2 test fixes (my design choices, tell me if you want any changed)
A CronJob named pgbackrest-backup in pg-ha, in a new file k8s/pg/60-backup-cronjob.yaml, together with a ConfigMap that holds its script.
Schedule: daily at 02:00 UTC (0 2 * * *).
Decision logic: the script reads pgbackrest info --output=json. It runs a full backup if no full backup exists or the last full is older than BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS (default 604800, 7 days). Otherwise it runs an incremental one. Parsing uses python3, which the Dockerfile installs.
Spec:
Forbid, backoffLimit: 0 and activeDeadlineSeconds set.
Same pinned image as the cluster.
No ServiceAccount token, so no new RBAC.
The three pod hosts as TLS pg hosts, with the client Secret.
Log line: backup-cronjob: type=full or type=incr, once per run.
Item 4b: the same test covers incrementals. I would report 4b as covered by this test unless you say otherwise.
What the test checks
Spec: the checks listed above, and no secret value in the CronJob or in any ConfigMap.
Four Jobs built from the CronJob template:
Job 1 has no override. It makes a full backup if none exists, otherwise an incremental one. The full and incremental counts in pgbackrest info rise by exactly the expected amount.
Job 2 has no override and must make an incremental backup.
Job 3 sets the interval to 0 and must make a full backup. This is the control for the decision logic.
Job 4 points all three CA file paths at a nonexistent file. It must fail, and the backup counts must not change. This is the control that a broken run turns the Job red.
Logs: the job logs contain no S3 secret and no private key line, and the scanner is shown firing on planted input first.
Left behind on purpose: the test leaves its backups in the repository, because RS-1 needs one. Retention stays unset until BK-3, so they are kept.
Not tested (gaps for known-limitations later): that Forbid actually blocks an overlapping run (only the setting is checked), that the real schedule fires on time, and which pod a backup reads from.
Red run
The test checks that the CronJob exists right after a healthy-cluster check, so in this run it stops with one FAIL before creating anything. Its exit cleanup sends one delete for Jobs with the test label, with --ignore-not-found.
