tee tests/bk-schedule.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# BK-2 (ADR-0002): one CronJob, pgbackrest-backup, runs scheduled backups through the pgserver endpoints. It makes a full
# backup when none exists or the last full is older than BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS, and an incremental one otherwise.
# Checked here: the CronJob spec (Forbid, schedule, deadline, no ServiceAccount token, pinned image, three TLS pg hosts,
# only the expected Secrets), no secret value in the CronJob or any ConfigMap, then four Jobs built from the CronJob template:
# job 1 (full if no full exists, else incremental), job 2 (incremental), job 3 (interval 0, so full), job 4 (all CA paths
# broken, must fail and must not add a backup). Counts come from pgbackrest info. The job logs hold no secret value.
# Left behind on purpose: the backups, because RS-1 needs one (retention is BK-3).
# Not covered: that Forbid blocks an overlapping run (only the setting is checked), that the real schedule fires on time,
# and which pod a backup reads from. Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CJ=pgbackrest-backup
IMG="${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}"
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  rm -rf "$work"
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete job -l app=bk-schedule-test --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

info_json() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info --output=json </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then echo "$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
count_type() { jq --arg t "$2" '[.[0].backup[]? | select(.type == $t)] | length' <<<"$1"; }

# make_job NAME [KEY=VALUE ...]: a Job from the CronJob's jobTemplate, with env overrides.
make_job() {
  local name=$1 kv key val filter
  shift
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  filter='{apiVersion:"batch/v1",kind:"Job",metadata:{name:$name,namespace:"pg-ha",labels:{app:"bk-schedule-test"}},spec:.spec.jobTemplate.spec}'
  for kv in "$@"; do
    key=${kv%%=*}
    val=${kv#*=}
    filter="$filter | .spec.template.spec.containers[0].env |= (map(select(.name != \"$key\")) + [{name:\"$key\",value:\"$val\"}])"
  done
  k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json | jq --arg name "$name" "$filter" | k apply -f - >/dev/null
}
wait_job() {
  local name=$1 deadline=$((SECONDS + $2)) s f
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    s=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.succeeded}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    f=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.failed}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "${s:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo complete; return 0; fi
    if [ "${f:-0}" -ge 1 ]; then echo failed; return 0; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo timeout
}
RESULT=""
LOG=""
JOBWAIT=600
run_job() {
  local name=$1
  shift
  make_job "$name" "$@"
  RESULT=$(wait_job "$name" "$JOBWAIT")
  LOG=$(k logs "job/$name" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)
  printf '%s\n' "$LOG" >>"$work/logs"
  echo "INFO: $name result=$RESULT, log lines=$(grep -c '' <<<"$LOG" || true)"
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
pass "cluster healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"

cj=$(k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json 2>/dev/null || true)
[ -n "$cj" ] || fail "CronJob $CJ does not exist"
[ "$(jq -r '.spec.concurrencyPolicy' <<<"$cj")" = Forbid ] || fail "concurrencyPolicy must be Forbid"
sched=$(jq -r '.spec.schedule // empty' <<<"$cj")
[ -n "$sched" ] || fail "the CronJob has no schedule"
[ "$(awk '{print NF}' <<<"$sched")" = 5 ] || fail "the schedule does not have 5 fields"
[ "$(jq -r '.spec.suspend // false' <<<"$cj")" = false ] || fail "the CronJob is suspended"
[ "$(jq -r '.spec.jobTemplate.spec.backoffLimit' <<<"$cj")" = 0 ] || fail "backoffLimit must be 0"
d=$(jq -r '.spec.jobTemplate.spec.activeDeadlineSeconds // 0' <<<"$cj")
[ "$d" -gt 0 ] || fail "activeDeadlineSeconds must be set"
[ "$(jq -r '.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.restartPolicy' <<<"$cj")" = Never ] || fail "restartPolicy must be Never"
[ "$(jq -r '.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.containers[0].image' <<<"$cj")" = "$IMG" ] || fail "the image must be $IMG"
[ "$(jq -r '.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.automountServiceAccountToken' <<<"$cj")" = false ] || fail "automountServiceAccountToken must be false"
pass "CronJob spec: Forbid, 5-field schedule, not suspended, backoffLimit 0, deadline, Never, pinned image, no ServiceAccount token"

for i in 1 2 3; do
  h=$(jq -r --arg n "PGBACKREST_PG${i}_HOST" '.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.containers[0].env[]? | select(.name == $n) | .value' <<<"$cj")
  [ "$h" = "pg-ha-$((i - 1)).pg-ha-headless.$NS.svc.cluster.local" ] || fail "PGBACKREST_PG${i}_HOST is not the pod DNS name of pg-ha-$((i - 1))"
  t=$(jq -r --arg n "PGBACKREST_PG${i}_HOST_TYPE" '.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.containers[0].env[]? | select(.name == $n) | .value' <<<"$cj")
  [ "$t" = tls ] || fail "PGBACKREST_PG${i}_HOST_TYPE is not tls"
done
secrets=$(jq -r '[.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec | ((.volumes[]? | .secret.secretName // empty), (.containers[0].env[]? | .valueFrom.secretKeyRef.name // empty))] | unique | join(",")' <<<"$cj")
[ "$secrets" = "objstore-credentials,objstore-tls,pgbackrest-tls-client" ] || fail "the CronJob references unexpected Secrets: $secrets"
pass "three TLS pg hosts with the pod DNS names; only the expected three Secrets are referenced"

sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
kl=$(k get secret pgbackrest-tls-client -o json | jq -r '.data["tls.key"] // empty' | base64 -d | sed -n 2p)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$kl" ] || fail "no key line could be read for the leak control"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$kl" >"$work/pat"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "leak control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $kl inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "leak control: the scanner did not fire on a planted key line"
cms=$(k get configmap -o json)
[ -n "$cms" ] || fail "no ConfigMap listing, so the scan would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$cj" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value appears in the CronJob"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$cms" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value appears in a ConfigMap"
pass "no secret value in the CronJob or any ConfigMap (scanner fired on planted input)"

k delete job -l app=bk-schedule-test --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null

Block 2: create the CronJob files (repo files only)

What the files do:

k8s/pg/60-backup-cronjob.yaml holds a ConfigMap with the script and the CronJob pgbackrest-backup: daily 02:00 UTC, Forbid, backoffLimit: 0, a 1800 s deadline, no ServiceAccount token, and the pinned image through __IMAGE__.
The three pod hosts are TLS pg hosts with the client Secret. Only objstore-credentials, objstore-tls and pgbackrest-tls-client are referenced.
The script reads pgbackrest info --output=json. It makes a full backup if there is none or the last full one is older than 7 days, and an incremental one otherwise. It logs backup-cronjob: type=full or type=incr once.
scripts/pg-up.sh gets one line at its end that applies the file with the image substituted.
Before and after for pg-ha-up.sh, in the block itself:

Before, the last line is kubectl -n pg-ha rollout status sts/pg-ha --timeout=600s.
After, the same line, then sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMG}|g" k8s/pg/60-backup-cronjob.yaml | kubectl apply -f -.
Checks before copying: the old files are as expected, shellcheck passes on the script, the rendered YAML passes a client-side and a server-side dry run, kubeconform, 23 env entries, and exactly the three expected Secrets. If any check fails, nothing is copied.

#!/bin/sh
# BK-2: scheduled backup through the pgserver endpoints. Full if no full backup exists or the last full backup is older
# than BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS, incremental otherwise. The state is the repository (pgbackrest info), not this pod.
set -eu
STANZA="${PGBACKREST_STANZA:?PGBACKREST_STANZA is not set}"
INTERVAL="${BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS:-604800}"
now=$(date +%s)
info=$(pgbackrest --stanza="$STANZA" info --output=json) || { echo "backup-cronjob: cannot read the repository info" >&2; exit 1; }
last_full=$(printf '%s' "$info" | python3 -c '
import json, sys
d = json.load(sys.stdin)
t = [b["timestamp"]["stop"] for s in d for b in s.get("backup", []) if b.get("type") == "full"]
print(max(t) if t else 0)
')
if [ "$last_full" -eq 0 ] || [ $((now - last_full)) -ge "$INTERVAL" ]; then
  type=full
else
  type=incr
fi
echo "backup-cronjob: type=$type"
pgbackrest --stanza="$STANZA" --type="$type" backup
echo "backup-cronjob: done"
EOF
shellcheck -s sh "$w/script.sh" || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean for the script, nothing changed"; exit 1; }

Cause: my check was wrong, not the manifest.

bash

cat >"$w/script.sh" <<'EOF'
#!/bin/sh
# BK-2: scheduled backup through the pgserver endpoints. Full if no full backup exists or the last full backup is older
# than BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS, incremental otherwise. The state is the repository (pgbackrest info), not this pod.
set -eu
STANZA="${PGBACKREST_STANZA:?PGBACKREST_STANZA is not set}"
INTERVAL="${BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS:-604800}"
now=$(date +%s)
info=$(pgbackrest --stanza="$STANZA" info --output=json) || { echo "backup-cronjob: cannot read the repository info" >&2; exit 1; }
last_full=$(printf '%s' "$info" | python3 -c '
import json, sys
d = json.load(sys.stdin)
t = [b["timestamp"]["stop"] for s in d for b in s.get("backup", []) if b.get("type") == "full"]
print(max(t) if t else 0)
')
if [ "$last_full" -eq 0 ] || [ $((now - last_full)) -ge "$INTERVAL" ]; then
  type=full
else
  type=incr
fi
echo "backup-cronjob: type=$type"
pgbackrest --stanza="$STANZA" --type="$type" backup
echo "backup-cronjob: done"
EOF
shellcheck -s sh "$w/script.sh" || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean for the script, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean for the script"

# BK-2 (ADR-0002): one CronJob runs scheduled backups through the pgserver TLS endpoints of the three pods.
# The script is in the ConfigMap below. No secret value is stored here: the S3 keys come from the Secret objstore-credentials

apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap

  namespace: pg-ha
data:
  backup-cronjob.sh: |
EOF

---
apiVersion: batch/v1
kind: CronJob
metadata:
  name: pgbackrest-backup
  namespace: pg-ha
spec:
  schedule: "0 2 * * *"
  timeZone: Etc/UTC
  concurrencyPolicy: Forbid
  startingDeadlineSeconds: 3600
  successfulJobsHistoryLimit: 3
  failedJobsHistoryLimit: 3
  jobTemplate:
    spec:
      backoffLimit: 0
      activeDeadlineSeconds: 1800
      template:
        spec:
          restartPolicy: Never
          automountServiceAccountToken: false

    "                  value: tls" \

    "                  value: /client/ca.crt" \
    "                - name: PGBACKREST_PG${i}_HOST_CERT_FILE" \
    "                  value: /client/tls.crt" \
    "                - name: PGBACKREST_PG${i}_HOST_KEY_FILE" \
    "                  value: /client/tls.key"
  if [ "$i" != 1 ]; then
    printf '%s\n' \
      "                - name: PGBACKREST_PG${i}_PATH" \
      "                  value: /pgdata/data" \
      "                - name: PGBACKREST_PG${i}_SOCKET_PATH" \
      "                  value: /run/postgresql"
  fi
done
cat <<'EOF'

The plan names the namespace pg-restore. Tell me if you want another name. It is created by a manifest and a script (k8s/restore/ and scripts/restore-ns-up.sh, written test first). RS-1, RS-2 and RS-3 would all use it. Each test checks that it starts empty.

Facts for the design (predictions unless marked):

RBAC: a Role in pg-restore only (pods, PVCs, Jobs). No cluster-wide rights, which keeps SEC-4 simple. This avoids the namespace-creation permission I warned about earlier.
Secrets: Secrets are per namespace, so the S3 key and the object-store CA are copied from pg-ha by a script, without printing values. The copy carries the full rights of that key. I would record that in known-limitations.
Reaching the store: the object store’s certificate already names objstore.pg-ha.svc.cluster.local. That is a fact from gen-objstore-secrets.sh. So a restore pod in pg-restore can use it with certificate checks on. The cluster has no NetworkPolicies today, and SEC-3 would later need an allow for this path.
Cost: a second Postgres runs next to the 3 pods on one laptop, so memory matters (item 40, .wslconfig).
Deleting it:

kubectl delete namespace pg-restore would remove the restored Postgres pods, their PVCs, the copied Secrets, the Role and the CronJob in it.
It does not touch pg-ha, the repository or any backup.

Block E2: the green run of tests/bk-schedule.sh
This is attempt 1 of the green run for BK-2. If it fails, I report, and I do not propose a second attempt without a diagnosis.

What it creates in pg-ha:

4 Jobs from the CronJob template, labelled app=bk-schedule-test. Cleanup removes them at exit, so their pods and logs disappear too. The logs it needs are collected before that.
Backups in the repository, which stay on purpose. RS-1 needs one, and retention is unset until BK-3.
If no full backup exists, it makes 2 full backups and 1 incremental one in total (Job 1 full, Job 2 incremental, Job 3 full). Job 4 is the deliberate failure and adds none.
A run can take several minutes. Each Job waits up to 600 s, and the failing one up to 300 s.
It saves to BK-2.txt on a pass, or BK-2-run2-FAILED.txt on a fail, and stops if either exists.

The BK-2 commit worked: 9f230a5, 7 files, 387 insertions, gitleaks found no leaks on the staged diff (about 20 KB, not the history), and git status --short shows 0 entries. Items 4a and 4b are done and committed locally. Four commits are not pushed: 8ffeaca, cb12c19, c97506f, 9f230a5.

BK-3 contract the test fixes (my choices, tell me if you want any changed)
Setting: PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL=2 in the ConfigMap pgbackrest-env, with the default type count. The WAL retention stays at its default, because the pgBackRest help recommends that. The backup Jobs read it, because the CronJob loads that ConfigMap.
Why 2: your repository holds 2 full backups now, so one more full backup is the smallest test that must expire something. The help text says a backup counts only once complete, so the oldest goes after the third full backup.
What the test checks
Setting: it is exactly 2 and the type is count. The CronJob reads the ConfigMap and does not override it. The warning about the missing setting is gone from every job log, with a control that the matcher fires on planted text.
State before: it runs full backups until 2 exist (at most 3 tries) and asserts that exactly 2 exist before the final Job. A third one there would mean retention is not capping the count.
Final full Job: afterwards exactly 2 full backups remain, and the oldest one is gone. The newer one is still there, and the new one is present. Every incremental backup that depended on the oldest is gone too. The total count drops by exactly that number of incrementals, and the pass line prints that number, so a vacuous check is visible.
WAL: the oldest archived WAL moves up, and it does not pass the start WAL of the oldest retained full backup. So nothing needed for a restore from the kept backups is removed.
Logs: no secret value or key line, with the scanner shown firing on planted input first.
Red run
In this run it stops at the setting check, so it creates nothing. Its exit cleanup sends one delete for Jobs labelled app=bk-retention-test, with --ignore-not-found.

RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/bk-retention.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-3-FAILED.txt ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-3.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/bk-retention.sh or a BK-3 evidence file already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee tests/bk-retention.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# BK-3: retention is enforced by pgBackRest settings. PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL=2 (type count, the default) is set in the
# ConfigMap pgbackrest-env, which the backup CronJob reads. Checked here: the setting, that the CronJob does not override it,
# that no job log carries the warning about the missing setting, then the effect: full backups are made until 2 exist, exactly 2
# exist before the final job, and after one more full backup exactly 2 remain, the oldest full backup and the incrementals that
# depended on it are gone, the newer full backup stays, and the oldest archived WAL moves up but not past the start WAL of the
# oldest retained full backup. The job logs hold no secret value.
# Left behind on purpose: 2 full backups (RS-1 needs one).
# Not covered: time-based retention, differential backups, expiry of a backup that is in use by a restore, a backup during
# an expire run. Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CJ=pgbackrest-backup
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  rm -rf "$work"
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete job -l app=bk-retention-test --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

info_json() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info --output=json </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then echo "$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
count_type() { jq --arg t "$2" '[.[0].backup[]? | select(.type == $t)] | length' <<<"$1"; }
full_labels() { jq -r '[.[0].backup[]? | select(.type == "full") | .label] | sort | join(",")' <<<"$1"; }

make_job() {
  local name=$1 kv key val filter
  shift
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  filter='{apiVersion:"batch/v1",kind:"Job",metadata:{name:$name,namespace:"pg-ha",labels:{app:"bk-retention-test"}},spec:.spec.jobTemplate.spec}'
  for kv in "$@"; do
    key=${kv%%=*}
    val=${kv#*=}
    filter="$filter | .spec.template.spec.containers[0].env |= (map(select(.name != \"$key\")) + [{name:\"$key\",value:\"$val\"}])"
  done
  k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json | jq --arg name "$name" "$filter" | k apply -f - >/dev/null
}
wait_job() {
  local name=$1 deadline=$((SECONDS + $2)) s f
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    s=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.succeeded}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    f=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.failed}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "${s:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo complete; return 0; fi
    if [ "${f:-0}" -ge 1 ]; then echo failed; return 0; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo timeout
}
RESULT=""
LOG=""
run_job() {
  local name=$1
  shift
  make_job "$name" "$@"
  RESULT=$(wait_job "$name" 600)
  LOG=$(k logs "job/$name" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)
  printf '%s\n' "$LOG" >>"$work/logs"
  echo "INFO: $name result=$RESULT, log lines=$(grep -c '' <<<"$LOG" || true)"
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
pass "cluster healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"

cm=$(k get configmap pgbackrest-env -o json 2>/dev/null || true)
[ -n "$cm" ] || fail "ConfigMap pgbackrest-env does not exist"
rf=$(jq -r '.data.PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL // empty' <<<"$cm")
[ -n "$rf" ] || fail "PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL is not set in the ConfigMap pgbackrest-env"
[ "$rf" = 2 ] || fail "PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL is $rf, expected 2"
rt=$(jq -r '.data.PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL_TYPE // "count"' <<<"$cm")
[ "$rt" = count ] || fail "the retention type is $rt, expected count"
pass "retention-full is 2 with type count in pgbackrest-env"

cj=$(k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json 2>/dev/null || true)
[ -n "$cj" ] || fail "CronJob $CJ does not exist"
n=$(jq '[.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.containers[0].envFrom[]? | select(.configMapRef.name == "pgbackrest-env")] | length' <<<"$cj")
[ "$n" = 1 ] || fail "the CronJob does not read pgbackrest-env"
n=$(jq '[.spec.jobTemplate.spec.template.spec.containers[0].env[]? | select(.name | startswith("PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION"))] | length' <<<"$cj")
[ "$n" = 0 ] || fail "the CronJob overrides a retention variable in its own env"
pass "the CronJob reads pgbackrest-env and does not override retention"

warn="option 'repo1-retention-full' is not set"
[ "$(grep -F -c "$warn" <<<"WARN: $warn for 'repo1-retention-full-type=count'" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the warning matcher did not fire on planted text"
sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
kl=$(k get secret pgbackrest-tls-client -o json | jq -r '.data["tls.key"] // empty' | base64 -d | sed -n 2p)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$kl" ] || fail "no key line could be read for the leak control"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$kl" >"$work/pat"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $kl inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted key line"
pass "controls: the warning matcher and the leak scanner fire on planted input"

k delete job -l app=bk-retention-test --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null
info0=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info"
echo "INFO: baseline full=$(count_type "$info0" full) incr=$(count_type "$info0" incr)"
guard=0
nf=$(count_type "$info0" full)
while [ "$nf" -lt 2 ]; do
  guard=$((guard + 1))
  [ "$guard" -le 3 ] || fail "could not reach 2 full backups in 3 jobs"
  run_job "bk-retention-test-pre-$guard" BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS=0
  [ "$RESULT" = complete ] || { printf '%s\n' "$LOG" | tail -n 8; fail "preparation job $guard ended with $RESULT"; }
  info0=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info"
  nf=$(count_type "$info0" full)
done

info1=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info before the final job"
nf1=$(count_type "$info1" full)
[ "$nf1" = 2 ] || fail "expected exactly 2 full backups before the final job, found $nf1 (retention is not capping the count)"
oldest=$(jq -r '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full") | .label] | sort | .[0]' <<<"$info1")
newest=$(jq -r '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full") | .label] | sort | .[-1]' <<<"$info1")
{ [ -n "$oldest" ] && [ "$oldest" != null ] && [ -n "$newest" ] && [ "$newest" != null ] && [ "$oldest" != "$newest" ]; } || fail "could not name the oldest and the newest full backup"
dep=$(jq --arg o "$oldest" '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type != "full") | select(.label | startswith($o + "_"))] | length' <<<"$info1")
nb1=$(jq '.[0].backup | length' <<<"$info1")
amin1=$(jq -r '[.[0].archive[].min] | sort | .[0]' <<<"$info1")
keep=$(jq -r --arg l "$newest" '.[0].backup[] | select(.label == $l) | .archive.start' <<<"$info1")
{ [ -n "$amin1" ] && [ "$amin1" != null ] && [ -n "$keep" ] && [ "$keep" != null ]; } || fail "could not read the archive range or the start WAL of the kept full backup"
echo "INFO: before the final job: full=$nf1 oldest=$oldest newest=$newest incrementals depending on the oldest=$dep total=$nb1 archive min=$amin1 kept start=$keep"
pass "exactly 2 full backups exist before the final job"

run_job bk-retention-test-final BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS=0
[ "$RESULT" = complete ] || { printf '%s\n' "$LOG" | tail -n 8; fail "the final job ended with $RESULT"; }
info2=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info after the final job"
nf2=$(count_type "$info2" full)
[ "$nf2" = 2 ] || fail "expected exactly 2 full backups after the final job, found $nf2"
gone=$(jq --arg o "$oldest" '[.[0].backup[] | select(.label == $o)] | length' <<<"$info2")
[ "$gone" = 0 ] || fail "the oldest full backup $oldest was not expired"
kept=$(jq --arg l "$newest" '[.[0].backup[] | select(.label == $l)] | length' <<<"$info2")
[ "$kept" = 1 ] || fail "the newer full backup $newest is gone"
lab2=$(full_labels "$info2")
newfull=$(jq -r --arg l "$newest" '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full") | .label] | sort | .[-1] | select(. != $l)' <<<"$info2")
[ -n "$newfull" ] || fail "no new full backup appeared (labels: $lab2)"
orph=$(jq --arg o "$oldest" '[.[0].backup[] | select(.label | startswith($o + "_"))] | length' <<<"$info2")
[ "$orph" = 0 ] || fail "$orph incremental backups of the expired full backup are still listed"
nb2=$(jq '.[0].backup | length' <<<"$info2")
[ "$nb2" = $((nb1 - dep)) ] || fail "the total went from $nb1 to $nb2, expected $((nb1 - dep))"
pass "the oldest full backup $oldest and its $dep incremental backups were expired, $newest stays, $newfull is new, total $nb1 to $nb2"

amin2=$(jq -r '[.[0].archive[].min] | sort | .[0]' <<<"$info2")
[ -n "$amin2" ] && [ "$amin2" != null ] || fail "could not read the archive range after the final job"
[[ "$amin2" > "$amin1" ]] || fail "the oldest archived WAL did not move up (before $amin1, after $amin2)"
{ [[ "$amin2" < "$keep" ]] || [ "$amin2" = "$keep" ]; } || fail "the oldest archived WAL $amin2 is newer than the start WAL $keep of the kept full backup"
pass "the oldest archived WAL moved up from $amin1 to $amin2 and does not pass the start WAL $keep of the oldest kept full backup"

logs=$(cat "$work/logs")
[ -n "$logs" ] || fail "the collected job logs are empty, so the scans would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c "$warn" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a job log still carries the warning about the missing retention setting"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value or key line appears in a job log"
[ "$(grep -F -c 'PRIVATE KEY' <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a private key marker appears in a job log"
pass "no missing-retention warning, secret value, key line or private key marker in the job logs"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"

The edit, exactly:

File: k8s/pg/25-pgbackrest.yaml, in the ConfigMap pgbackrest-env.

Nothing removed. The type stays at the default, count, and WAL retention stays at its default.
What this ConfigMap feeds: it is read by the postgres, backup and pgserver containers and by the CronJob. In the running pods it has no effect until their next restart, and then only the backup commands use it (prediction: other commands ignore options that do not apply to them). The CronJob reads it at each Job start.

The block checks that the old line is there exactly once and that no retention variable exists yet. It then builds the new file in a temp location, checks the diff counts, and validates the result with a client-side dry run, kubeconform and a server-side dry run. It copies the file only if every check passes.

RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
f=k8s/pg/25-pgbackrest.yaml
[ "$(git status --short | wc -l)" = 3 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly the 3 known uncommitted entries, nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c '^  PGBACKREST_REPO1_STORAGE_CA_FILE: /certs/ca.crt$' "$f" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the CA file line is not there exactly once, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c 'RETENTION' "$f" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: $f already mentions retention, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
w=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$w"' EXIT
awk '{ print } $0=="  PGBACKREST_REPO1_STORAGE_CA_FILE: /certs/ca.crt" { print "  PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL: \"2\"" }' "$f" >"$w/new.yaml"
d=$(diff "$f" "$w/new.yaml" || true)
echo "removed $(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true), added $(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true) (expected 0 and 1)"
[ "$(grep -c '^<' <<<"$d" || true)" = 0 ] && [ "$(grep -c '^>' <<<"$d" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: diff is not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$w/new.yaml" -o json >"$w/raw" || { echo "STOP: the manifest is not valid, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
jq -s '.' "$w/raw" >"$w/j.json" || { echo "STOP: the dry-run output could not be read, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
kinds=$(jq -r '[.[].kind] | join(",")' "$w/j.json")
val=$(jq -r '.[] | select(.metadata.name == "pgbackrest-env") | .data.PGBACKREST_REPO1_RETENTION_FULL' "$w/j.json")
keys=$(jq '.[] | select(.metadata.name == "pgbackrest-env") | .data | length' "$w/j.json")
echo "kinds: $kinds (expected ConfigMap,ConfigMap)"
echo "retention value: $val (expected 2)"
echo "keys in pgbackrest-env: $keys (expected 14)"
[ "$kinds" = "ConfigMap,ConfigMap" ] && [ "$val" = 2 ] && [ "$keys" = 14 ] || { echo "STOP: the checks above are not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
if command -v kubeconform >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubeconform -strict -summary "$w/new.yaml" || { echo "STOP: kubeconform rejected the manifest, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
else
  echo "NOTE: kubeconform is not on the PATH, that check was skipped"
fi
echo "--- server-side dry run (nothing is changed)"
kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f "$w/new.yaml" 2>&1
cp "$w/new.yaml" "$f"
echo "--- git diff --stat"
git diff --stat
echo "--- git status (expected 4 lines)"
git status --short
)

d) What I propose next, and why: RS-1 (item 7), the biggest item.

Before I write any test, there are facts and one design choice for you. The SPEC line says “Full cluster restore into an empty namespace by one script. Pass: restored data matches the sentinel dataset”, with test tests/rs-full.sh.

Namespace: the fixed pg-restore namespace you chose. It is created by a manifest and a script, with a Role in that namespace only. I would write the delete script so it refuses any other name.
Sentinel dataset: I have not seen one in the repo, so my plan is that the test creates it. It writes a table with known rows into appdb, records a checksum, and takes a full backup through the CronJob template. The restored database must give the same checksum. The kept backups hold no sentinel, so the test makes a new backup. That adds a third full backup and expires 20261121-165421F with retention-full=2.
Danger I want written into the design: a restored instance has archive_mode on in the restored settings. If it archives into the production repository and stanza, it would corrupt the archive. The restored instance must have archiving turned off. The test must check that, and I would add a negative control.
Secrets: the S3 key and the object-store CA are copied from pg-ha into pg-restore by a script, without printing values. The restore pods reach the store by its full name objstore.pg-ha.svc.cluster.local, which the certificate already names.
Your design choice, which I will not make:
Option A, single Postgres in pg-restore: one pod runs pgbackrest restore, then Postgres. It is the smallest. It restores the data, but not a cluster, so “full cluster” in the SPEC line is only partly met.
Option B, a 3-pod Patroni cluster in pg-restore: it bootstraps from the repository through a custom Patroni bootstrap method. It is closer to the SPEC wording and exercises more, but it is much larger: a StatefulSet, a Service set, Secrets for Patroni, and probably a restart of the memory budget on one laptop (item 40).
Option C, A first and B later: meets the data check first and leaves the cluster form open.
Costs in rounds of “I give a block, you run it” are a prediction: A about 6 to 8, B about 12 to 16.

What the test needs (the check you asked for in the rules)
The test needs the following before it can pass, so I list it now to avoid a repeat of the R5 problem:

In pg-ha:
A healthy cluster, the repository with the stanza, and the CronJob pgbackrest-backup.
The Secrets pg-credentials, objstore-credentials and objstore-tls.
The object store running.
The restore script scripts/restore-full.sh. The test checks that it exists first and touches nothing if it does not, like the earlier red runs.
In pg-restore: nothing. The script creates the namespace.
Plan: the files and how they work (all new, all predictions until tested)
k8s/restore/, with a manifest set like k8s/pg/:
Namespace and RBAC: pg-restore with the same Pod Security labels as pg-ha, and a Role only in that namespace (ConfigMaps and pods, the same verbs as the patroni Role).
Services: a read-write, a read-only and a headless Service.
StatefulSet: 3 pods, one patroni container each, no backup sidecar and no pgserver. It keeps the hostname anti-affinity and drops the fake-zone spread. It uses the same pinned image, so no new image.
Config: a Patroni config with scope pg-restore and a custom bootstrap method that runs pgbackrest restore. It sets archive_mode=off, and it has no post_bootstrap, because the restored data already contains the app role and a second CREATE ROLE would fail.
scripts/restore-full.sh, the one script SPEC asks for:
It creates the namespace.
It copies pg-credentials and objstore-credentials from pg-ha, and only ca.crt of objstore-tls as a new Secret, without printing any value.
It applies the manifests and waits for 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas.
scripts/restore-down.sh: it deletes only the namespace pg-restore, and it refuses any other name.
tests/rs-full.sh: written first, red first.
docs/decisions/0006-restore-design.md: the ADR for these choices (rule 8). I show the text before it is written.
How the test checks the result
Namespace start: it starts with an empty pg-restore. This needs a delete if the namespace exists, and I list that before each run.
Sentinel: the test creates the table rs_sentinel in the live appdb (1000 rows with an MD5 checksum), then takes a full backup through the CronJob template so the backup contains it.
After the restore script:
3 pods are ready.
Patroni in pg-restore reports 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas.
The row count and the checksum in the restored leader equal the live ones.
The same query also runs on the replicas.
Control for the data check: the same query with one value changed must give a different checksum, so a comparison that cannot fail is ruled out.
Archiving off: archive_mode is off on every restored member. The restored cluster promotes and gets a new timeline, so the test also checks that no WAL file of a newer timeline than the live one appeared in the repository. That is the check that the restore did not write into the production archive. A planted-input control fires first.
pg-ha untouched: its pods have the same restart counts as before.
Leaks: no secret value in the restored pods’ logs, with the scanner shown firing first.

The plan above, including the name pg-restore.
The sentinel table in the live appdb, and the extra full backup. With retention-full=2 that backup expires 20261121-165421F (about 4 MB of repository data). It stays in pg-ha on purpose, because RS-2 needs it. I list this again before that run, and I wait for your go.
Copying pg-credentials into pg-restore. The restored roles carry the live passwords, so the restored cluster must use the same ones. This is how a real disaster recovery works too. The namespace then holds the live database passwords and the S3 read-write key. I write this into known-limitations, and it goes away when you delete the namespace.
That the test deletes and recreates pg-restore itself. It deletes only that namespace: the restored pods, their 3 volumes, the copied Secrets, the Role and the Services. It never touches pg-ha. Each run needs your explicit go, with that list.

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/rs-full.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/RS-1-FAILED.txt ] || [ -e docs/evidence/RS-1.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/rs-full.sh or an RS-1 evidence file already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee tests/rs-full.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# RS-1: full cluster restore into an empty namespace by one script. scripts/restore-full.sh creates the fixed scratch
# namespace pg-restore, copies the Secrets it needs from pg-ha without printing them, and brings up a 3-pod Patroni cluster
# whose bootstrap runs pgbackrest restore from the repository of the live stanza pg-ha, with archiving off.
# Checked here: pg-ha healthy, the BK-2 CronJob and both scripts exist, planted-input controls, then the sentinel table
# rs_sentinel (1000 rows) is written into the live appdb and a full backup is made through the CronJob template; pg-restore is
# deleted (restore-down.sh) and must not exist; restore-full.sh runs; 3 pods ready, 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas; only
# the expected three Secrets exist and objstore-ca holds only ca.crt; row count and checksum equal the live ones on all three
# pods (control: a changed-value checksum equals its live twin and differs from the plain one); archive_mode is off on every
# member; the restored timeline is newer than the repository's newest timeline and that newest timeline is unchanged (nothing
# was written into the live archive); the pg-ha restart counts are unchanged; no secret value in the restored logs.
# Left behind on purpose: the table rs_sentinel in the live appdb (RS-2 needs it), the new full backup (the third full backup
# expires the oldest one, retention-full=2), and the running pg-restore namespace (scripts/restore-down.sh deletes it).
# Not covered: restore of a backup older than the newest, restore with an archive that holds a newer timeline, two restores at
# the same time. Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

RNS=pg-restore
CJ=pgbackrest-backup
SUM_SQL="select count(*) || ':' || md5(string_agg(id::text || ':' || v, ',' order by id)) from rs_sentinel"
CTL_SQL="select count(*) || ':' || md5(string_agg(id::text || ':' || v || 'x', ',' order by id)) from rs_sentinel"
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }
kr() { kubectl -n "$RNS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  rm -rf "$work"
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete job -l app=rs-full-test --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

live_sql() {
  local p
  p=$(current_leader)
  timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atq -c "$1" </dev/null
}
rest_sql() {
  timeout 60 kubectl -n "$RNS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atq -c "$2" </dev/null
}
rest_state() {
  local p
  for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
    timeout 20 kubectl -n "$RNS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list -f json </dev/null 2>/dev/null && return 0
  done
  return 1
}
info_json() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info --output=json </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then echo "$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
tl_of() { sed -E 's#.*/##' | { grep -o -E '^[0-9A-F]{8}' || true; } | sort | tail -n 1; }
repo_tl() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 120 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest repo-ls archive/pg-ha --recurse </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then tl_of <<<"$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
restarts() { k get pods -l application=pg-ha -o json | jq '[.items[].status.containerStatuses[].restartCount] | add'; }
make_job() {
  local name=$1 kv key val filter
  shift
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  filter='{apiVersion:"batch/v1",kind:"Job",metadata:{name:$name,namespace:"pg-ha",labels:{app:"rs-full-test"}},spec:.spec.jobTemplate.spec}'
  for kv in "$@"; do
    key=${kv%%=*}
    val=${kv#*=}
    filter="$filter | .spec.template.spec.containers[0].env |= (map(select(.name != \"$key\")) + [{name:\"$key\",value:\"$val\"}])"
  done
  k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json | jq --arg name "$name" "$filter" | k apply -f - >/dev/null
}
wait_job() {
  local name=$1 deadline=$((SECONDS + $2)) s f
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    s=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.succeeded}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    f=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.failed}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "${s:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo complete; return 0; fi
    if [ "${f:-0}" -ge 1 ]; then echo failed; return 0; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo timeout
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas in pg-ha"
pass "pg-ha healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
k get cronjob "$CJ" >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "CronJob $CJ does not exist (BK-2)"
pass "CronJob $CJ exists"
for s in scripts/restore-full.sh scripts/restore-down.sh; do
  [ -x "$s" ] || fail "$s does not exist or is not executable"
done
pass "restore-full.sh and restore-down.sh are present and executable"

sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
pw=$(k get secret pg-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["superuser-password"] // empty' | base64 -d)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$pw" ] || fail "the superuser password could not be read"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$pw" >"$work/pat"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret key"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $pw inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted password"
[ "$(printf '%s\n' 'x/0000000600000000' | tl_of)" = 00000006 ] || fail "control: the timeline matcher did not fire on planted input"
pass "controls: the leak scanner and the timeline matcher fire on planted input"

r0=$(restarts)
[ -n "$r0" ] || fail "could not read the pg-ha restart counts"
live_sql "drop table if exists rs_sentinel; create table rs_sentinel(id int primary key, v text not null); insert into rs_sentinel select g, md5(g::text) from generate_series(1,1000) g" >/dev/null
live_sum=$(live_sql "$SUM_SQL")
live_ctl=$(live_sql "$CTL_SQL")
[ "${live_sum%%:*}" = 1000 ] || fail "the live sentinel table does not have 1000 rows (got '${live_sum%%:*}')"
[ "$live_ctl" != "$live_sum" ] || fail "control: the changed-value checksum equals the plain checksum, so the data check could not fail"
t_sentinel=$(date +%s)
pass "sentinel table rs_sentinel written in the live appdb: 1000 rows; the changed-value control differs"

k delete job -l app=rs-full-test --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null
make_job rs-full-backup BACKUP_FULL_INTERVAL_SECONDS=0
res=$(wait_job rs-full-backup 600)
[ "$res" = complete ] || fail "the full backup job ended with $res"
info=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info"
last_stop=$(jq '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full") | .timestamp.stop] | max' <<<"$info")
{ [ -n "$last_stop" ] && [ "$last_stop" != null ]; } || fail "could not read the stop time of the newest full backup"
[ "$last_stop" -ge "$t_sentinel" ] || fail "the newest full backup ended before the sentinel was written"
echo "INFO: full backups in the repository: $(jq '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full")] | length' <<<"$info")"
pass "a full backup made after the sentinel exists in the repository"

live_tl=$(live_sql "select timeline_id from pg_control_checkpoint()")
tl_before=$(repo_tl) || fail "cannot list the archive of stanza pg-ha"
[ -n "$tl_before" ] || fail "the archive listing holds no timeline, so the archive check would prove nothing"
echo "INFO: live timeline $live_tl, newest timeline in the repository before the restore: $tl_before"

timeout 300 scripts/restore-down.sh >"$work/down.log" 2>&1 || { tail -n 6 "$work/down.log"; fail "restore-down.sh failed"; }
if kubectl get ns "$RNS" >/dev/null 2>&1; then fail "namespace $RNS still exists after restore-down.sh"; fi
pass "$RNS does not exist: the restore starts from an empty namespace"

rc=0
timeout 1500 scripts/restore-full.sh >"$work/restore.log" 2>&1 || rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] || { tail -n 12 "$work/restore.log"; fail "restore-full.sh failed (exit $rc)"; }
pass "restore-full.sh finished with exit 0"

ok=no
st=""
for _ in $(seq 1 80); do
  st=$(rest_state || true)
  l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  f=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  if [ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$f" = 2 ]; then ok=yes; break; fi
  sleep 3
done
[ "$ok" = yes ] || fail "the restored cluster does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas within 240 s"
pods=$(kr get pods -l application=pg-restore -o json)
[ "$(jq '.items | length' <<<"$pods")" = 3 ] || fail "expected 3 pods in $RNS"
[ "$(jq '[.items[] | select(all(.status.containerStatuses[]?; .ready == true))] | length' <<<"$pods")" = 3 ] || fail "not all 3 restored pods are ready"
pass "3 restored pods are ready; Patroni reports 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"

[ "$(kr get secret -o json | jq -r '[.items[].metadata.name] | sort | join(",")')" = "objstore-ca,objstore-credentials,pg-credentials" ] || fail "$RNS must hold exactly the Secrets objstore-ca, objstore-credentials and pg-credentials"
[ "$(kr get secret objstore-ca -o json | jq -c '[.data | keys[]] | sort')" = '["ca.crt"]' ] || fail "objstore-ca must hold only ca.crt"
pass "$RNS holds exactly the three expected Secrets, and objstore-ca holds only ca.crt (no private key copied)"

rl=$(jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .Member' <<<"$st")
[ -n "$rl" ] || fail "no leader name in the restored cluster"
for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
  got=""
  for _ in $(seq 1 20); do
    got=$(rest_sql "$p" "$SUM_SQL" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$got" = "$live_sum" ]; then break; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  [ "$got" = "$live_sum" ] || fail "restored data on $p differs from the live data (row count '${got%%:*}')"
done
pass "row count 1000 and checksum equal the live ones on all three restored pods"

ctl=$(rest_sql "$rl" "$CTL_SQL")
[ "$ctl" = "$live_ctl" ] || fail "control: the changed-value checksum on the restored leader differs from its live twin"
[ "$ctl" != "$live_sum" ] || fail "control: the changed-value checksum equals the plain checksum"
pass "control: the changed-value checksum matches its live twin and differs from the plain checksum"

for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
  m=$(rest_sql "$p" "show archive_mode" || true)
  [ "$m" = off ] || fail "archive_mode is '${m:-unknown}' on $p, expected off"
done
pass "archive_mode is off on every restored member"

rtl=$(rest_sql "$rl" "select timeline_id from pg_control_checkpoint()")
[[ "$rtl" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || fail "could not read the restored timeline"
rtl_hex=$(printf '%08X' "$rtl")
tl_after=$(repo_tl) || fail "cannot list the archive after the restore"
echo "INFO: restored timeline $rtl_hex, newest repository timeline before $tl_before and after $tl_after"
[[ "$rtl_hex" > "$tl_before" ]] || fail "the restored cluster did not move to a newer timeline, so the archive check proves nothing"
[ "$tl_after" = "$tl_before" ] || fail "the newest timeline in the live archive changed from $tl_before to $tl_after: the restored cluster wrote into the repository"
pass "the restored cluster is on a newer timeline and the live archive got no newer timeline"

r1=$(restarts)
[ "$r1" = "$r0" ] || fail "the pg-ha restart counts changed from $r0 to $r1"
pass "pg-ha was not restarted by the restore"

logs=""
for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
  logs+=$(kr logs "$p" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)$'\n'
done
logs+=$(cat "$work/restore.log")
[ -n "$logs" ] || fail "the collected logs are empty, so the scan would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value appears in a restored pod log or in the restore script output"
pass "no secret value in the restored pod logs or the restore script output"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rs-full.sh
[ "$(tail -n 1 tests/rs-full.sh)" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the test file is incomplete"; exit 1; }
echo "lines: $(wc -l < tests/rs-full.sh)"
shellcheck tests/rs-full.sh || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, no run"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean"
tests/rs-full.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/RS-1-FAILED.txt
echo "exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
)

# ADR-0006: restore into a fixed scratch namespace as a 3-pod Patroni cluster

Status: Accepted (Phase 3).

## Context
RS-1 asks for a full cluster restore into an empty namespace by one script. RS-2 (point in time) and RS-3 (restore test) reuse it.

## Options
1. A single Postgres pod that runs pgbackrest restore. Smallest, but it restores data and not a cluster.
2. A 3-pod Patroni cluster whose bootstrap runs pgbackrest restore. Closer to the SPEC wording, larger.
3. Option 1 first, option 2 later.

## Decision
Option 2. The namespace is the fixed scratch namespace pg-restore, created by scripts/restore-full.sh and removed by
scripts/restore-down.sh, which refuses any other namespace name. Patroni runs a custom bootstrap method that calls
pgbackrest restore from the repository of the live stanza. The restored cluster runs with archiving off
(archive_mode=off and the restore option archive-mode=off), so it never writes into the live repository. It has no
post_bootstrap step, because the restored data already holds the application role. The two replicas are created from the
restored leader by Patroni's normal base backup. The same pinned image is used, so no new image.
The Secrets pg-credentials and objstore-credentials are copied from pg-ha without printing them, and only ca.crt of
objstore-tls, as the Secret objstore-ca. The restored roles carry the live passwords, so the restored cluster needs them.

## Consequences
- pg-restore holds the live database passwords and the read-write object store key until the namespace is deleted.
  The two protections against a write into the live archive are archiving off and the timeline check in tests/rs-full.sh.
- The restored cluster uses about 2.3 GB of memory limits at most on a laptop with about 5 GB available.
- RS-2 and RS-3 reuse the namespace and the script with other restore options.
- NetworkPolicies of SEC-3 must allow pg-restore to reach the object store in pg-ha.

## Not verified yet
That Patroni keeps the restore settings with keep_existing_recovery_conf, that the restored leader promotes by itself,
that the replicas catch up from the leader, and that archiving stays off on every member.
What I need from you before any more files are written
Reply with your answers. I count each as separate approvals.

ADR-0006 text above: approve, change or reject.
The name pg-restore: keep it, or give another.
The sentinel table in the live appdb, and the extra full backup. With retention-full=2 the third full backup expires 20261121-165421F (about 4 MB of repository data). The table stays in pg-ha for RS-2.
Copying pg-credentials and the S3 key into pg-restore, as the ADR says. The restored cluster could write to the live repository if its protections failed.
Deleting and recreating pg-restore in each test run. It deletes the namespace, the restored pods and their 3 volumes, and the copied Secrets, nothing else. I list this again before every run and wait for your go.
What comes next, in order
After your answers: the ADR file, with a guarded block.
The files for the restore, shown for approval before they are written: k8s/restore/ (namespace, Role, Services, StatefulSet, Patroni config with the pgBackRest bootstrap and a restore ConfigMap), scripts/restore-full.sh and scripts/restore-down.sh. Offline checks only: shellcheck, kubeconform, and the server-side dry run. The restricted admission check for the pod template runs in a dry run before any apply.
The green run of tests/rs-full.sh with its own explicit go, because it:
writes rs_sentinel into the live appdb,
makes a full backup and so expires the oldest full backup,
creates the pg-restore namespace, 3 pods and 3 volumes,
and deletes pg-restore first if it exists.
Header entries, the git add list and the commit.
When RS-1 is done I tell you, and then write the handoff for the new chat.
Not verified (predictions, 6):

The header count is 61.
Shellcheck for the files to come.
Patroni needs keep_existing_recovery_conf here.
The restored leader promotes on its own, with a new timeline.
A restore pod reaches the object store across namespaces.
The restored replicas can use the copied replication password.

The namespace name pg-restore: keep it, or give another.

Copying pg-credentials and the S3 key into pg-restore. The restored roles carry the live passwords, and the key can write to the live repository. The two protections are archiving off and the timeline check in the test. I record it in known-limitations.
Deleting and recreating pg-restore in each test run. It deletes the namespace, the restored pods and their 3 volumes, and the copied Secrets, and nothing else. I list this again before every run and wait for your go each time.

Block 1: write ADR-0006 (the approved text)
It stops if the file exists or the tree is not as expected, and it checks the line count after writing.

RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
f=docs/decisions/0006-restore-design.md
[ ! -e "$f" ] || { echo "STOP: $f already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(git status --short | wc -l)" = 3 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly the 3 known uncommitted entries, nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
tee "$f" >/dev/null <<'EOFADR'
# ADR-0006: restore into a fixed scratch namespace as a 3-pod Patroni cluster

Status: Accepted (Phase 3).

## Context
RS-1 asks for a full cluster restore into an empty namespace by one script. RS-2 (point in time) and RS-3 (restore test) reuse it.

## Options
1. A single Postgres pod that runs pgbackrest restore. Smallest, but it restores data and not a cluster.
2. A 3-pod Patroni cluster whose bootstrap runs pgbackrest restore. Closer to the SPEC wording, larger.
3. Option 1 first, option 2 later.

## Decision
Option 2. The namespace is the fixed scratch namespace pg-restore, created by scripts/restore-full.sh and removed by
scripts/restore-down.sh, which refuses any other namespace name. Patroni runs a custom bootstrap method that calls
pgbackrest restore from the repository of the live stanza. The restored cluster runs with archiving off
(archive_mode=off and the restore option archive-mode=off), so it never writes into the live repository. It has no
post_bootstrap step, because the restored data already holds the application role. The two replicas are created from the
restored leader by Patroni's normal base backup. The same pinned image is used, so no new image.
The Secrets pg-credentials and objstore-credentials are copied from pg-ha without printing them, and only ca.crt of
objstore-tls, as the Secret objstore-ca. The restored roles carry the live passwords, so the restored cluster needs them.

## Consequences
- pg-restore holds the live database passwords and the read-write object store key until the namespace is deleted.
  The two protections against a write into the live archive are archiving off and the timeline check in tests/rs-full.sh.
- The restored cluster uses about 2.3 GB of memory limits at most on a laptop with about 5 GB available.
- RS-2 and RS-3 reuse the namespace and the script with other restore options.
- NetworkPolicies of SEC-3 must allow pg-restore to reach the object store in pg-ha.

## Not verified yet
That Patroni keeps the restore settings with keep_existing_recovery_conf, that the restored leader promotes by itself,
that the replicas catch up from the leader, and that archiving stays off on every member.
EOFADR
echo "lines: $(wc -l < "$f") (expected 31)"
git status --short
)
Expected (prediction): lines: 31 and 4 status lines.

Block 2: the restore files, with offline checks
The full text of every file is inside the block, so read it before you run it. Running it is your approval. It writes 7 new repo files and changes nothing in the cluster.

Design points that matter:

Names: the pods are pg-restore-0..2 with label application=pg-restore, and the container is postgres, because tests/rs-full.sh expects exactly those.
Bootstrap: Patroni calls sh /etc/patroni/restore.sh, which creates /pgdata/data and runs pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha --archive-mode=off restore. The settings are no_params: true and keep_existing_recovery_conf: true, and there is no post_bootstrap.
Archiving: archive_mode is "off" in the Patroni config, and the ConfigMap pgbackrest-env has no retention setting, so a restore pod can never expire anything.
Secrets: the StatefulSet reads pg-credentials, objstore-credentials and objstore-ca. No secret value is in any file.
Scripts: both refuse any namespace other than pg-restore. restore-full.sh stops if the namespace already exists.
No backup sidecar and no pgserver. The zone spread is dropped, and the hostname anti-affinity stays.
Checks before copying:

shellcheck on both scripts.
All YAML parses, with exactly the 10 expected objects.
The Patroni config parses with PyYAML, read through the Patroni Python in a live pod, via stdin and with no write.
kubeconform.
The pod template passes restricted admission in a server-side dry run in pg-ha. It is an admission check only, and nothing is created.
If any check fails, nothing is copied.

for s in scripts/restore-full.sh scripts/restore-down.sh; do [ ! -e "$s" ] || { echo "STOP: $s already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }; done
[ -f docs/decisions/0006-restore-design.md ] || { echo "STOP: run block 1 first, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(git status --short | wc -l)" = 4 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly 4 uncommitted entries, nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
w=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$w"' EXIT

# RS-1 (ADR-0006): the fixed scratch namespace for restores. Created by scripts/restore-full.sh, removed by scripts/restore-down.sh.

    pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce-version: latest
EOFNS

cat >"$w/k8s/restore/10-rbac.yaml" <<'EOFRBAC'
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: patroni
  namespace: pg-restore
---
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: patroni
  namespace: pg-restore
rules:
  # Leader lock, cluster config and sync state live in ConfigMaps.
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["configmaps"]
    verbs: ["create", "get", "list", "patch", "update", "watch", "delete", "deletecollection"]
  # Patroni sets the role label on its own pod.
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "patch", "update", "watch"]
---
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1

  name: patroni
  namespace: pg-restore
roleRef:
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
  kind: Role
  name: patroni
subjects:
  - kind: ServiceAccount
    name: patroni
    namespace: pg-restore
EOFRBAC

cat >"$w/k8s/restore/20-config.yaml" <<'EOFCFG'
# No secret is stored here. The restored cluster reads the live stanza pg-ha from the live repository, with archiving off.
# There is no retention setting on purpose: nothing in pg-restore can expire a backup.
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: pgbackrest-env
  namespace: pg-restore
data:
  PGBACKREST_STANZA: pg-ha
  PGBACKREST_PG1_PATH: /pgdata/data
  PGBACKREST_PG1_SOCKET_PATH: /run/postgresql
  PGBACKREST_LOG_LEVEL_CONSOLE: warn
  PGBACKREST_LOG_LEVEL_FILE: "off"
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_TYPE: s3
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_PATH: /pg-ha
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_BUCKET: pgbackrest
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_ENDPOINT: objstore.pg-ha.svc.cluster.local
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_STORAGE_PORT: "3900"
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_REGION: garage
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_URI_STYLE: path
  PGBACKREST_REPO1_STORAGE_CA_FILE: /certs/ca.crt
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: patroni-static-config
  namespace: pg-restore
data:
  patroni.yml: |
    scope: pg-restore
    restapi:
      listen: 0.0.0.0:8008
    kubernetes:
      use_endpoints: false
      scope_label: cluster-name
      role_label: role
      leader_label_value: primary
      standby_leader_label_value: primary
      follower_label_value: replica
      labels:
        application: pg-restore
    bootstrap:
      method: pgbackrest_restore
      pgbackrest_restore:
        command: sh /etc/patroni/restore.sh
        no_params: true
        keep_existing_recovery_conf: true
      dcs:
        ttl: 30
        loop_wait: 10
        retry_timeout: 10
        maximum_lag_on_failover: 1048576
        synchronous_mode: true
        synchronous_mode_strict: false
        synchronous_node_count: 1
        postgresql:
          use_pg_rewind: true
          use_slots: true
          parameters:
            wal_level: replica
            hot_standby: "on"
            max_wal_senders: 10
            max_replication_slots: 10
            wal_log_hints: "on"
            max_slot_wal_keep_size: 1GB
            archive_mode: "off"
            password_encryption: scram-sha-256
            unix_socket_directories: /run/postgresql
    postgresql:
      listen: 0.0.0.0:5432
      data_dir: /pgdata/data
      bin_dir: /usr/lib/postgresql/18/bin
      use_unix_socket: true
      pgpass: /tmp/pgpass
      authentication:
        superuser:
          username: postgres
        replication:
          username: replicator
        rewind:
          username: rewind_user
      pg_hba:
        - local all postgres peer
        - host all all 127.0.0.1/32 scram-sha-256
        - host replication replicator 127.0.0.1/32 scram-sha-256
        - host replication replicator 192.168.0.0/16 scram-sha-256
        - host all all 192.168.0.0/16 scram-sha-256
  restore.sh: |
    #!/bin/sh
    # Patroni bootstrap method: restore the newest backup of the live stanza into an empty data directory.
    # archive-mode=off keeps this cluster from ever pushing WAL into the live repository.
    set -eu
    mkdir -p /pgdata/data
    chmod 0700 /pgdata/data
    exec pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha --archive-mode=off restore
EOFCFG

cat >"$w/k8s/restore/50-statefulset.yaml" <<'EOFSTS'
# RS-1 (ADR-0006): a 3-pod Patroni cluster whose bootstrap restores the live stanza. No backup sidecar, no pgserver.
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: StatefulSet
metadata:
  name: pg-restore
  namespace: pg-restore
  labels:
    application: pg-restore
    cluster-name: pg-restore
spec:
  serviceName: pg-restore-headless
  replicas: 3
  podManagementPolicy: Parallel
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      application: pg-restore
      cluster-name: pg-restore
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        application: pg-restore
        cluster-name: pg-restore
    spec:
      serviceAccountName: patroni
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        runAsUser: 999
        runAsGroup: 999
        fsGroup: 999
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      affinity:
        podAntiAffinity:
          requiredDuringSchedulingIgnoredDuringExecution:
            - topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname
              labelSelector:
                matchLabels:
                  application: pg-restore
                  cluster-name: pg-restore
      containers:
        - name: postgres
          image: __IMAGE__
          imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
          ports:
            - name: postgres
              containerPort: 5432
            - name: patroni
              containerPort: 8008
          envFrom:
            - configMapRef:
                name: pgbackrest-env
          env:
            - name: PATRONI_NAME
              valueFrom:
                fieldRef:
                  fieldPath: metadata.name
            - name: PATRONI_KUBERNETES_NAMESPACE
              valueFrom:
                fieldRef:
                  fieldPath: metadata.namespace
            - name: POD_IP
              valueFrom:
                fieldRef:
                  fieldPath: status.podIP
            - name: PATRONI_KUBERNETES_POD_IP
              value: "$(POD_IP)"
            - name: PATRONI_RESTAPI_CONNECT_ADDRESS
              value: "$(POD_IP):8008"
            - name: PATRONI_POSTGRESQL_CONNECT_ADDRESS
              value: "$(POD_IP):5432"
            - name: PATRONI_SUPERUSER_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: superuser-password
            - name: PATRONI_REPLICATION_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: replication-password
            - name: PATRONI_REWIND_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: rewind-password
            - name: PATRONI_RESTAPI_USERNAME
              value: patroni
            - name: PATRONI_RESTAPI_PASSWORD
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: pg-credentials
                  key: restapi-password
            - name: PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: objstore-credentials
                  key: access-key-id
            - name: PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY_SECRET
              valueFrom:
                secretKeyRef:
                  name: objstore-credentials
                  key: secret-access-key
          startupProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /liveness
              port: 8008
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 60
          livenessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /liveness
              port: 8008
            periodSeconds: 10
            failureThreshold: 6
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /readiness
              port: 8008
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 3
          resources:
            requests:
              cpu: 100m
              memory: 256Mi
            limits:
              memory: 768Mi
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
          volumeMounts:
            - name: data
              mountPath: /pgdata
            - name: patroni-config
              mountPath: /etc/patroni
              readOnly: true
            - name: run
              mountPath: /run/postgresql
            - name: tmp
              mountPath: /tmp
            - name: home
              mountPath: /home/postgres
            - name: objstore-ca
              mountPath: /certs
              readOnly: true
      volumes:
        - name: patroni-config
          configMap:
            name: patroni-static-config
        - name: run
          emptyDir: {}
        - name: tmp
          emptyDir: {}
        - name: home
          emptyDir: {}
        - name: objstore-ca
          secret:
            secretName: objstore-ca
  volumeClaimTemplates:
    - metadata:
        name: data
      spec:
        accessModes: ["ReadWriteOnce"]
        resources:
          requests:
            storage: 2Gi
EOFSTS

cat >"$w/scripts/restore-full.sh" <<'EOFFULL'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# RS-1 (ADR-0006): full cluster restore into the fixed scratch namespace pg-restore, by one script.
# Creates the namespace, copies the Secrets it needs from pg-ha without printing them (pg-credentials, objstore-credentials,
# and ca.crt of objstore-tls as objstore-ca), applies the manifests of k8s/restore and waits for the restored 3-pod cluster.
# Stops if pg-restore already exists (run scripts/restore-down.sh first). The only namespace it accepts is pg-restore.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env

SRC=pg-ha
DST=pg-restore
IMG="${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}"
[ "${1:-$DST}" = "$DST" ] || { echo "restore-full: the only namespace allowed is $DST" >&2; exit 2; }
if kubectl get ns "$DST" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "restore-full: namespace $DST already exists; run scripts/restore-down.sh first" >&2
  exit 1
fi
for s in pg-credentials objstore-credentials objstore-tls; do
  kubectl -n "$SRC" get secret "$s" >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "restore-full: secret $s is missing in $SRC" >&2; exit 1; }
done

kubectl apply -f k8s/restore/00-namespace.yaml >/dev/null
echo "restore-full: namespace $DST created"
for s in pg-credentials objstore-credentials; do
  kubectl -n "$SRC" get secret "$s" -o json \
    | jq --arg ns "$DST" '{apiVersion:"v1",kind:"Secret",metadata:{name:.metadata.name,namespace:$ns},type:.type,data:.data}' \
    | kubectl apply -f - >/dev/null
  echo "restore-full: secret $s copied (values not printed)"
done
kubectl -n "$SRC" get secret objstore-tls -o json \
  | jq --arg ns "$DST" '{apiVersion:"v1",kind:"Secret",metadata:{name:"objstore-ca",namespace:$ns},type:"Opaque",data:{"ca.crt":.data["ca.crt"]}}' \
  | kubectl apply -f - >/dev/null
echo "restore-full: secret objstore-ca created from ca.crt only"

kubectl apply -f k8s/restore/10-rbac.yaml -f k8s/restore/20-config.yaml -f k8s/restore/30-services.yaml >/dev/null
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMG}|g" k8s/restore/50-statefulset.yaml | kubectl apply -f - >/dev/null
echo "restore-full: manifests applied, waiting for the restored cluster"
kubectl -n "$DST" rollout status sts/pg-restore --timeout=900s
echo "restore-full: done"
EOFFULL

cat >"$w/scripts/restore-down.sh" <<'EOFDOWN'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# RS-1 (ADR-0006): delete the fixed scratch namespace pg-restore: its pods, their volumes, the copied Secrets, the Role and the
# Services. It deletes nothing else and refuses any other namespace name. A missing namespace is not an error.
set -euo pipefail
NS=pg-restore
[ "${1:-$NS}" = "$NS" ] || { echo "restore-down: the only namespace this script deletes is $NS" >&2; exit 2; }
if ! kubectl get ns "$NS" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "restore-down: namespace $NS does not exist, nothing to delete"
  exit 0
fi
echo "restore-down: deleting namespace $NS, which holds:"
kubectl -n "$NS" get sts,pod,pvc,secret,cm,svc,role,rolebinding,sa -o name | sed 's/^/  /'
kubectl delete namespace "$NS" --wait=true --timeout=300s
echo "restore-down: namespace $NS deleted"
EOFDOWN

shellcheck "$w/scripts/restore-full.sh" "$w/scripts/restore-down.sh" || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean for both scripts"

: >"$w/kinds"
for f in 00-namespace 10-rbac 20-config 30-services; do
  kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$w/k8s/restore/$f.yaml" -o json >"$w/$f.raw" || { echo "STOP: $f.yaml is not valid, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
  jq -s -r '.[] | if .kind == "List" then .items[].kind else .kind end' "$w/$f.raw" >>"$w/kinds"
done
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}|g" "$w/k8s/restore/50-statefulset.yaml" >"$w/sts.img.yaml"
kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$w/sts.img.yaml" -o json >"$w/sts.json" || { echo "STOP: the StatefulSet is not valid, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
jq -r '.kind' "$w/sts.json" >>"$w/kinds"
kinds=$(sort "$w/kinds" | tr '\n' ',' | sed 's/,$//')
echo "kinds: $kinds"
echo "expected: ConfigMap,ConfigMap,Namespace,Role,RoleBinding,Service,Service,Service,ServiceAccount,StatefulSet"
[ "$kinds" = "ConfigMap,ConfigMap,Namespace,Role,RoleBinding,Service,Service,Service,ServiceAccount,StatefulSet" ] || { echo "STOP: the objects are not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
cn=$(jq -r '[.spec.template.spec.containers[].name] | join(",")' "$w/sts.json")
rep=$(jq -r '.spec.replicas' "$w/sts.json")
echo "containers: $cn (expected postgres), replicas: $rep (expected 3)"
[ "$cn" = postgres ] && [ "$rep" = 3 ] || { echo "STOP: the StatefulSet is not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
if command -v kubeconform >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  cp "$w"/k8s/restore/0*.yaml "$w"/k8s/restore/1*.yaml "$w"/k8s/restore/2*.yaml "$w"/k8s/restore/30-services.yaml "$w/" 2>/dev/null || true
  kubeconform -strict -summary "$w/00-namespace.yaml" "$w/10-rbac.yaml" "$w/20-config.yaml" "$w/30-services.yaml" "$w/sts.img.yaml" || { echo "STOP: kubeconform rejected a file, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
else
  echo "NOTE: kubeconform is not on the PATH, that check was skipped"
fi
echo "--- Patroni config parsed with PyYAML in a live pod (stdin only, nothing is written)"
kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$w/k8s/restore/20-config.yaml" -o json \
  | jq -s -r '.[] | select(.kind == "ConfigMap" and .metadata.name == "patroni-static-config") | .data["patroni.yml"]' >"$w/patroni.yml"
[ -s "$w/patroni.yml" ] || { echo "STOP: could not extract patroni.yml, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
kubectl -n pg-ha exec -i pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- python3 -c 'import sys,yaml; d=yaml.safe_load(sys.stdin); b=d["bootstrap"]; print("scope=%s method=%s command=%s keep=%s no_params=%s archive_mode=%s post_bootstrap=%s" % (d["scope"], b["method"], b[b["method"]]["command"], b[b["method"]]["keep_existing_recovery_conf"], b[b["method"]]["no_params"], b["dcs"]["postgresql"]["parameters"]["archive_mode"], b.get("post_bootstrap","none")))' <"$w/patroni.yml"
echo "--- pod template under restricted admission (server-side dry run in pg-ha, nothing is created)"
jq '{apiVersion:"v1",kind:"Pod",metadata:{name:"pg-restore-psa-check",namespace:"pg-ha",labels:.spec.template.metadata.labels},spec:(.spec.template.spec | .volumes += [{"name":"data","emptyDir":{}}])}' "$w/sts.json" >"$w/pod.json"
kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f "$w/pod.json" 2>&1
echo "--- no leftover (expected NotFound)"
kubectl -n pg-ha get pod pg-restore-psa-check --no-headers 2>&1 || true

mkdir -p "$d"
cp "$w"/k8s/restore/00-namespace.yaml "$w"/k8s/restore/10-rbac.yaml "$w"/k8s/restore/20-config.yaml "$w"/k8s/restore/30-services.yaml "$w"/k8s/restore/50-statefulset.yaml "$d/"

- Retention is retention-full=2 (type count). BK-3 shows the oldest full backup and its incremental are expired. Not tested:
  time-based retention, differential backups, expiry during a restore. After expiry, point-in-time recovery to a time
  before the oldest kept full backup is no longer possible.
- The pgBackRest server certificates are valid for 825 days (to Feb 2029) with no rotation. The CA key is removed with rm,
  which is not a secure erase. Authorisation by certificate name and stanza is configured but not tested, because the
  cluster holds no CA key to sign another name.
- The restore namespace pg-restore holds the live database passwords and the read-write object store key until it is deleted
  (ADR-0006). The restored cluster has the same Patroni system identifier as the live one. Only archive_mode=off keeps it
  from writing into the live archive, and tests/rs-full.sh checks the timeline.
- RS-1 did not cover restoring an older backup, an archive that holds a newer timeline, two restores at the same time, or a
  failed restore script.

  and no write probe ran. Overnight, pg-ha-2 failed one liveness probe (about 02:24 UTC) and one readiness probe (about

RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab && (
echo "=== identifiers in git history (counts only)"
line=$(grep -E '^pat=' .githooks/pre-commit || true)
[ -n "$line" ] && [ "$(grep -c '' <<<"$line")" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly one pat= line in the hook, no scan"; exit 1; }
case "$line" in
  pat=\"*\") ;;
  *) echo "STOP: the pat= line is not a plain double-quoted string, no scan"; exit 1 ;;
esac
inner=${line#pat=\"}
pat=${inner%\"}
case "$pat" in
  *'$'*|*'`'*|*'"'*) echo "STOP: the pattern holds shell expansion characters, no scan"; exit 1 ;;
esac
[ -n "$pat" ] || { echo "STOP: the pattern is empty, no scan"; exit 1; }
echo "pattern length: ${#pat} characters (text not printed)"
IFS='|' read -r -a alts <<<"$pat"
fired=0
for a in "${alts[@]}"; do
  [ -n "$a" ] || continue
  n=$(printf '%s\n' "$a" | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)
  [ "${n:-0}" -ge 1 ] && fired=$((fired + 1))
done
echo "control: the pattern matches its own alternatives for $fired of ${#alts[@]} (must be above 0)"
[ "$fired" -gt 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the control did not fire, so the scan would prove nothing"; exit 1; }
revs=$(git rev-list --all)
echo "commits: $(grep -c '' <<<"$revs")"
printf 'pgbackrest\n' >/tmp/ctl.$$
c=$(git grep -I -F -l -f /tmp/ctl.$$ $revs | wc -l)
rm -f /tmp/ctl.$$
echo "control: git grep over all commits finds a known word in $c file versions (must be above 0)"
[ "$c" -gt 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the git grep control found nothing"; exit 1; }
hits=$(git grep -I -l -i -E "$pat" $revs || true)
echo "file versions with the pattern in their content: $(grep -c . <<<"$hits")"
[ -z "$hits" ] || { echo "paths (names that match the pattern are hidden):"; printf '%s\n' "$hits" | cut -d: -f2- | sort -u | grep -v -i -E "$pat" || true; }
echo "commit message lines with the pattern: $(git log --all --format=%B | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "author and committer lines with the pattern: $(git log --all --format='%an %ae %cn %ce' | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "file paths with the pattern, across all commits: $(git log --all --name-only --format= | sort -u | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "ref names with the pattern: $(git for-each-ref --format='%(refname)' | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "binary files are skipped by this scan"
echo "=== redaction marker in the committed logs (counts per file)"
git grep -c 'REDACTED:company' HEAD -- docs/ai-process/redacted || true
echo "=== log capture helpers"
ls docs/ai-process/redacted docs/ai-process/prompts
cat docs/ai-process/markers-phase3-chat.txt
cat scripts/check-log.sh
) 2>&1 | cat
