Run the one bash block below in your terminal, in ~/postgres-ha-lab. I never run anything. It reads files and git history, and it prints counts and public file names, never the pattern or any matched text.

What happened
The block stopped on its own safety check, and nothing was scanned. The hook’s pat= line holds shell expansion characters, which I had predicted it would not. The masked hook output fits a pattern built inside one quoted string, for example by a command substitution. I cannot confirm that without seeing it, and I did not look.
Counts only, as before. The scan never prints the pattern.
My plan changes in one place. The hook runs this exact line on every commit, so evaluating the one pat= line in a subshell is the same step git already takes for you. The block writes nothing to disk and prints only the pattern length, never the pattern. If the line does not give a non-empty one-line pattern, it stops and scans nothing.
The block
It does the following:

Evaluates only the pat= line, in a subshell.
Checks that the pattern has one line and a plausible length, and that it matches its own alternatives.
Checks that git grep can find a known word across all commits.
Counts matches in file content, commit messages, author and committer lines, file paths and ref names across all 34 commits. Binary files are skipped.
Prints the redaction marker counts from the committed logs, then the log capture helpers, as in my last message.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab && (
echo "=== identifiers in git history (counts only)"
line=$(grep -E '^pat=' .githooks/pre-commit || true)
[ -n "$line" ] && [ "$(grep -c '' <<<"$line")" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly one pat= line in the hook, no scan"; exit 1; }
echo "hook line: $(grep -o -F '$(' <<<"$line" | grep -c '' || true) command substitutions, $(grep -o -F '`' <<<"$line" | grep -c '' || true) backticks (text not printed)"
pat=""
eval "$line"
[ -n "$pat" ] || { echo "STOP: the evaluated pattern is empty, no scan"; exit 1; }
[ "$(printf '%s\n' "$pat" | grep -c '')" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the evaluated pattern spans several lines, no scan"; exit 1; }
echo "pattern length: ${#pat} characters (text not printed)"
IFS='|' read -r -a alts <<<"$pat"
fired=0
for a in "${alts[@]}"; do
  [ -n "$a" ] || continue
  n=$(printf '%s\n' "$a" | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)
  [ "${n:-0}" -ge 1 ] && fired=$((fired + 1))
done
echo "alternatives: ${#alts[@]}, matching themselves: $fired (must be above 0)"
[ "$fired" -gt 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the control did not fire, so the scan would prove nothing"; exit 1; }
revs=$(git rev-list --all)
echo "commits: $(grep -c '' <<<"$revs")"
printf 'pgbackrest\n' >/tmp/ctl.$$
c=$(git grep -I -F -l -f /tmp/ctl.$$ $revs | wc -l)
rm -f /tmp/ctl.$$
echo "control: git grep over all commits finds a known word in $c file versions (must be above 0)"
[ "$c" -gt 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the git grep control found nothing"; exit 1; }
hits=$(git grep -I -l -i -E "$pat" $revs || true)
echo "file versions with the pattern in their content: $(grep -c . <<<"$hits")"
[ -z "$hits" ] || { echo "paths (names that match the pattern are hidden):"; printf '%s\n' "$hits" | cut -d: -f2- | sort -u | grep -v -i -E "$pat" || true; }
echo "commit message lines with the pattern: $(git log --all --format=%B | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "author and committer lines with the pattern: $(git log --all --format='%an %ae %cn %ce' | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "file paths with the pattern, across all commits: $(git log --all --name-only --format= | sort -u | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "ref names with the pattern: $(git for-each-ref --format='%(refname)' | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)"
echo "binary files are skipped by this scan"
echo "=== redaction marker in the committed logs (counts per file)"
git grep -c 'REDACTED:company' HEAD -- docs/ai-process/redacted || true
echo "=== log capture helpers"
ls docs/ai-process/redacted docs/ai-process/prompts
cat docs/ai-process/markers-phase3-chat.txt
cat scripts/check-log.sh
) 2>&1 | cat

Block 1: B, a short live test of the CronJob controller
What it changes, and what it does not:

The controller should create a Job at once, because the CronJob has never run and startingDeadlineSeconds is 3600 (prediction). As soon as a Job appears, the block restores the schedule. A trap restores it on any exit, including Ctrl-C.
Backup it adds: one Job runs the backup script. The newest full backup is from 04:16 UTC today, under 7 days old, so I predict type=incr. An incremental backup is small and expires nothing. If it chose full instead, that would be a third full backup, and it would expire the oldest one, 20261121-172516F. The block prints the type and the counts before and after.
Left behind: the Job stays (the history limit is 3), together with one incremental backup. Nothing is deleted or restarted.
After the test: a check that the live object equals the committed file (kubectl diff, exit 0 predicted).
Evidence: it saves to docs/evidence/BK-2-cron-fire.txt, a new file, and stops if it exists. It prints only backup-cronjob: log lines, counts and names.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
[ ! -e docs/evidence/BK-2-cron-fire.txt ] || { echo "STOP: docs/evidence/BK-2-cron-fire.txt already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
NS=pg-ha
CJ=pgbackrest-backup
anypl() { local p; for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list "$@" </dev/null 2>/dev/null && return 0; done; return 1; }
counts() {
  local p out f i
  for p in pg-ha-2 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-0; do
    if out=$(timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info --output=json </dev/null 2>/dev/null) && [ -n "$out" ]; then
      f=$(jq '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full")] | length' <<<"$out")
      i=$(jq '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "incr")] | length' <<<"$out")
      echo "full=$f incr=$i"
      return 0
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
cj_jobs() { kubectl -n "$NS" get jobs -o json | jq -r --arg cj "$CJ" '[.items[] | select(any(.metadata.ownerReferences[]?; .kind == "CronJob" and .name == $cj)) | .metadata.name] | sort | join(",")'; }
echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
state=$(anypl -f json || true)
l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
s=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
[ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$s" = 2 ] || { echo "STOP: Patroni is not 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
orig=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get cronjob "$CJ" -o jsonpath='{.spec.schedule}')
[ "$orig" = "0 2 * * *" ] || { echo "STOP: the live schedule is not 0 2 * * *, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(kubectl -n "$NS" get cronjob "$CJ" -o jsonpath='{.spec.suspend}')" != true ] || { echo "STOP: the CronJob is suspended, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ -z "$(cj_jobs)" ] || { echo "STOP: a Job from the CronJob already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "precheck: Patroni healthy, schedule '$orig', not suspended, no Job from the CronJob yet"
echo "--- before: backups $(counts)"
echo "--- before: kube-controller-manager (read-only)"
kubectl -n kube-system get pods -l component=kube-controller-manager --no-headers
echo "lines with cronjob in its log, last 15 h: $(kubectl -n kube-system logs -l component=kube-controller-manager --since=15h --tail=-1 2>&1 | grep -c -i cronjob || true)"
restored=no
restore() {
  if [ "$restored" = no ]; then
    if kubectl -n "$NS" patch cronjob "$CJ" --type merge -p "{\"spec\":{\"schedule\":\"$orig\"}}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then restored=yes; else echo "WARNING: the schedule could not be restored, run: kubectl -n pg-ha apply -f k8s/pg/60-backup-cronjob.yaml (after the sed image step)"; fi
  fi
}
trap restore EXIT
trap 'exit 130' INT TERM HUP
echo "--- patch the live schedule to every 2 minutes (temporary)"
kubectl -n "$NS" patch cronjob "$CJ" --type merge -p '{"spec":{"schedule":"*/2 * * * *"}}'
job=""
deadline=$((SECONDS + 300))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  job=$(cj_jobs | cut -d, -f1)
  [ -n "$job" ] && break
  sleep 5
done
echo "--- restore the committed schedule now"
restore
echo "schedule now: $(kubectl -n "$NS" get cronjob "$CJ" -o jsonpath='{.spec.schedule}') (expected $orig)"
if [ -z "$job" ]; then
  echo "RESULT: no Job appeared from the CronJob within 300 s"
else
  echo "RESULT: the CronJob controller created Job $job at $(kubectl -n "$NS" get job "$job" -o jsonpath='{.metadata.creationTimestamp}')"
  res=none
  deadline=$((SECONDS + 300))
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    sc=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get job "$job" -o jsonpath='{.status.succeeded}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    fl=$(kubectl -n "$NS" get job "$job" -o jsonpath='{.status.failed}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "${sc:-0}" = 1 ]; then res=complete; break; fi
    if [ "${fl:-0}" -ge 1 ]; then res=failed; break; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  [ "$res" != none ] || res=timeout
  echo "job result: $res"
  echo "backup-cronjob lines in the job log:"
  kubectl -n "$NS" logs "job/$job" --all-containers 2>&1 | grep 'backup-cronjob:' || true
  echo "Jobs from the CronJob now: $(cj_jobs) (expected exactly one)"
fi
echo "lastScheduleTime: $(kubectl -n "$NS" get cronjob "$CJ" -o jsonpath='{.status.lastScheduleTime}')"
echo "--- after: backups $(counts)"
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}|g" k8s/pg/60-backup-cronjob.yaml | kubectl diff -f - >/dev/null 2>&1
echo "kubectl diff exit=$? (0 means the live objects equal the committed file)"
echo "--- after: kube-controller-manager, last cronjob lines (read-only, cut to 200 characters)"
kubectl -n kube-system logs -l component=kube-controller-manager --since=10m --tail=-1 2>&1 | grep -i cronjob | tail -n 6 | cut -c1-200 || true
echo "--- pods untouched (expected 3 pods, 3/3, same ages)"
kubectl -n "$NS" get pods -l application=pg-ha --no-headers
echo "--- git status"
git status --short
) 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/BK-2-cron-fire.txt

## Backups, retention and restore (Phase 3, ADR-0002, ADR-0006)
- The BK-2 CronJob (daily 02:00 UTC) did not fire on its own: it was created on 2026-11-21 at about 16:53 UTC, and the 02:00 UTC
  run on 2026-11-22 left no Job and no event (lastScheduleTime was unset at 04:20 UTC). The cause was not found. With a temporary
  schedule of every 2 minutes the controller created a Job about 2 minutes later and it completed (docs/evidence/BK-2-cron-fire.txt),
  so the controller can start this CronJob. The real 02:00 firing is not shown. tests/bk-schedule.sh uses Jobs started by hand from
  the CronJob template.
- concurrencyPolicy Forbid is checked as a setting only. An overlapping run was not tested.
- Job 4 of tests/bk-schedule.sh (broken CA paths) must fail, and it does, but the test does not check why. Found by reading it.
- Retention is retention-full=2 (type count). BK-3 shows the oldest full backup and its incremental are expired. Not tested:
  time-based retention, differential backups, expiry during a restore. After expiry, point-in-time recovery to a time
  before the oldest kept full backup is no longer possible.
- The pgBackRest server certificates are valid for 825 days (to Feb 2029) with no rotation. The CA key is removed with rm,
  which is not a secure erase. Authorisation by certificate name and stanza is configured but not tested, because the
  cluster holds no CA key to sign another name.
- The restore namespace pg-restore holds the live database passwords and the read-write object store key until it is deleted
  (ADR-0006). The restored cluster has the same Patroni system identifier as the live one. Only archive_mode=off keeps it
  from writing into the live archive, and tests/rs-full.sh checks the timeline.
- RS-1 did not cover restoring an older backup, an archive that holds a newer timeline, two restores at the same time, or a
  failed restore script.
- No NetworkPolicy exists. The pgserver port (8432) and the path from pg-restore to the object store in pg-ha are open. SEC-3
  must allow both.
- Observed and not investigated: the second rolling update moved the timeline from 2 to 5 (three promotions in one update),
  and no write probe ran. Overnight, pg-ha-2 failed one liveness probe (about 02:24 UTC) and one readiness probe (about
  04:04 UTC) and pg-ha-0 one readiness probe (about 04:17 UTC), with no restart and no promotion.
- History scan: gitleaks over 34 commits found nothing, a scan for the current secret values found 0 files, and a scan with
  the pre-commit hook's pattern found 0 matches in file content, commit messages, author lines, file paths and refs. Not
  covered: secret values from before the BK-1 rebuild, binary files, and objects that GitHub served before the repository was
  recreated.

--- restore target-action
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'restore' command - 'target-action' option help

Action to take when recovery target is reached.

When hot_standby=on, the default since PostgreSQL 10, this option consistently
controls what the cluster does when the target is reached or there is no more
WAL in the archive.

When hot_standby=off in PostgreSQL >= 12, pause acts like shutdown. When
hot_standby=off in PostgreSQL < 12, pause acts like promote.

The following actions are supported:

pause - pause when recovery target is reached.
promote - promote and switch timeline when recovery target is reached.
shutdown - shutdown server when recovery target is reached. (PostgreSQL >=
9.5)
--- restore target-exclusive
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'restore' command - 'target-exclusive' option help
Stop just before the recovery target is reached.

Defines whether recovery to the target would be exclusive (the default is
inclusive) and is only valid when --type is lsn, time or xid. For example,
using --target-exclusive would exclude the contents of transaction 1007 when
--type=xid and --target=1007. See the recovery_target_inclusive option in the
PostgreSQL docs for more information.
--- restore set
pgBackRest 2.60.0 - 'restore' command - 'set' option help

Backup set to restore.

The backup set to be restored. latest will restore the latest backup, otherwise
provide the name of the backup to restore.

default: latest

=== restore.sh in the committed manifest
  restore.sh: |
    #!/bin/sh
    # Patroni bootstrap method: restore the newest backup of the live stanza into an empty data directory.
    # archive-mode=off keeps this cluster from ever pushing WAL into the live repository.
    set -eu
    mkdir -p /pgdata/data
    chmod 0700 /pgdata/data
    exec pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha --archive-mode=off restore

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/rs-pitr.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/RS-2-FAILED.txt ] || [ -e docs/evidence/RS-2.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/rs-pitr.sh or an RS-2 evidence file already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee tests/rs-pitr.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# RS-2: point-in-time recovery to a timestamp. scripts/restore-full.sh must read RESTORE_TYPE, RESTORE_TARGET and
# RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION (static check with controls). Then the table rs_pitr is written into the live appdb: 500 "before"
# rows, a pause, the target time taken from the database clock, a pause, 500 "after" rows; the WAL is switched and the test
# waits until the segment is archived. pg-restore is deleted (restore-down.sh) and must not exist; restore-full.sh runs with
# type time, that target and action promote. On all three restored pods: 500 before rows with the live checksum, 0 after rows,
# 500 rows in total (the live table holds 1000, so the check can fail); 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas; archive_mode off;
# the restored timeline is newer than the newest timeline in the live archive and that timeline is unchanged; pg-ha was not
# restarted; no secret value in the restored logs or the script output.
# Left behind on purpose: the table rs_pitr in the live appdb and the running pg-restore namespace (RS-3 reuses it).
# Not covered: a target before the oldest kept backup, a target inside a failover, exclusive targets, recovery types other than
# time. Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

RNS=pg-restore
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }
kr() { kubectl -n "$RNS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() { rm -rf "$work"; }
trap cleanup EXIT

live_sql() {
  local p
  p=$(current_leader)
  timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atq -c "$1" </dev/null
}
rest_sql() {
  timeout 60 kubectl -n "$RNS" exec "$1" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atq -c "$2" </dev/null
}
rest_state() {
  local p
  for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
    timeout 20 kubectl -n "$RNS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list -f json </dev/null 2>/dev/null && return 0
  done
  return 1
}
tl_of() { sed -E 's#.*/##' | { grep -o -E '^[0-9A-F]{8}' || true; } | sort | tail -n 1; }
repo_tl() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 120 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest repo-ls archive/pg-ha --recurse </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then tl_of <<<"$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
restarts() { k get pods -l application=pg-ha -o json | jq '[.items[].status.containerStatuses[].restartCount] | add'; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas in pg-ha"
pass "pg-ha healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
for s in scripts/restore-full.sh scripts/restore-down.sh; do
  [ -x "$s" ] || fail "$s does not exist or is not executable"
done
pass "restore-full.sh and restore-down.sh are present and executable"

[ "$(printf '%s\n' 'x RESTORE_TARGET:- y' | grep -c -E 'RESTORE_TARGET([^A-Z_]|$)' || true)" = 1 ] || fail "control: the static matcher did not fire on planted input"
[ "$(printf '%s\n' 'x RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION y' | grep -c -E 'RESTORE_TARGET([^A-Z_]|$)' || true)" = 0 ] || fail "control: the matcher for RESTORE_TARGET also matches RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION"
pass "controls: the static matcher fires on planted input and tells RESTORE_TARGET from RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION"
for v in RESTORE_TYPE RESTORE_TARGET RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION; do
  [ "$(grep -c -E "${v}([^A-Z_]|\$)" scripts/restore-full.sh || true)" -ge 1 ] || fail "scripts/restore-full.sh does not read $v (point-in-time target not supported yet)"
done
pass "restore-full.sh reads RESTORE_TYPE, RESTORE_TARGET and RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION"

sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
pw=$(k get secret pg-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["superuser-password"] // empty' | base64 -d)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$pw" ] || fail "the superuser password could not be read"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$pw" >"$work/pat"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret key"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $pw inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted password"
[ "$(printf '%s\n' 'x/0000000600000000' | tl_of)" = 00000006 ] || fail "control: the timeline matcher did not fire on planted input"
pass "controls: the leak scanner and the timeline matcher fire on planted input"

r0=$(restarts)
[ -n "$r0" ] || fail "could not read the pg-ha restart counts"
live_sql "drop table if exists rs_pitr; create table rs_pitr(id int primary key, phase text not null); insert into rs_pitr select g, 'before' from generate_series(1,500) g" >/dev/null
sleep 2
target=$(live_sql "select to_char(clock_timestamp() at time zone 'UTC', 'YYYY-MM-DD HH24:MI:SS.US') || '+00'")
[[ "$target" =~ ^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}\ [0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}\.[0-9]{6}\+00$ ]] || fail "the target time '$target' does not have the expected format"
sleep 2
live_sql "insert into rs_pitr select g, 'after' from generate_series(501,1000) g" >/dev/null
live_before=$(live_sql "select count(*) from rs_pitr where phase = 'before'")
live_after=$(live_sql "select count(*) from rs_pitr where phase = 'after'")
live_md5=$(live_sql "select md5(string_agg(id::text, ',' order by id)) from rs_pitr where phase = 'before'")
{ [ "$live_before" = 500 ] && [ "$live_after" = 500 ]; } || fail "the live table does not hold 500 before and 500 after rows (got $live_before and $live_after)"
pass "live rs_pitr holds 500 before rows and 500 after rows; the target time is $target"

seg=$(live_sql "select pg_walfile_name(pg_current_wal_lsn())")
live_sql "select pg_switch_wal()" >/dev/null
la=""
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do
  la=$(live_sql "select coalesce(last_archived_wal, '') from pg_stat_archiver" || true)
  if [[ "$la" > "$seg" ]] || [ "$la" = "$seg" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
{ [[ "$la" > "$seg" ]] || [ "$la" = "$seg" ]; } || fail "WAL segment $seg was not archived within 180 s (last archived '$la')"
pass "WAL segment $seg, which holds the after rows, is archived"

tl_before=$(repo_tl) || fail "cannot list the archive of stanza pg-ha"
[ -n "$tl_before" ] || fail "the archive listing holds no timeline, so the archive check would prove nothing"
echo "INFO: newest timeline in the live archive before the restore: $tl_before"

timeout 300 scripts/restore-down.sh >"$work/down.log" 2>&1 || { tail -n 6 "$work/down.log"; fail "restore-down.sh failed"; }
if kubectl get ns "$RNS" >/dev/null 2>&1; then fail "namespace $RNS still exists after restore-down.sh"; fi
pass "$RNS does not exist: the restore starts from an empty namespace"

rc=0
RESTORE_TYPE=time RESTORE_TARGET="$target" RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION=promote timeout 1500 scripts/restore-full.sh >"$work/restore.log" 2>&1 || rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] || { tail -n 12 "$work/restore.log"; fail "restore-full.sh failed (exit $rc)"; }
pass "restore-full.sh finished with exit 0 with a point-in-time target"

ok=no
st=""
for _ in $(seq 1 80); do
  st=$(rest_state || true)
  l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  f=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
  if [ "$l" = 1 ] && [ "$f" = 2 ]; then ok=yes; break; fi
  sleep 3
done
[ "$ok" = yes ] || fail "the restored cluster does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas within 240 s"
pods=$(kr get pods -l application=pg-restore -o json)
[ "$(jq '.items | length' <<<"$pods")" = 3 ] || fail "expected 3 pods in $RNS"
[ "$(jq '[.items[] | select(all(.status.containerStatuses[]?; .ready == true))] | length' <<<"$pods")" = 3 ] || fail "not all 3 restored pods are ready"
pass "3 restored pods are ready; Patroni reports 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"

for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
  got=""
  for _ in $(seq 1 20); do
    got=$(rest_sql "$p" "select count(*) || ':' || count(*) filter (where phase = 'before') || ':' || count(*) filter (where phase = 'after') || ':' || coalesce((select md5(string_agg(id::text, ',' order by id)) from rs_pitr where phase = 'before'), '') from rs_pitr" 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "$got" = "500:500:0:$live_md5" ]; then break; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  [ "$got" = "500:500:0:$live_md5" ] || fail "restored data on $p is not as expected (total:before:after '${got%:*}')"
done
pass "on all three restored pods: 500 rows, 500 before rows with the live checksum, 0 after rows"

for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
  m=$(rest_sql "$p" "show archive_mode" || true)
  [ "$m" = off ] || fail "archive_mode is '${m:-unknown}' on $p, expected off"
done
pass "archive_mode is off on every restored member"

rl=$(jq -r '.[] | select(.Role == "Leader") | .Member' <<<"$st")
rtl=$(rest_sql "$rl" "select timeline_id from pg_control_checkpoint()")
[[ "$rtl" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || fail "could not read the restored timeline"
rtl_hex=$(printf '%08X' "$rtl")
tl_after=$(repo_tl) || fail "cannot list the archive after the restore"
echo "INFO: restored timeline $rtl_hex, newest live archive timeline before $tl_before and after $tl_after"
[[ "$rtl_hex" > "$tl_before" ]] || fail "the restored cluster did not move to a newer timeline, so the archive check proves nothing"
[ "$tl_after" = "$tl_before" ] || fail "the newest timeline in the live archive changed from $tl_before to $tl_after: the restored cluster wrote into the repository"
pass "the restored cluster is on a newer timeline and the live archive got no newer timeline"

r1=$(restarts)
[ "$r1" = "$r0" ] || fail "the pg-ha restart counts changed from $r0 to $r1"
pass "pg-ha was not restarted by the restore"

logs=""
for p in pg-restore-0 pg-restore-1 pg-restore-2; do
  logs+=$(kr logs "$p" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)$'\n'
done
logs+=$(cat "$work/restore.log")
[ -n "$logs" ] || fail "the collected logs are empty, so the scan would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value appears in a restored pod log or in the restore script output"
pass "no secret value in the restored pod logs or the restore script output"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF

# Optional point-in-time target (RS-2): RESTORE_TARGET, RESTORE_TYPE (default time), RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION (default promote).
# pgBackRest reads them from the ConfigMap pgbackrest-env in pg-restore; the pods read it when they start, after this patch.
RESTORE_TYPE="${RESTORE_TYPE:-time}"
RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION="${RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION:-promote}"
if [ -n "${RESTORE_TARGET:-}" ]; then
  kubectl -n "$DST" patch configmap pgbackrest-env --type merge \
    -p "$(jq -n --arg t "$RESTORE_TYPE" --arg g "$RESTORE_TARGET" --arg a "$RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION" '{data:{PGBACKREST_TYPE:$t,PGBACKREST_TARGET:$g,PGBACKREST_TARGET_ACTION:$a}}')" >/dev/null
  echo "restore-full: point-in-time target set: type $RESTORE_TYPE, target $RESTORE_TARGET, action $RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION"
fi
What it does:

With no RESTORE_TARGET, the script behaves exactly as in RS-1: the if is skipped.
With a target, it patches the ConfigMap pgbackrest-env in pg-restore. pgBackRest reads PGBACKREST_TYPE, PGBACKREST_TARGET and PGBACKREST_TARGET_ACTION from the environment, and the pods read the ConfigMap when they start, which is after the patch (prediction).
The target is a timestamp, not a secret, so the script prints it.
The block’s checks:

The file has no uncommitted changes.
The anchor line occurs exactly once, and the script does not mention RESTORE_TARGET yet.
The diff is 0 removed and 9 added.
shellcheck and bash -n are clean.
The test’s own static matcher finds all three variable names.
The file stays executable.
If any check fails, nothing is copied.

cat >"$w/ins.txt" <<'EOF'
# Optional point-in-time target (RS-2): RESTORE_TARGET, RESTORE_TYPE (default time), RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION (default promote).

    -p "$(jq -n --arg t "$RESTORE_TYPE" --arg g "$RESTORE_TARGET" --arg a "$RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION" '{data:{PGBACKREST_TYPE:$t,PGBACKREST_TARGET:$g,PGBACKREST_TARGET_ACTION:$a}}')" >/dev/null
  echo "restore-full: point-in-time target set: type $RESTORE_TYPE, target $RESTORE_TARGET, action $RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION"

EOF
add=$(grep -c '' "$w/ins.txt")
awk -v af="$w/ins.txt" -v anchor="$anchor" '{ print } $0 == anchor { while ((getline l < af) > 0) print l; close(af) }' "$f" >"$w/new.sh"

shellcheck "$w/new.sh" || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck and bash -n clean"
for v in RESTORE_TYPE RESTORE_TARGET RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION; do
  echo "static matcher of tests/rs-pitr.sh, $v: $(grep -c -E "${v}([^A-Z_]|\$)" "$w/new.sh" || true) lines (must be at least 1)"
  [ "$(grep -c -E "${v}([^A-Z_]|\$)" "$w/new.sh" || true)" -ge 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the matcher would still not find $v, nothing changed"; exit 1; }

cp "$w/new.sh" "$f"

d) What I propose next, and why: RS-3. It needs two decisions from you first, and I make neither.

Decision 1: the SPEC line. You said your earlier “yes” was about the new namespace, so the RS-3 line is not confirmed. Here are the lines. I edit nothing before you approve.

text
text only, do not run

- RS-3: Scheduled restore test to a scratch instance, with a data check.

Option A: leave it unchanged. It stays “Scheduled”, so the build needs a CronJob that runs the restore test in pg-restore. That is more work, and the SPEC line says what you asked for when you said “i want rs3 scheduled”.
Option B: change to on-demand. The proposed line (full) would be:
text
text only, do not run

- RS-3: Restore test to a scratch instance, run on demand by one script, with a data check.
  Test: `tests/rs-scheduled.sh`. Evidence: `docs/evidence/RS-3.txt`
Your earlier words were “i want rs3 scheduled in short time”, so I do not recommend B. The handoff says only you decide.
Decision 2: the design for a scheduled restore test, facts and costs. A CronJob in pg-ha cannot reach pg-restore’s pods without new rights. These are the options:

1: CronJob in pg-restore.
It runs the restore test script as a Job in the fixed namespace, with a ServiceAccount and a Role there, so only that namespace is reachable.
It needs to create and delete the restored cluster each time, which means a StatefulSet and PVCs, but a namespaced Role can do that.
The restored cluster stays up between runs unless the Job removes it, and that uses about 2.3 GB of memory limits continuously.
It cannot delete the namespace itself, which is cluster-scoped.
It needs the live passwords and the store key in pg-restore, as ADR-0006 already says.
2: CronJob in pg-restore with a smaller restore. One pod instead of three, to save memory. The SPEC wording for RS-3 says “scratch instance”, so one pod fits it, and RS-1 already shows the cluster form. This is cheaper, but it is a different shape than RS-1.
3: a CronJob that only runs pgbackrest verify plus a restore into an emptyDir. The smallest, with no Patroni. It checks backups, which is BK-4’s wording too (“pgbackrest verify plus the restore test in RS-3”), and it needs no PVCs. It is the cheapest and it fits best with BK-4, but it does not exercise the full restore path.
What I would do after your answers: the red test tests/rs-scheduled.sh, the header, the files shown for your approval, a green run with its own go, and the commit. That is about 8 to 12 rounds (an estimate, not measured). Then BK-4 (which needs RS-3), item 45, the final handoff update and the log capture for this chat.

Not verified (predictions, 4):

The header count is 65.
The pre-commit hook passes the commit.
A CronJob with a namespaced Role can create and delete a StatefulSet and PVCs in pg-restore.
A single-pod restore in an emptyDir fits the 768Mi limit.

What “scratch instance” means for the on-demand script:
1: the full 3-pod cluster again, reusing restore-full.sh. It adds little over RS-1, but it needs no new files.
2: a single restored pod, restoring the latest backup (no point-in-time target) and checking the data against the live table. It is cheaper in memory, and it is a different shape from RS-1.
3: a restore into an emptyDir plus pgbackrest verify, with no Patroni and no PVCs. It is the smallest, and the BK-4 wording (“pgbackrest verify plus the restore test in RS-3”) fits it best. It does not exercise the full restore path.

Run the one bash block below in your terminal, in ~/postgres-ha-lab. I never run anything. It is the red run for RS-3. It writes one test file and reads the cluster. It creates, deletes and restarts nothing, because it stops at its second check.

What your output shows
The SPEC edit worked: 1 line removed and 1 added, and the RS-3 entry now reads “Restore test to a scratch instance, run on demand by one script, with a data check.” SPEC.md is the only uncommitted file, and it goes into the RS-3 commit.

Design I will build (predictions until tested)
A new script scripts/restore-test.sh and a pod manifest k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml. I show both in full, with offline checks, after the red run, and you approve them before they are written.
What the script does:
It uses the fixed namespace pg-restore. It creates the namespace if it is missing and copies objstore-credentials and ca.crt as objstore-ca if they are missing. It refuses any other namespace.
It starts one Pod pg-restore-test. The pod restores the latest backup plus the archived WAL with archive-mode=off into an emptyDir. It then starts plain Postgres without Patroni, with archiving off.
It waits until recovery has ended, then runs the data check. The check is a SQL query that the caller passes in, with the expected result. The script prints counts only.
It deletes the pod at exit, unless RESTORE_TEST_KEEP=1.
Why the pod does not read the pgbackrest-env ConfigMap in pg-restore: RS-2 patched it with a point-in-time target, so the pod would restore to that old time and not to the latest state. The pod carries its own settings.
The running 3-pod cluster in pg-restore stays untouched. There is room for one more pod, up to 768Mi.
What the test checks
Before the run: it writes the table rs_scratch (300 rows) into the live appdb. Its checksum differs from a changed-value control. It switches the WAL and waits until the segment is archived, because the restore reads only archived WAL.
The script run: it runs with RESTORE_TEST_KEEP=1, and the test then checks the pod itself, not the script’s claims.
The pod is ready and recovery has ended.
The checksum equals the live one, and the control matches its live twin.
archive_mode is off.
The scratch timeline is newer than the newest timeline in the live archive, and that timeline did not change.
pg-ha was not restarted, and the 3-pod cluster in pg-restore is unchanged (same pod UIDs).
Negative control: the test deletes the pod and runs the script again with a wrong expected value. The script must exit non-zero, and it must remove the pod.
Logs: no secret value appears in the script output or the pod logs. The scanner is shown firing on planted input first.
Left behind on purpose: the table rs_scratch in the live appdb. The test makes no backup, so retention expires nothing.
Not covered: a scheduled run, production-sized data, a restore when the archive lacks WAL, two restores at once, and structural checks such as pg_amcheck.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/rs-scheduled.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/RS-3-FAILED.txt ] || [ -e docs/evidence/RS-3.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/rs-scheduled.sh or an RS-3 evidence file already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee tests/rs-scheduled.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# RS-3 (on demand, ADR-0006, single-pod option): scripts/restore-test.sh restores the latest backup plus the archived WAL into one
# scratch Postgres pod in the fixed namespace pg-restore (emptyDir data, plain Postgres without Patroni, archiving off), waits for
# the end of recovery, runs a data check given by the caller, and removes the pod unless RESTORE_TEST_KEEP=1. The file keeps its
# SPEC name (rs-scheduled.sh) although the test is on demand.
# Checked here: pg-ha healthy, the script and its pod manifest exist, planted-input controls; the table rs_scratch (300 rows) is
# written into the live appdb and its WAL is archived; the script runs with the kept pod; independently of the script: the pod is
# ready, recovery has ended, the checksum equals the live one (control: a changed-value checksum matches its live twin and
# differs from the plain one), archive_mode is off, the scratch timeline is newer than the newest live archive timeline and that
# timeline is unchanged, pg-ha was not restarted, the 3-pod cluster in pg-restore is unchanged; negative control: with a wrong
# expected value the script must fail and must remove its pod; no secret value in the script output or the pod logs.
# Left behind on purpose: the table rs_scratch in the live appdb. The test makes no backup, so retention expires nothing.
# Not covered: a scheduled run, production-sized data, a restore when the archive lacks WAL, two restores at once, pg_amcheck.
# Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

RNS=pg-restore
POD=pg-restore-test
SUM_SQL="select count(*) || ':' || md5(string_agg(id::text || ':' || v, ',' order by id)) from rs_scratch"
CTL_SQL="select count(*) || ':' || md5(string_agg(id::text || ':' || v || 'x', ',' order by id)) from rs_scratch"
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }
kr() { kubectl -n "$RNS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  kubectl -n "$RNS" delete pod "$POD" --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  rm -rf "$work"
}
trap cleanup EXIT

live_sql() {
  local p
  p=$(current_leader)
  timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- psql -U postgres -d appdb -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atq -c "$1" </dev/null
}
scratch_sql() {
  timeout 60 kubectl -n "$RNS" exec "$POD" -c postgres -- psql -h /run/postgresql -U postgres -d appdb -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atq -c "$1" </dev/null
}
tl_of() { sed -E 's#.*/##' | { grep -o -E '^[0-9A-F]{8}' || true; } | sort | tail -n 1; }
repo_tl() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 120 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest repo-ls archive/pg-ha --recurse </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then tl_of <<<"$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
restarts() { k get pods -l application=pg-ha -o json | jq '[.items[].status.containerStatuses[].restartCount] | add'; }
cluster_uids() { { kubectl -n "$RNS" get pods -l application=pg-restore -o json 2>/dev/null || true; } | jq -r '[.items[]?.metadata.uid] | sort | join(",")'; }
scratch_pods() { kubectl -n "$RNS" get pods -o json 2>/dev/null | jq --arg p "$POD" '[.items[] | select(.metadata.name == $p)] | length'; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas in pg-ha"
pass "pg-ha healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
[ -x scripts/restore-test.sh ] || fail "scripts/restore-test.sh does not exist or is not executable"
[ -f k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml ] || fail "k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml does not exist"
pass "restore-test.sh and its pod manifest are present"

sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
pw=$(k get secret pg-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["superuser-password"] // empty' | base64 -d)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$pw" ] || fail "the superuser password could not be read"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$pw" >"$work/pat"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret key"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $pw inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted password"
[ "$(printf '%s\n' 'x/0000000600000000' | tl_of)" = 00000006 ] || fail "control: the timeline matcher did not fire on planted input"
pass "controls: the leak scanner and the timeline matcher fire on planted input"

r0=$(restarts)
[ -n "$r0" ] || fail "could not read the pg-ha restart counts"
u0=$(cluster_uids)
live_sql "drop table if exists rs_scratch; create table rs_scratch(id int primary key, v text not null); insert into rs_scratch select g, md5(g::text) from generate_series(1,300) g" >/dev/null
live_sum=$(live_sql "$SUM_SQL")
live_ctl=$(live_sql "$CTL_SQL")
[ "${live_sum%%:*}" = 300 ] || fail "the live rs_scratch table does not have 300 rows (got '${live_sum%%:*}')"
[ "$live_ctl" != "$live_sum" ] || fail "control: the changed-value checksum equals the plain checksum, so the data check could not fail"
seg=$(live_sql "select pg_walfile_name(pg_current_wal_lsn())")
live_sql "select pg_switch_wal()" >/dev/null
la=""
for _ in $(seq 1 60); do
  la=$(live_sql "select coalesce(last_archived_wal, '') from pg_stat_archiver" || true)
  if [[ "$la" > "$seg" ]] || [ "$la" = "$seg" ]; then break; fi
  sleep 3
done
{ [[ "$la" > "$seg" ]] || [ "$la" = "$seg" ]; } || fail "WAL segment $seg was not archived within 180 s (last archived '$la')"
pass "live rs_scratch written (300 rows, the changed-value control differs); WAL segment $seg is archived"

tl_before=$(repo_tl) || fail "cannot list the archive of stanza pg-ha"
[ -n "$tl_before" ] || fail "the archive listing holds no timeline, so the archive check would prove nothing"
echo "INFO: newest timeline in the live archive before the restore test: $tl_before"

rc=0
RESTORE_TEST_KEEP=1 RESTORE_TEST_SQL="$SUM_SQL" RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT="$live_sum" timeout 900 scripts/restore-test.sh >"$work/ok.log" 2>&1 || rc=$?
[ "$rc" = 0 ] || { tail -n 12 "$work/ok.log"; fail "restore-test.sh failed (exit $rc)"; }
pass "restore-test.sh finished with exit 0 and kept the scratch pod for inspection"

[ "$(kr get pod "$POD" -o json | jq -r '.status.containerStatuses[0].ready')" = true ] || fail "the scratch pod is not ready"
[ "$(scratch_sql "select pg_is_in_recovery()")" = f ] || fail "the scratch instance is still in recovery"
got=$(scratch_sql "$SUM_SQL")
[ "$got" = "$live_sum" ] || fail "the restored data differs from the live data (row count '${got%%:*}')"
ctl=$(scratch_sql "$CTL_SQL")
[ "$ctl" = "$live_ctl" ] || fail "control: the changed-value checksum on the scratch instance differs from its live twin"
[ "$ctl" != "$got" ] || fail "control: the changed-value checksum equals the plain checksum"
pass "the scratch instance is ready, recovery has ended, and row count 300 and checksum equal the live ones (control matches)"

m=$(scratch_sql "show archive_mode" || true)
[ "$m" = off ] || fail "archive_mode is '${m:-unknown}' on the scratch instance, expected off"
rtl=$(scratch_sql "select timeline_id from pg_control_checkpoint()")
[[ "$rtl" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || fail "could not read the scratch timeline"
rtl_hex=$(printf '%08X' "$rtl")
tl_after=$(repo_tl) || fail "cannot list the archive after the restore test"
echo "INFO: scratch timeline $rtl_hex, newest live archive timeline before $tl_before and after $tl_after"
[[ "$rtl_hex" > "$tl_before" ]] || fail "the scratch instance did not move to a newer timeline, so the archive check proves nothing"
[ "$tl_after" = "$tl_before" ] || fail "the newest timeline in the live archive changed from $tl_before to $tl_after: the scratch instance wrote into the repository"
pass "archive_mode is off, the scratch instance is on a newer timeline, and the live archive got no newer timeline"

r1=$(restarts)
[ "$r1" = "$r0" ] || fail "the pg-ha restart counts changed from $r0 to $r1"
u1=$(cluster_uids)
[ "$u1" = "$u0" ] || fail "the 3-pod cluster in $RNS changed during the restore test"
pass "pg-ha was not restarted and the 3-pod cluster in $RNS is unchanged"

plog=$(kr logs "$POD" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)
kr delete pod "$POD" --wait=true --timeout=120s >/dev/null || fail "could not delete the scratch pod"
[ "$(scratch_pods)" = 0 ] || fail "the scratch pod is still there after the delete"

rcn=0
RESTORE_TEST_SQL="$SUM_SQL" RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT="0:bad" timeout 900 scripts/restore-test.sh >"$work/neg.log" 2>&1 || rcn=$?
[ "$rcn" != 0 ] || fail "negative control: restore-test.sh passed with a wrong expected value"
[ -s "$work/neg.log" ] || fail "negative control: the failed run printed nothing, so the control proves nothing"
[ "$(scratch_pods)" = 0 ] || fail "the failed run left the scratch pod behind"
pass "negative control: with a wrong expected value the script fails and removes its pod"

logs="$plog"$'\n'"$(cat "$work/ok.log")"$'\n'"$(cat "$work/neg.log")"
[ -n "$plog" ] || fail "the scratch pod log is empty, so the scan would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value appears in the script output or the scratch pod log"
pass "no secret value in the script output or the scratch pod log"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/rs-scheduled.sh
[ "$(tail -n 1 tests/rs-scheduled.sh)" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the test file is incomplete"; exit 1; }
echo "lines: $(wc -l < tests/rs-scheduled.sh)"
shellcheck tests/rs-scheduled.sh || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, no run"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean"
tests/rs-scheduled.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/RS-3-FAILED.txt
echo "exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
)

Block 2: the two new files, with offline checks
The full text is inside the block, so read it before you run it. Running it is your approval. It writes 2 new files and changes no committed file.

Design points that matter:

The pod does not read the pgbackrest-env ConfigMap in pg-restore. RS-2 patched it with a point-in-time target, so the pod would restore to that old time. The pod carries its own settings: repository, S3 key from objstore-credentials, and the CA from objstore-ca. It has no PGBACKREST_TYPE or PGBACKREST_TARGET, and the block checks that.
The pod restores the latest backup plus the archived WAL with --archive-mode=off. It then starts plain Postgres from /pgdata/data in an emptyDir, with archive_mode=off, no TCP listener, and no synchronous_standby_names on the command line. It is not Patroni.
It has the label app=restore-test, not application=pg-restore, so the Services and the test’s pod-UID check ignore it. It uses restartPolicy: Never, a 1500 s deadline, restricted security settings and a 768Mi limit.
Readiness: the probe asks pg_is_in_recovery() and passes only when it returns f. It avoids grep, because the image has none.
The script:
It refuses any namespace but pg-restore, before it touches anything.
It creates the namespace and copies the two Secrets only if they are missing. It prints no values.
It starts the pod and waits for ready or failed. It runs a CHECKPOINT after recovery, so the new timeline shows in pg_control for the test.
It runs the caller’s SQL and compares the result with the expected value. It prints only row counts.
It deletes the pod at exit unless RESTORE_TEST_KEEP=1. It never deletes the namespace.
Checks before copying:

shellcheck on the script, and sh -n on the pod’s start command and probe command, which the block extracts.
The pod YAML parses as a single Pod, with 1 container, 14 env entries, no envFrom, and exactly the Secrets objstore-ca and objstore-credentials.
kubeconform, and a server-side admission dry run in pg-restore, which creates nothing.
The script refuses another namespace name with exit 2.
If any check fails, nothing is copied.

RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
pod=k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml
scr=scripts/restore-test.sh
[ ! -e "$pod" ] || { echo "STOP: $pod already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ ! -e "$scr" ] || { echo "STOP: $scr already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(git status --short | wc -l)" = 4 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly 4 uncommitted entries (run block 1 first), nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
w=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$w"' EXIT
mkdir -p "$w/k8s/restore" "$w/scripts"

cat >"$w/k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml" <<'EOFPOD'
# RS-3 (ADR-0006, on-demand single-pod option): one scratch Postgres that restores the latest backup plus the archived WAL
# of the live stanza into an emptyDir, with archiving off. Plain Postgres, no Patroni. It does NOT read the ConfigMap
# pgbackrest-env of this namespace: RS-2 patches a point-in-time target into it. No secret value is stored here.
apiVersion: v1
kind: Pod
metadata:
  name: pg-restore-test
  namespace: pg-restore
  labels:
    app: restore-test
spec:
  restartPolicy: Never
  activeDeadlineSeconds: 1500
  automountServiceAccountToken: false
  securityContext:
    runAsNonRoot: true
    runAsUser: 999
    runAsGroup: 999
    fsGroup: 999
    seccompProfile:
      type: RuntimeDefault
  containers:
    - name: postgres
      image: __IMAGE__
      imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent
      command:
        - sh
        - -c
        - |
          set -eu
          mkdir -p /pgdata/data
          chmod 0700 /pgdata/data
          pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha --archive-mode=off restore
          exec /usr/lib/postgresql/18/bin/postgres -D /pgdata/data -c archive_mode=off -c listen_addresses= -c synchronous_standby_names= -c unix_socket_directories=/run/postgresql
      env:
        - name: PGBACKREST_STANZA
          value: pg-ha
        - name: PGBACKREST_PG1_PATH
          value: /pgdata/data
        - name: PGBACKREST_LOG_LEVEL_CONSOLE
          value: warn
        - name: PGBACKREST_LOG_LEVEL_FILE

        - name: PGBACKREST_REPO1_S3_KEY_SECRET

        periodSeconds: 3

cat >"$w/scripts/restore-test.sh" <<'EOFSCR'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# RS-3 (ADR-0006, on-demand single-pod option): restore the latest backup plus the archived WAL of the live stanza into ONE
# scratch Postgres pod in the fixed namespace pg-restore (emptyDir data, plain Postgres, archiving off), wait for the end of
# recovery, run the caller's data check and compare it with the expected value, and remove the pod unless RESTORE_TEST_KEEP=1.
# Inputs (environment): RESTORE_TEST_SQL (a query that returns one value), RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT (the expected value),
# RESTORE_TEST_KEEP (1 keeps the pod), RESTORE_TEST_TIMEOUT (seconds to wait, default 600).
# Creates the namespace and copies the Secrets objstore-credentials and objstore-ca (ca.crt only) from pg-ha only when they are
# missing; values are never printed. It never deletes a namespace and accepts only pg-restore. Prints row counts, not data.
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
SRC=pg-ha
DST=pg-restore
POD=pg-restore-test
[ "${1:-$DST}" = "$DST" ] || { echo "restore-test: the only namespace allowed is $DST" >&2; exit 2; }
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source versions.env
IMG="${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}"
SQL="${RESTORE_TEST_SQL:?restore-test: RESTORE_TEST_SQL is not set}"
EXPECT="${RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT:?restore-test: RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT is not set}"
KEEP="${RESTORE_TEST_KEEP:-0}"
WAIT="${RESTORE_TEST_TIMEOUT:-600}"

# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  if [ "$KEEP" != 1 ]; then
    kubectl -n "$DST" delete pod "$POD" --ignore-not-found --wait=true --timeout=120s >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
  fi
}
trap cleanup EXIT

for s in objstore-credentials objstore-tls; do
  kubectl -n "$SRC" get secret "$s" >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "restore-test: secret $s is missing in $SRC" >&2; exit 1; }
done
if ! kubectl get ns "$DST" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubectl apply -f k8s/restore/00-namespace.yaml >/dev/null
  echo "restore-test: namespace $DST created"
fi
if ! kubectl -n "$DST" get secret objstore-credentials >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubectl -n "$SRC" get secret objstore-credentials -o json \
    | jq --arg ns "$DST" '{apiVersion:"v1",kind:"Secret",metadata:{name:"objstore-credentials",namespace:$ns},type:.type,data:.data}' \
    | kubectl apply -f - >/dev/null
  echo "restore-test: secret objstore-credentials copied (values not printed)"
fi
if ! kubectl -n "$DST" get secret objstore-ca >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubectl -n "$SRC" get secret objstore-tls -o json \
    | jq --arg ns "$DST" '{apiVersion:"v1",kind:"Secret",metadata:{name:"objstore-ca",namespace:$ns},type:"Opaque",data:{"ca.crt":.data["ca.crt"]}}' \
    | kubectl apply -f - >/dev/null
  echo "restore-test: secret objstore-ca created from ca.crt only"
fi

kubectl -n "$DST" delete pod "$POD" --ignore-not-found --wait=true --timeout=120s >/dev/null
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMG}|g" k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml | kubectl apply -f - >/dev/null
echo "restore-test: pod $POD started, waiting for the restore and the end of recovery"

state=waiting
deadline=$((SECONDS + WAIT))
while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
  st=$(kubectl -n "$DST" get pod "$POD" -o json 2>/dev/null || true)
  phase=$(jq -r '.status.phase // "unknown"' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo unknown)
  ready=$(jq -r '.status.containerStatuses[0].ready // false' <<<"$st" 2>/dev/null || echo false)
  if [ "$ready" = true ]; then state=ready; break; fi
  if [ "$phase" = Failed ]; then state=failed; break; fi
  sleep 3
done
if [ "$state" != ready ]; then
  echo "restore-test: the scratch instance did not become ready ($state)" >&2
  kubectl -n "$DST" logs "$POD" --tail=12 >&2 || true
  exit 1
fi
echo "restore-test: the scratch instance is ready, recovery has ended"
# A checkpoint makes the new timeline visible in pg_control for callers that read it.
kubectl -n "$DST" exec "$POD" -c postgres -- psql -h /run/postgresql -U postgres -d appdb -Atq -c "checkpoint" </dev/null >/dev/null

got=$(kubectl -n "$DST" exec "$POD" -c postgres -- psql -h /run/postgresql -U postgres -d appdb -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -Atq -c "$SQL" </dev/null) \
  || { echo "restore-test: the data query failed" >&2; exit 1; }
if [ "$got" != "$EXPECT" ]; then
  echo "restore-test: data check FAILED: rows ${got%%:*}, expected ${EXPECT%%:*}, checksum match: no" >&2
  exit 1
fi
echo "restore-test: data check passed: rows ${got%%:*}, checksum match: yes"
EOFSCR

shellcheck "$w/scripts/restore-test.sh" || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean for restore-test.sh"
kubectl create --dry-run=client -f "$w/k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml" -o json >"$w/pod.json" || { echo "STOP: the pod manifest is not valid, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
kind=$(jq -r '.kind' "$w/pod.json")
cn=$(jq -r '[.spec.containers[].name] | join(",")' "$w/pod.json")
envn=$(jq '[.spec.containers[0].env[]] | length' "$w/pod.json")

bad=$(jq '[.spec.containers[0].env[] | select(.name == "PGBACKREST_TYPE" or .name == "PGBACKREST_TARGET" or .name == "PGBACKREST_TARGET_ACTION")] | length' "$w/pod.json")
secs=$(jq -r '[.spec | ((.volumes[]? | .secret.secretName // empty), (.containers[0].env[]? | .valueFrom.secretKeyRef.name // empty))] | unique | join(",")' "$w/pod.json")
lbl=$(jq -r '.metadata.labels | to_entries | map("\(.key)=\(.value)") | join(",")' "$w/pod.json")
echo "kind: $kind (expected Pod), containers: $cn (expected postgres), env entries: $envn (expected 14), envFrom: $efrom (expected 0)"
echo "recovery-target variables: $bad (expected 0), secrets referenced: $secs (expected objstore-ca,objstore-credentials), labels: $lbl (expected app=restore-test)"
[ "$kind" = Pod ] && [ "$cn" = postgres ] && [ "$envn" = 14 ] && [ "$efrom" = 0 ] && [ "$bad" = 0 ] && [ "$secs" = "objstore-ca,objstore-credentials" ] && [ "$lbl" = "app=restore-test" ] || { echo "STOP: the pod manifest is not as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
jq -r '.spec.containers[0].command[2]' "$w/pod.json" >"$w/start.sh"
jq -r '.spec.containers[0].readinessProbe.exec.command[2]' "$w/pod.json" >"$w/probe.sh"
[ -s "$w/start.sh" ] && [ -s "$w/probe.sh" ] || { echo "STOP: could not extract the pod commands, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
sh -n "$w/start.sh" || { echo "STOP: the pod start command has a syntax error, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
sh -n "$w/probe.sh" || { echo "STOP: the readiness probe has a syntax error, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "sh -n clean for the pod start command ($(grep -c '' "$w/start.sh") lines) and the probe"
sed "s|__IMAGE__|${IMAGE_NAME}:${IMAGE_TAG}|g" "$w/k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml" >"$w/pod.img.yaml"
if command -v kubeconform >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubeconform -strict -summary "$w/pod.img.yaml" || { echo "STOP: kubeconform rejected the manifest, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
else
  echo "NOTE: kubeconform is not on the PATH, that check was skipped"
fi
echo "--- server-side admission dry run in pg-restore (nothing is created)"
if kubectl get ns pg-restore >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f "$w/pod.img.yaml" 2>&1
  echo "leftover pod (expected NotFound): $(kubectl -n pg-restore get pod pg-restore-test --no-headers 2>&1 | cut -c1-80)"
else
  echo "NOTE: namespace pg-restore does not exist, the admission dry run was skipped"
fi
rc=0
RESTORE_TEST_SQL=x RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT=y bash "$w/scripts/restore-test.sh" some-other-namespace >/dev/null 2>"$w/ns.err" || rc=$?
echo "other namespace refused: exit $rc (expected 2), message printed: $(grep -c 'only namespace allowed' "$w/ns.err" || true) (expected 1)"
[ "$rc" = 2 ] || { echo "STOP: the script did not refuse another namespace, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
cp "$w/k8s/restore/60-restore-test-pod.yaml" "$pod"
cp "$w/scripts/restore-test.sh" "$scr"
chmod +x "$scr"
echo "--- git status (expected 6 lines)"
git status --short
)

Options for the scheduling, facts and costs:

BK-4 design (predictions until tested)
The change:
It is in k8s/pg/60-backup-cronjob.yaml, a committed BK-2 file, inside the script in the ConfigMap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts. I show the exact lines before and after for your approval. I make no edit before the red run.
It adds about 10 lines after the backup command, and removes none. If verify exits non-zero, the script prints backup-cronjob: verify FAILED and exits 1.
An optional variable VERIFY_SET passes --set to verify. The test uses it as a negative control, so production runs leave it unset.
A cost of option 2, which I said before: a failing verify marks the backup Job as failed, even though the backup itself worked.
Applying it changes the ConfigMap only. The CronJob stays the same, and no pod restarts. New Jobs read the new script.
Not rerun: the BK-2 test checks the same script, and its checks should survive, but I did not run it again. A rerun would make more backups and expire a full one, so I leave it to item 30, the final rerun of the suite.
What the test checks
Before any Job:
pg-ha is healthy and the CronJob exists.
docs/evidence/RS-3.txt exists and ends with ALL CHECKS PASSED, because SPEC names the RS-3 restore test as part of BK-4.
Planted-input controls fire for the leak scanner and for the verify-line matcher.
The live ConfigMap script contains a verify command. The red run fails here.
The newest full backup is under 6 days old. Otherwise the Job would make a full backup and expire one, and the test refuses to run.
Job 1, no override:
It completes, logs verify ok once and type=incr once, and adds exactly one incremental backup.
The test then runs pgbackrest verify itself in a pg pod, and it must exit 0. That is independent of the script’s claim.
Negative control A: verify of a set that does not exist (19990101-000000F) must exit non-zero with some output.
Negative control B: Job 2, with that value in VERIFY_SET, must fail and log verify FAILED. It must also have added one incremental backup, which shows that the backup ran and verify made the Job fail.
Logs: no secret value or key line, with the scanner shown firing first.
Left behind on purpose: 2 incremental backups. No full backup is made, so retention expires nothing.
Not covered: a damaged repository (I damage nothing on purpose), verify of a large repository, and a scheduled run.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/bk-verify.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-4-FAILED.txt ] || [ -e docs/evidence/BK-4.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/bk-verify.sh or a BK-4 evidence file already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
tee tests/bk-verify.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# BK-4: backups are verified automatically. The script of the backup CronJob (ConfigMap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts) runs
# "pgbackrest verify" after each backup and a failed verify fails the Job. SPEC: "pgbackrest verify plus the restore test in RS-3".
# Checked here: pg-ha healthy, the CronJob exists, docs/evidence/RS-3.txt exists and ends with ALL CHECKS PASSED, planted-input
# controls, the live ConfigMap script contains a verify command, the newest full backup is younger than 6 days (so the Job makes an
# incremental backup and no full backup expires); Job 1 (no override) completes, logs "verify ok" once and type=incr once and adds
# exactly one incremental backup; a direct "pgbackrest verify" in a pg pod exits 0; negative control A: verify of a backup set that
# does not exist exits non-zero with output; negative control B: Job 2 with VERIFY_SET set to that set fails, logs "verify FAILED"
# and still added one incremental backup (the backup ran, verify made the Job fail); no secret value in the job logs.
# Left behind on purpose: 2 incremental backups (no full backup is made, so retention expires nothing).
# Not covered: a damaged repository (nothing is damaged on purpose), verify of a large repository, a scheduled run.
# Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CJ=pgbackrest-backup
BAD_SET=19990101-000000F
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  rm -rf "$work"
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete job -l app=bk-verify-test --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

info_json() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info --output=json </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then echo "$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
count_type() { jq --arg t "$2" '[.[0].backup[]? | select(.type == $t)] | length' <<<"$1"; }

make_job() {
  local name=$1 kv key val filter
  shift
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  filter='{apiVersion:"batch/v1",kind:"Job",metadata:{name:$name,namespace:"pg-ha",labels:{app:"bk-verify-test"}},spec:.spec.jobTemplate.spec}'
  for kv in "$@"; do
    key=${kv%%=*}
    val=${kv#*=}
    filter="$filter | .spec.template.spec.containers[0].env |= (map(select(.name != \"$key\")) + [{name:\"$key\",value:\"$val\"}])"
  done
  k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json | jq --arg name "$name" "$filter" | k apply -f - >/dev/null
}
wait_job() {
  local name=$1 deadline=$((SECONDS + $2)) s f
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    s=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.succeeded}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    f=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.failed}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "${s:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo complete; return 0; fi
    if [ "${f:-0}" -ge 1 ]; then echo failed; return 0; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo timeout
}
RESULT=""
LOG=""
run_job() {
  local name=$1
  shift
  make_job "$name" "$@"
  RESULT=$(wait_job "$name" 900)
  LOG=$(k logs "job/$name" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)
  printf '%s\n' "$LOG" >>"$work/logs"
  echo "INFO: $name result=$RESULT, log lines=$(grep -c '' <<<"$LOG" || true)"
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
pass "pg-ha healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
k get cronjob "$CJ" >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "CronJob $CJ does not exist (BK-2)"
pass "CronJob $CJ exists"
[ -s docs/evidence/RS-3.txt ] || fail "docs/evidence/RS-3.txt does not exist or is empty (the restore test of RS-3 is part of BK-4)"
[ "$(tail -n 1 docs/evidence/RS-3.txt)" = "ALL CHECKS PASSED" ] || fail "docs/evidence/RS-3.txt does not end with ALL CHECKS PASSED"
pass "the RS-3 restore test evidence exists and ends with ALL CHECKS PASSED"

sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
kl=$(k get secret pgbackrest-tls-client -o json | jq -r '.data["tls.key"] // empty' | base64 -d | sed -n 2p)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$kl" ] || fail "no key line could be read for the leak control"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$kl" >"$work/pat"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $kl inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted key line"
vpat='^[[:space:]]*if pgbackrest .* verify'
# shellcheck disable=SC2016
planted='    if pgbackrest "$@" verify; then'
planted2='    pgbackrest --stanza=x --type=incr backup'
[ "$(grep -c -E "$vpat" <<<"$planted" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the verify matcher did not fire on planted input"
[ "$(grep -c -E "$vpat" <<<"$planted2" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "control: the verify matcher also matches a backup command"
pass "controls: the leak scanner and the verify matcher fire on planted input, and the matcher ignores a backup command"

scr=$(k get configmap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts -o json | jq -r '.data["backup-cronjob.sh"] // empty')
[ -n "$scr" ] || fail "the ConfigMap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts holds no backup-cronjob.sh"
[ "$(grep -c -E "$vpat" <<<"$scr" || true)" -ge 1 ] || fail "the backup CronJob script does not run pgbackrest verify (BK-4 is not implemented yet)"
pass "the backup CronJob script runs pgbackrest verify"

k delete job -l app=bk-verify-test --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null
info0=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info"
f0=$(count_type "$info0" full)
i0=$(count_type "$info0" incr)
stop=$(jq '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full") | .timestamp.stop] | max' <<<"$info0")
{ [ -n "$stop" ] && [ "$stop" != null ]; } || fail "could not read the stop time of the newest full backup"
age=$(($(date +%s) - stop))
[ "$age" -lt 518400 ] || fail "the newest full backup is $age s old (limit 518400 s): the Job would make a full backup and expire one, so the test refuses to run"
echo "INFO: baseline full=$f0 incr=$i0, the newest full backup is $age s old"
pass "the newest full backup is younger than 6 days, so the Jobs make incremental backups and nothing expires"

run_job bk-verify-test-1
[ "$RESULT" = complete ] || { printf '%s\n' "$LOG" | tail -n 8; fail "job 1 ended with $RESULT"; }
info1=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info after job 1"
f1=$(count_type "$info1" full)
i1=$(count_type "$info1" incr)
{ [ "$f1" = "$f0" ] && [ "$i1" = $((i0 + 1)) ]; } || fail "job 1 should add exactly one incremental backup (full $f0 to $f1, incr $i0 to $i1)"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify ok' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "the job 1 log does not say verify ok exactly once"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: type=incr' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "the job 1 log does not say type=incr exactly once"
[ "$(grep -c 'verify FAILED' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 0 ] || fail "the job 1 log says verify FAILED"
pass "job 1 added one incremental backup (incr $i0 to $i1), verified it and logged verify ok"

rcv=0
outv=$(timeout 300 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha verify </dev/null 2>&1) || rcv=$?
[ "$rcv" = 0 ] || { printf '%s\n' "$outv" | tail -n 6; fail "a direct pgbackrest verify exited $rcv"; }
pass "a direct pgbackrest verify in a pg pod exits 0 (independent of the script)"

rca=0
outa=$(timeout 300 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha verify --set="$BAD_SET" </dev/null 2>&1) || rca=$?
[ "$rca" != 0 ] || fail "negative control A: verify of the missing set $BAD_SET exited 0"
[ -n "$outa" ] || fail "negative control A: the failed verify printed nothing, so the control proves nothing"
pass "negative control A: verify of a backup set that does not exist exits $rca with output"

run_job bk-verify-test-2 VERIFY_SET="$BAD_SET"
[ "$RESULT" = failed ] || { printf '%s\n' "$LOG" | tail -n 8; fail "negative control B: job 2 with a missing verify set ended with $RESULT, expected failed"; }
info2=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info after job 2"
f2=$(count_type "$info2" full)
i2=$(count_type "$info2" incr)
{ [ "$f2" = "$f1" ] && [ "$i2" = $((i1 + 1)) ]; } || fail "negative control B: the backup of job 2 should have run (full $f1 to $f2, incr $i1 to $i2)"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify FAILED' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "negative control B: the job 2 log does not say verify FAILED exactly once"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify ok' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 0 ] || fail "negative control B: the job 2 log also says verify ok"
pass "negative control B: the backup ran (incr $i1 to $i2) and the failed verify failed the Job"

logs=$(cat "$work/logs")
[ -n "$logs" ] || fail "the collected job logs are empty, so the scan would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value or key line appears in a job log"
[ "$(grep -F -c 'PRIVATE KEY' <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a private key marker appears in a job log"
pass "no secret value, key line or private key marker in the job logs"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/bk-verify.sh
[ "$(tail -n 1 tests/bk-verify.sh)" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the test file is incomplete"; exit 1; }
echo "lines: $(wc -l < tests/bk-verify.sh)"
shellcheck tests/bk-verify.sh || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, no run"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean"
tests/bk-verify.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/BK-4-FAILED.txt
echo "exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"

What it does:

After a successful backup, the script runs pgbackrest verify. A non-zero exit prints verify FAILED and ends the script with exit 1, so the Job fails. done is not printed then.
VERIFY_SET is optional and unset in production. The test uses it for the negative control.
Verify reads the repository only, so it should need no pg host (prediction). It runs after backup, so the new backup is verified too.
The block’s checks, before it copies anything:

The file has no uncommitted changes, and the anchor line (the backup command) occurs exactly once. The file does not mention verify yet.
The diff is 0 removed and 11 added (the block computes the real number).
The result is a valid manifest with the kinds ConfigMap,CronJob.
The script extracted from the new ConfigMap passes shellcheck -s sh and sh -n, and the test’s verify matcher finds it.
A local simulation with a stand-in pgbackrest in a temp folder (it needs python3 on your machine, and the block says if it skips):
Normal run: exit 0, and the output has type=incr, verify ok and done, each once.
With VERIFY_SET set: exit 1, verify FAILED once, and no done.
With a stand-in whose verify always fails, and no VERIFY_SET: exit 1. This shows that a failed verify fails the script and not only the override.
If any check fails, nothing is copied.

What the output decides, and candidate options (I choose none)

It tests “pgBackRest reports a missing set”, but the script would still rely on the exit code for real problems, which the control would no longer exercise.

## Amendment 1 (BK-4 test, 2026-11-22)
Old check: the first version of tests/bk-verify.sh (its failed run is docs/evidence/BK-4-run2-FAILED.txt) had two negative
controls after the positive path: (A) pgbackrest verify of a backup set that does not exist must exit non-zero, (B) a Job with
that set in VERIFY_SET must fail and log "verify FAILED".
New check: both controls are removed. The test keeps the positive path: the backup script runs verify, Job 1 adds one
incremental backup and logs "verify ok", and a direct verify in a pg pod exits 0.
Why: pgBackRest 2.60.0 verify exits 0 and prints "status: error" and "backup set ... is not valid" for a set that does not
exist ("verify command end: completed successfully"). The controls assumed a non-zero exit and could not pass. Of the
alternatives (judge the output text, use an invalid option, damage a copy of a repository) the human chose to drop the controls.
Not verified: that a failing verify fails the Job. The script checks only the exit code, and verify returned 0 for the one error
case tried. Whether verify exits non-zero for a damaged backup or archive file was not tested. The failure paths of the script
were run only against a stand-in for pgbackrest, and that output is not saved in the repository.
The block’s checks:

It stops unless exactly 6 uncommitted entries exist, and unless ADR-0002 and known-limitations have no uncommitted changes.
It stops unless each marker line of the test occurs exactly once, and unless the last ## heading of known-limitations is the section named above.
It builds all three files in a temp folder. The new test must pass shellcheck and bash -n, hold exactly 9 pass lines, no negative control text and no BAD_SET, and end with ALL CHECKS PASSED.
Only if every check passes does it copy all three files.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
t=tests/bk-verify.sh
adr=docs/decisions/0002-backup-tool.md
kl=docs/known-limitations.md
secthead='## Backups, retention and restore (Phase 3, ADR-0002, ADR-0006)'
oldc='# "pgbackrest verify" after each backup and a failed verify fails the Job. SPEC: "pgbackrest verify plus the restore test in RS-3".'
newc='# "pgbackrest verify" after each backup and a non-zero exit of verify fails the Job. SPEC: "pgbackrest verify plus the restore test in RS-3".'
[ "$(git status --short | wc -l)" = 6 ] || { echo "STOP: expected exactly 6 uncommitted entries, nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
{ git diff --quiet -- "$adr" "$kl" && git diff --cached --quiet -- "$adr" "$kl"; } || { echo "STOP: $adr or $kl has uncommitted changes, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
for m in '^# Checked here:' '^# Not covered:' '^BAD_SET=' '^rca=0$' '^pass "negative control B:'; do
  [ "$(grep -c -E -- "$m" "$t" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the test does not hold exactly one line matching $m, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
done
[ "$(grep -c -F -x -- "$oldc" "$t" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the comment line about a failed verify is not there exactly once, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
n_a=$(grep -n -E '^rca=0$' "$t" | cut -d: -f1)
n_b=$(grep -n -E '^pass "negative control B:' "$t" | cut -d: -f1)
[ "$n_a" -lt "$n_b" ] || { echo "STOP: the control block is not in the expected order, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c -F -x -- "$secthead" "$kl" || true)" = 1 ] || { echo "STOP: the section heading is not in $kl exactly once, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep '^## ' "$kl" | tail -n 1)" = "$secthead" ] || { echo "STOP: that section is not the last one in $kl, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c 'Amendment 1' "$adr" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: $adr already has an Amendment 1, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
for f in "$adr" "$kl"; do [ "$(tail -c 1 "$f" | od -An -c | tr -d ' ')" = '\n' ] || { echo "STOP: $f does not end with a newline, nothing changed"; exit 1; }; done
w=$(mktemp -d)
trap 'rm -rf "$w"' EXIT

# controls, the live ConfigMap script contains a verify command, the newest full backup is younger than 6 days (so the Job makes an

# only (ADR-0002 amendment 1): pgbackrest 2.60.0 verify exits 0 and prints status error for a backup set that does not exist, so a
# failing verify is not tested here.
# Left behind on purpose: 1 incremental backup (no full backup is made, so retention expires nothing).
# Not covered: that a failing verify fails the Job, a damaged repository, verify of a large repository, a scheduled run.
EOFC
awk -v cf="$w/comment.txt" -v old="$oldc" -v new="$newc" '
$0 == old { print new; next }
/^# Checked here:/ { skipc = 1; while ((getline l < cf) > 0) print l; close(cf); next }
skipc == 1 { if ($0 ~ /^# Not covered:/) { skipc = 0 } ; next }
/^BAD_SET=/ { next }
/^rca=0$/ { skipb = 1 }
skipb == 1 { if ($0 ~ /^pass "negative control B:/) { skipb = 0 } ; next }
{ print }' "$t" >"$w/t.new"
[ "$(grep -c -i 'negative control' "$w/t.new" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the new test still mentions a negative control, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c 'BAD_SET' "$w/t.new" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the new test still mentions BAD_SET, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
np=$(grep -c -E '^pass ' "$w/t.new" || true)
echo "pass lines in the new test: $np (expected 9)"
[ "$np" = 9 ] || { echo "STOP: the pass line count is not 9, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(tail -n 1 "$w/t.new")" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the new test does not end as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
bash -n "$w/t.new" || { echo "STOP: bash -n rejects the new test, nothing changed"; exit 1; }

skipb == 1 { if ($0 ~ /^pass "negative control B:/) { skipb = 0 } ; next }
{ print }' "$t" >"$w/t.new"
[ "$(grep -c -i 'negative control' "$w/t.new" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the new test still mentions a negative control, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(grep -c 'BAD_SET' "$w/t.new" || true)" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the new test still mentions BAD_SET, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
np=$(grep -c -E '^pass ' "$w/t.new" || true)
echo "pass lines in the new test: $np (expected 9)"
[ "$np" = 9 ] || { echo "STOP: the pass line count is not 9, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ "$(tail -n 1 "$w/t.new")" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the new test does not end as expected, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
bash -n "$w/t.new" || { echo "STOP: bash -n rejects the new test, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
shellcheck "$w/t.new" || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean for the new test, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck and bash -n clean for the new test ($(grep -c '' "$t") lines before, $(grep -c '' "$w/t.new") after)"

{
cat "$adr"
cat <<'EOFADR'

## Amendment 1 (BK-4 test, 2026-11-22)
Old check: the first version of tests/bk-verify.sh (its failed run is docs/evidence/BK-4-run2-FAILED.txt) had two negative
controls after the positive path: (A) pgbackrest verify of a backup set that does not exist must exit non-zero, (B) a Job with
that set in VERIFY_SET must fail and log "verify FAILED".
New check: both controls are removed. The test keeps the positive path: the backup script runs verify, Job 1 adds one
incremental backup and logs "verify ok", and a direct verify in a pg pod exits 0.
Why: pgBackRest 2.60.0 verify exits 0 and prints "status: error" and "backup set ... is not valid" for a set that does not
exist ("verify command end: completed successfully"). The controls assumed a non-zero exit and could not pass. Of the
alternatives (judge the output text, use an invalid option, damage a copy of a repository) the human chose to drop the controls.
Not verified: that a failing verify fails the Job. The script checks only the exit code, and verify returned 0 for the one error
case tried. Whether verify exits non-zero for a damaged backup or archive file was not tested. The failure paths of the script
were run only against a stand-in for pgbackrest, and that output is not saved in the repository.
EOFADR
} >"$w/adr.new"

- pgbackrest verify (2.60.0) exited 0 and printed "status: error" and "backup set ... is not valid" when asked for a backup set
