What the test checks
It is a static check that the document does not contradict the repository. It does not prove that the steps work, because RS-1 to RS-3 prove that.

Controls first, with planted input: each checker must find a bad case and accept a good one. The checkers cover paths, variables, required mentions, placeholders and section extraction.
The file: docs/runbook.md must exist, with exactly one ## Restore heading and a section of at least 20 lines.
Required topics: 15 must be mentioned: the three restore scripts, the five RESTORE_* variables, the three RS evidence files, known-limitations.md, ADR-0006, archive_mode and retention.
Paths and variables:
Every path named under scripts/, k8s/, tests/ or docs/ must exist, and at least 10 must be named.
Every RESTORE_* variable named must appear in a script, and at least 6 must be named.
No placeholders: no TODO, FIXME or <placeholder>, which follows your rule about exact commands.
No leaks:
Hook pattern: no match of the pre-commit hook’s pattern, as a count only. The test evaluates the hook’s own pat= line, as my earlier history scan did.
Secret values: no live secret value. This part reads two Secrets from the cluster, read-only, and it runs only after the other checks pass.
RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL:

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
if [ -e tests/doc-restore.sh ] || [ -e docs/evidence/DOC-restore-FAILED.txt ] || [ -e docs/evidence/DOC-restore.txt ]; then echo "STOP: tests/doc-restore.sh or a DOC-restore evidence file already exists, nothing changed"; exit 1; fi
[ ! -e docs/runbook.md ] || { echo "STOP: docs/runbook.md already exists, a red run would prove nothing, nothing changed"; exit 1; }
[ -z "$(git status --short)" ] || { echo "STOP: the tree is not clean, nothing changed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
tee tests/doc-restore.sh >/dev/null <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Item 45 (documented restore procedure): docs/runbook.md holds a "## Restore" section built from RS-1 to RS-3 and BK-2 to BK-4.
# This is a static check that the document does not contradict the repository; it does not prove that the steps work (the restore
# tests do). Checked: planted-input controls for every checker; the file and exactly one "## Restore" heading; the section is not
# trivially short; 15 required topics are mentioned; every path named (scripts/, k8s/, tests/, docs/) exists and at least 10 are
# named; every RESTORE_* variable named appears in a script and at least 6 are named; no TODO, FIXME or <placeholder>; no match of
# the pre-commit hook's pattern (count only); no live secret value (read-only cluster reads, the scanner fires on planted input first).
# The section must not use wildcards in paths. Not covered: that the commands work, the wording, the order of the steps.
# Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

DOC=docs/runbook.md
REQ=(scripts/restore-full.sh scripts/restore-down.sh scripts/restore-test.sh RESTORE_TARGET RESTORE_TYPE RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION
  RESTORE_TEST_SQL RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT docs/evidence/RS-1.txt docs/evidence/RS-2.txt docs/evidence/RS-3.txt
  docs/known-limitations.md docs/decisions/0006-restore-design.md archive_mode retention)
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() { rm -rf "$work"; }
trap cleanup EXIT

section() { awk '/^## Restore$/ { on = 1; next } on && /^## / { exit } on { print }' "$1"; }
paths_of() { { grep -o -E '(scripts|k8s|tests|docs)/[A-Za-z0-9_./-]+' || true; } | sed -E 's/[.,]+$//' | sort -u; }
count_paths() { paths_of <<<"$1" | grep -c . || true; }
missing_paths() {
  local p n=0
  while IFS= read -r p; do
    [ -n "$p" ] || continue
    [ -e "$p" ] || n=$((n + 1))
  done < <(paths_of <<<"$1")
  echo "$n"
}
vars_of() { { grep -o -E 'RESTORE_[A-Z_]+' || true; } | sort -u; }
count_vars() { vars_of <<<"$1" | grep -c . || true; }
missing_vars() {
  local v n=0
  while IFS= read -r v; do
    [ -n "$v" ] || continue
    [ "$(grep -r -l -F -- "$v" scripts | wc -l)" -ge 1 ] || n=$((n + 1))
  done < <(vars_of <<<"$1")
  echo "$n"
}
mentions_missing() {
  local m n=0
  for m in "${REQ[@]}"; do
    [ "$(grep -c -E -- "(^|[^A-Za-z0-9_])${m}([^A-Za-z0-9_]|\$)" <<<"$1" || true)" -ge 1 ] || n=$((n + 1))
  done
  echo "$n"
}
placeholders() { grep -c -E 'TODO|FIXME|<[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_-]*>' <<<"$1" || true; }

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
c1="x tests/lib/probe.sh y"
{ [ "$(count_paths "$c1")" = 1 ] && [ "$(missing_paths "$c1")" = 0 ]; } || fail "control: the path checker does not accept an existing path"
[ "$(missing_paths "x scripts/no-such-file.sh y")" = 1 ] || fail "control: the path checker does not catch a missing path"
[ "$(missing_vars "use RESTORE_TARGET here")" = 0 ] || fail "control: the variable checker does not accept a known variable"
[ "$(missing_vars "use RESTORE_NO_SUCH_VAR here")" = 1 ] || fail "control: the variable checker does not catch an unknown variable"
[ "$(mentions_missing "nothing here")" = "${#REQ[@]}" ] || fail "control: the mention checker does not report all ${#REQ[@]} topics missing for an empty text"
[ "$(placeholders "run <name> now")" = 1 ] || fail "control: the placeholder checker does not catch a placeholder"
[ "$(placeholders "run it now")" = 0 ] || fail "control: the placeholder checker flags a clean line"
printf '%s\n' '## Other' 'x' '## Restore' 'body1' 'body2' '## Next' 'y' >"$work/sec.md"
[ "$(section "$work/sec.md" | tr '\n' ' ')" = "body1 body2 " ] || fail "control: the section extractor does not return exactly the Restore section"
pass "controls: every checker accepts a good case and catches a bad one (planted input)"

[ -f "$DOC" ] || fail "docs/runbook.md does not exist"
[ "$(grep -c -x '## Restore' "$DOC" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "docs/runbook.md must hold exactly one '## Restore' heading"
sec=$(section "$DOC")
[ "$(grep -c '' <<<"$sec")" -ge 20 ] || fail "the Restore section has fewer than 20 lines"
pass "docs/runbook.md exists with one Restore section of $(grep -c '' <<<"$sec") lines"

mm=$(mentions_missing "$sec")
[ "$mm" = 0 ] || fail "$mm of ${#REQ[@]} required topics are not mentioned in the Restore section"
pass "all ${#REQ[@]} required topics are mentioned"

np=$(count_paths "$sec")
echo "INFO: paths named: $np"
[ "$np" -ge 10 ] || fail "fewer than 10 paths are named in the Restore section"
mp=$(missing_paths "$sec")
[ "$mp" = 0 ] || fail "$mp path(s) named in the Restore section do not exist"
nv=$(count_vars "$sec")
echo "INFO: RESTORE_* variables named: $nv"
[ "$nv" -ge 6 ] || fail "fewer than 6 RESTORE_* variables are named in the Restore section"
mv=$(missing_vars "$sec")
[ "$mv" = 0 ] || fail "$mv variable(s) named in the Restore section appear in no script"
[ "$(placeholders "$sec")" = 0 ] || fail "the Restore section holds a TODO, FIXME or <placeholder>"
pass "every named path exists, every named RESTORE_* variable is in a script, and there is no placeholder"

line=$(grep -E '^pat=' .githooks/pre-commit || true)
{ [ -n "$line" ] && [ "$(grep -c '' <<<"$line")" = 1 ]; } || fail "expected exactly one pat= line in the hook"
pat=""
eval "$line"
[ -n "$pat" ] || fail "the evaluated hook pattern is empty"
IFS='|' read -r -a alts <<<"$pat"
fired=0
for a in "${alts[@]}"; do
  [ -n "$a" ] || continue
  if [ "$(printf '%s\n' "$a" | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)" -ge 1 ]; then fired=$((fired + 1)); fi
done
[ "$fired" -ge 1 ] || fail "control: the hook pattern does not match its own alternatives, so the scan would prove nothing"
hits=$(grep -c -i -E "$pat" "$DOC" || true)
[ "${hits:-0}" = 0 ] || fail "docs/runbook.md holds $hits line(s) that match the pre-commit hook's pattern"
pass "no line of docs/runbook.md matches the pre-commit hook's pattern (control: the pattern matches $fired of ${#alts[@]} of its own alternatives)"

sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
pw=$(k get secret pg-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["superuser-password"] // empty' | base64 -d)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$pw" ] || fail "the superuser password could not be read"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$pw" >"$work/secrets"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/secrets" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret key"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/secrets" <<<"control line with $pw inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted password"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/secrets" "$DOC" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a live secret value appears in docs/runbook.md"
pass "no live secret value in docs/runbook.md (the scanner fired on planted input first)"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOF
chmod +x tests/doc-restore.sh
[ "$(tail -n 1 tests/doc-restore.sh)" = 'echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"' ] || { echo "STOP: the test file is incomplete"; exit 1; }
echo "lines: $(wc -l < tests/doc-restore.sh)"
shellcheck tests/doc-restore.sh || { echo "STOP: shellcheck is not clean, no run"; exit 1; }
echo "shellcheck clean"
tests/doc-restore.sh 2>&1 | tee docs/evidence/DOC-restore-FAILED.txt
echo "exit=${PIPESTATUS[0]}"
)

cat >"$w/t.new" <<'EOFT'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# BK-4: backups are verified automatically. The script of the backup CronJob (ConfigMap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts) runs
# "pgbackrest verify --output=text --verbose" after each backup and fails the Job unless the exit code is 0 and the report has the
# line "status: ok" (ADR-0002 amendment 2). pgBackRest 2.60.0 verify exits 0 even when it reports "status: error", and prints nothing
# unless --output=text is given. SPEC: "pgbackrest verify plus the restore test in RS-3".
# Checked here: pg-ha healthy, the CronJob exists, docs/evidence/RS-3.txt ends with ALL CHECKS PASSED, planted-input controls (leak
# scanner; the script matcher accepts the new verify command and rejects a backup command and the old verify form; the ok-line
# matcher accepts "status: ok" and rejects "status: error" and a backup line with "status: valid"), the live ConfigMap script runs
# verify --output=text --verbose and tests for "status: ok", the newest full backup is younger than 6 days (so the Jobs make
# incremental backups and no full backup expires); Job 1 completes, adds exactly one incremental backup, logs "verify ok" once and
# its log holds one "status: ok" line; a direct verify in a pg pod exits 0 and prints "status: ok"; a direct verify of a backup set
# that does not exist prints "status: error" and no "status: ok" (its exit code is only reported, it is 0); Job 2 with VERIFY_SET set
# to that set fails, logs "verify FAILED" once, never "verify ok", and still added one incremental backup; no secret value in the logs.
# Left behind on purpose: 2 incremental backups (no full backup is made, so retention expires nothing).
# Not covered: a damaged backup or archive file (only the missing-set error is produced), verify of a large repository, a scheduled
# run, a change of wording in another pgBackRest version (it would fail the Job, the safe direction).
# Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail
cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."
# shellcheck source=/dev/null
source tests/lib/probe.sh

CJ=pgbackrest-backup
BAD_SET=19990101-000000F
VCMD='verify --output=text --verbose'
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  rm -rf "$work"
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete job -l app=bk-verify-test --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

info_json() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info --output=json </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then echo "$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
count_type() { jq --arg t "$2" '[.[0].backup[]? | select(.type == $t)] | length' <<<"$1"; }
has_ok() { grep -c -x 'status: ok' <<<"$1" || true; }
has_err() { grep -c -x 'status: error' <<<"$1" || true; }

make_job() {
  local name=$1 kv key val filter
  shift
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  filter='{apiVersion:"batch/v1",kind:"Job",metadata:{name:$name,namespace:"pg-ha",labels:{app:"bk-verify-test"}},spec:.spec.jobTemplate.spec}'
  for kv in "$@"; do
    key=${kv%%=*}
    val=${kv#*=}
    filter="$filter | .spec.template.spec.containers[0].env |= (map(select(.name != \"$key\")) + [{name:\"$key\",value:\"$val\"}])"
  done
  k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json | jq --arg name "$name" "$filter" | k apply -f - >/dev/null
}
wait_job() {
  local name=$1 deadline=$((SECONDS + $2)) s f
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    s=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.succeeded}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    f=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.failed}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "${s:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo complete; return 0; fi
    if [ "${f:-0}" -ge 1 ]; then echo failed; return 0; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo timeout
}
RESULT=""
LOG=""
run_job() {
  local name=$1
  shift
  make_job "$name" "$@"
  RESULT=$(wait_job "$name" 900)
  LOG=$(k logs "job/$name" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)
  printf '%s\n' "$LOG" >>"$work/logs"
  echo "INFO: $name result=$RESULT, log lines=$(grep -c '' <<<"$LOG" || true)"
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
k get sts pg-ha >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "StatefulSet pg-ha is missing"
wait_healthy 120 || fail "Patroni does not report 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
pass "pg-ha healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"
k get cronjob "$CJ" >/dev/null 2>&1 || fail "CronJob $CJ does not exist (BK-2)"
pass "CronJob $CJ exists"
[ -s docs/evidence/RS-3.txt ] || fail "docs/evidence/RS-3.txt does not exist or is empty (the restore test of RS-3 is part of BK-4)"
[ "$(tail -n 1 docs/evidence/RS-3.txt)" = "ALL CHECKS PASSED" ] || fail "docs/evidence/RS-3.txt does not end with ALL CHECKS PASSED"
pass "the RS-3 restore test evidence exists and ends with ALL CHECKS PASSED"

sk=$(k get secret objstore-credentials -o json | jq -r '.data["secret-access-key"] // empty' | base64 -d)
kl=$(k get secret pgbackrest-tls-client -o json | jq -r '.data["tls.key"] // empty' | base64 -d | sed -n 2p)
[ -n "$sk" ] || fail "the secret access key could not be read"
[ -n "$kl" ] || fail "no key line could be read for the leak control"
printf '%s\n%s\n' "$sk" "$kl" >"$work/pat"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $sk inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted secret"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"control line with $kl inside" || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the scanner did not fire on a planted key line"
[ "$(grep -c -F "$VCMD" <<<'    vout=$(pgbackrest --stanza=x verify --output=text --verbose) || vrc=$?' || true)" -eq 1 ] || fail "control: the script matcher did not fire on the new verify command"
[ "$(grep -c -F "$VCMD" <<<'    pgbackrest --stanza=x --type=incr backup' || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "control: the script matcher also matches a backup command"
[ "$(grep -c -F "$VCMD" <<<'    if pgbackrest --stanza=x verify; then' || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "control: the script matcher also matches the old verify form"
[ "$(has_ok $'stanza: pg-ha\nstatus: ok\n  backup: 1F, status: valid')" = 1 ] || fail "control: the ok-line matcher did not fire on a good report"
[ "$(has_ok $'stanza: pg-ha\nstatus: error\n  backup set x is not valid')" = 0 ] || fail "control: the ok-line matcher accepts an error report"
[ "$(has_ok $'  backup: 1F, status: valid')" = 0 ] || fail "control: the ok-line matcher accepts a backup line"
[ "$(has_err $'stanza: pg-ha\nstatus: error\n  backup set x is not valid')" = 1 ] || fail "control: the error-line matcher did not fire on an error report"
pass "controls: the leak scanner fires, the script matcher rejects a backup command and the old verify form, and the ok-line and error-line matchers tell good from bad reports"

scr=$(k get configmap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts -o json | jq -r '.data["backup-cronjob.sh"] // empty')
[ -n "$scr" ] || fail "the ConfigMap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts holds no backup-cronjob.sh"
[ "$(grep -c -F "$VCMD" <<<"$scr" || true)" -ge 1 ] || fail "the backup CronJob script does not run pgbackrest $VCMD (ADR-0002 amendment 2 is not implemented yet)"
[ "$(grep -c -F 'status: ok' <<<"$scr" || true)" -ge 1 ] || fail "the backup CronJob script does not test the report for the line status: ok"
pass "the backup CronJob script runs pgbackrest $VCMD and tests the report for status: ok"

k delete job -l app=bk-verify-test --ignore-not-found --wait=true >/dev/null
info0=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info"
f0=$(count_type "$info0" full)
i0=$(count_type "$info0" incr)
stop=$(jq '[.[0].backup[] | select(.type == "full") | .timestamp.stop] | max' <<<"$info0")
{ [ -n "$stop" ] && [ "$stop" != null ]; } || fail "could not read the stop time of the newest full backup"
age=$(($(date +%s) - stop))
[ "$age" -lt 518400 ] || fail "the newest full backup is $age s old (limit 518400 s): a Job would make a full backup and expire one, so the test refuses to run"
echo "INFO: baseline full=$f0 incr=$i0, the newest full backup is $age s old"
pass "the newest full backup is younger than 6 days, so the Jobs make incremental backups and nothing expires"

run_job bk-verify-test-1
[ "$RESULT" = complete ] || { printf '%s\n' "$LOG" | tail -n 8; fail "job 1 ended with $RESULT"; }
info1=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info after job 1"
f1=$(count_type "$info1" full)
i1=$(count_type "$info1" incr)
{ [ "$f1" = "$f0" ] && [ "$i1" = $((i0 + 1)) ]; } || fail "job 1 should add exactly one incremental backup (full $f0 to $f1, incr $i0 to $i1)"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify ok' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "the job 1 log does not say verify ok exactly once"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: type=incr' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "the job 1 log does not say type=incr exactly once"
[ "$(grep -c 'verify FAILED' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 0 ] || fail "the job 1 log says verify FAILED"
[ "$(has_ok "$LOG")" = 1 ] || fail "the job 1 log does not hold exactly one status: ok line (the verify report)"
pass "job 1 added one incremental backup (incr $i0 to $i1), logged the verify report with status: ok and verify ok"

rcv=0
outv=$(timeout 300 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha $VCMD </dev/null 2>&1) || rcv=$?
[ "$rcv" = 0 ] || { printf '%s\n' "$outv" | tail -n 6; fail "a direct pgbackrest verify exited $rcv"; }
[ "$(has_ok "$outv")" = 1 ] || fail "a direct pgbackrest verify did not print exactly one status: ok line"
pass "a direct pgbackrest verify in a pg pod exits 0 and prints status: ok (independent of the script)"

rcm=0
outm=$(timeout 300 kubectl -n "$NS" exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha $VCMD --set="$BAD_SET" </dev/null 2>&1) || rcm=$?
echo "INFO: verify of the missing set $BAD_SET exited $rcm (reported, not asserted)"
[ -n "$outm" ] || fail "the verify of the missing set printed nothing, so the check would prove nothing"
[ "$(has_err "$outm")" = 1 ] || fail "the verify of the missing set did not print exactly one status: error line"
[ "$(has_ok "$outm")" = 0 ] || fail "the verify of the missing set printed status: ok"
pass "a direct verify of a backup set that does not exist prints status: error and no status: ok"

run_job bk-verify-test-2 VERIFY_SET="$BAD_SET"
[ "$RESULT" = failed ] || { printf '%s\n' "$LOG" | tail -n 8; fail "job 2 with a missing verify set ended with $RESULT, expected failed"; }
info2=$(info_json) || fail "cannot read pgbackrest info after job 2"
f2=$(count_type "$info2" full)
i2=$(count_type "$info2" incr)
{ [ "$f2" = "$f1" ] && [ "$i2" = $((i1 + 1)) ]; } || fail "the backup of job 2 should have run (full $f1 to $f2, incr $i1 to $i2)"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify FAILED' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "the job 2 log does not say verify FAILED exactly once"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify ok' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 0 ] || fail "the job 2 log also says verify ok"
[ "$(has_err "$LOG")" = 1 ] || fail "the job 2 log does not hold exactly one status: error line"
pass "job 2 with a missing verify set failed, the backup still ran (incr $i1 to $i2), and the log says verify FAILED"

logs=$(cat "$work/logs")
[ -n "$logs" ] || fail "the collected job logs are empty, so the scan would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value or key line appears in a job log"
[ "$(grep -F -c 'PRIVATE KEY' <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a private key marker appears in a job log"
pass "no secret value, key line or private key marker in the job logs"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOFT
np=$(grep -c -E '^pass ' "$w/t.new" || true)

## Amendment 2 (BK-4 script and test, 2026-11-22)
Old check: after Amendment 1 the backup script ran "pgbackrest verify" with the default output (none) and failed the Job only on a
non-zero exit; tests/bk-verify.sh showed the positive path only (docs/evidence/BK-4.txt).
New check: the script runs "pgbackrest verify --output=text --verbose", prints the report, and fails the Job unless the exit code
is 0 and the report has the line "status: ok" (option F2, chosen by the human). tests/bk-verify.sh also checks the live script
text, that a direct verify of a missing backup set prints "status: error", and that a Job with that set (VERIFY_SET) fails, logs
"verify FAILED" and still adds one incremental backup.
Why: pgBackRest 2.60.0 verify exits 0 when it reports "status: error" (observed for a missing backup set) and prints nothing unless
--output=text is given, so the old script could not fail on a reported error.
Not verified: that verify reports "status: error" for a damaged backup or archive file (only the missing-set error is produced
here); that another pgBackRest version keeps the wording "status: ok" (a different wording would fail the Job, the safe direction).
EOFADR

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# BK-4: backups are verified automatically. The script of the backup CronJob (ConfigMap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts) runs
# "pgbackrest verify --output=text --verbose" after each backup and fails the Job unless the exit code is 0 and the report has the
# line "status: ok" (ADR-0002 amendment 2). pgBackRest 2.60.0 verify exits 0 even when it reports "status: error", and prints nothing
# unless --output=text is given. SPEC: "pgbackrest verify plus the restore test in RS-3".
# Checked here: pg-ha healthy, the CronJob exists, docs/evidence/RS-3.txt ends with ALL CHECKS PASSED, planted-input controls (leak
# scanner; the script matcher accepts the new verify command and rejects a backup command and the old verify form; the ok-line
# matcher accepts "status: ok" and rejects "status: error" and a backup line with "status: valid"), the live ConfigMap script runs
# verify --output=text --verbose and tests for "status: ok", the newest full backup is younger than 6 days (so the Jobs make
# incremental backups and no full backup expires); Job 1 completes, adds exactly one incremental backup, logs "verify ok" once and
# its log holds one "status: ok" line; a direct verify in a pg pod exits 0 and prints "status: ok"; a direct verify of a backup set
# that does not exist prints "status: error" and no "status: ok" (its exit code is only reported, it is 0); Job 2 with VERIFY_SET set
# to that set fails, logs "verify FAILED" once, never "verify ok", and still added one incremental backup; no secret value in the logs.
# Left behind on purpose: 2 incremental backups (no full backup is made, so retention expires nothing).
# Not covered: a damaged backup or archive file (only the missing-set error is produced), verify of a large repository, a scheduled
# run, a change of wording in another pgBackRest version (it would fail the Job, the safe direction).
# Leak scans use grep -c, never "producer | grep -q".
set -euo pipefail

CJ=pgbackrest-backup

VCMD="${VARGS[*]}"
k() { kubectl -n "$NS" "$@"; }

umask 077
work=$(mktemp -d)
# shellcheck disable=SC2329
cleanup() {
  rm -rf "$work"
  kubectl -n "$NS" delete job -l app=bk-verify-test --ignore-not-found --wait=false >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
}
trap cleanup EXIT

info_json() {
  local p out
  for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do
    if out=$(timeout 60 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info --output=json </dev/null 2>/dev/null); then
      if [ -n "$out" ]; then echo "$out"; return 0; fi
    fi
  done
  return 1
}
count_type() { jq --arg t "$2" '[.[0].backup[]? | select(.type == $t)] | length' <<<"$1"; }
has_ok() { grep -c -x 'status: ok' <<<"$1" || true; }
has_err() { grep -c -x 'status: error' <<<"$1" || true; }

make_job() {
  local name=$1 kv key val filter
  shift
  # shellcheck disable=SC2016
  filter='{apiVersion:"batch/v1",kind:"Job",metadata:{name:$name,namespace:"pg-ha",labels:{app:"bk-verify-test"}},spec:.spec.jobTemplate.spec}'
  for kv in "$@"; do
    key=${kv%%=*}
    val=${kv#*=}
    filter="$filter | .spec.template.spec.containers[0].env |= (map(select(.name != \"$key\")) + [{name:\"$key\",value:\"$val\"}])"
  done
  k get cronjob "$CJ" -o json | jq --arg name "$name" "$filter" | k apply -f - >/dev/null
}
wait_job() {
  local name=$1 deadline=$((SECONDS + $2)) s f
  while [ "$SECONDS" -lt "$deadline" ]; do
    s=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.succeeded}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    f=$(k get job "$name" -o jsonpath='{.status.failed}' 2>/dev/null || true)
    if [ "${s:-0}" = 1 ]; then echo complete; return 0; fi
    if [ "${f:-0}" -ge 1 ]; then echo failed; return 0; fi
    sleep 3
  done
  echo timeout
}
RESULT=""
LOG=""
run_job() {
  local name=$1
  shift
  make_job "$name" "$@"
  RESULT=$(wait_job "$name" 900)
  LOG=$(k logs "job/$name" --all-containers 2>&1 || true)
  printf '%s\n' "$LOG" >>"$work/logs"
  echo "INFO: $name result=$RESULT, log lines=$(grep -c '' <<<"$LOG" || true)"
}

echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"

pass "pg-ha healthy: 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas"

pass "the RS-3 restore test evidence exists and ends with ALL CHECKS PASSED"

planted_bk="    pgbackrest --stanza=x --type=incr backup"

pass "controls: the leak scanner fires, the script matcher rejects a backup command and the old verify form, and the ok-line and error-line matchers tell good from bad reports"

[ "$(has_ok "$LOG")" = 1 ] || fail "the job 1 log does not hold exactly one status: ok line (the verify report)"

rcv=0

f2=$(count_type "$info2" full)
i2=$(count_type "$info2" incr)
{ [ "$f2" = "$f1" ] && [ "$i2" = $((i1 + 1)) ]; } || fail "the backup of job 2 should have run (full $f1 to $f2, incr $i1 to $i2)"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify FAILED' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 1 ] || fail "the job 2 log does not say verify FAILED exactly once"
[ "$(grep -c 'backup-cronjob: verify ok' <<<"$LOG" || true)" = 0 ] || fail "the job 2 log also says verify ok"
[ "$(has_err "$LOG")" = 1 ] || fail "the job 2 log does not hold exactly one status: error line"
pass "job 2 with a missing verify set failed, the backup still ran (incr $i1 to $i2), and the log says verify FAILED"

logs=$(cat "$work/logs")
[ -n "$logs" ] || fail "the collected job logs are empty, so the scan would prove nothing"
[ "$(grep -F -c -f "$work/pat" <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a secret value or key line appears in a job log"
[ "$(grep -F -c 'PRIVATE KEY' <<<"$logs" || true)" -eq 0 ] || fail "a private key marker appears in a job log"
pass "no secret value, key line or private key marker in the job logs"
echo "ALL CHECKS PASSED"
EOFT
tee "$stage/adr.add" >/dev/null <<'EOFADR'

## Amendment 2 (BK-4 script and test, 2026-11-22)
Old check: after Amendment 1 the backup script ran "pgbackrest verify" with the default output (none) and failed the Job only on a
non-zero exit; tests/bk-verify.sh showed the positive path only (docs/evidence/BK-4.txt).
New check: the script runs "pgbackrest verify --output=text --verbose", prints the report, and fails the Job unless the exit code
is 0 and the report has the line "status: ok" (option F2, chosen by the human). tests/bk-verify.sh also checks the live script
text, that a direct verify of a missing backup set prints "status: error", and that a Job with that set (VERIFY_SET) fails, logs
"verify FAILED" and still adds one incremental backup.
Why: pgBackRest 2.60.0 verify exits 0 when it reports "status: error" (observed for a missing backup set) and prints nothing unless
--output=text is given, so the old script could not fail on a reported error.
Not verified: that verify reports "status: error" for a damaged backup or archive file (only the missing-set error is produced
here); that another pgBackRest version keeps the wording "status: ok" (a different wording would fail the Job, the safe direction).
EOFADR

Block 2: the script change in k8s/pg/60-backup-cronjob.yaml
This file is from BK-2. The change is inside the script of the ConfigMap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts. Running the block is your approval. If you want a change, do not run it and tell me.

text
text only, do not run

Before (11 lines, after the backup command):
if [ -n "${VERIFY_SET:-}" ]; then
  set -- --set="$VERIFY_SET"
else
  set --
fi
if pgbackrest --stanza="$STANZA" verify "$@"; then
  echo "backup-cronjob: verify ok"
else
  echo "backup-cronjob: verify FAILED" >&2
  exit 1

After (22 lines):
if [ -n "${VERIFY_SET:-}" ]; then
  set -- verify --output=text --verbose --set="$VERIFY_SET"
else
  set -- verify --output=text --verbose
fi
vrc=0
vout=$(pgbackrest --stanza="$STANZA" "$@") || vrc=$?
echo "$vout"
vok=no
while IFS= read -r vline; do
  if [ "$vline" = "status: ok" ]; then
    vok=yes
  fi
done <<EOFV
$vout
EOFV
if [ "$vrc" -eq 0 ] && [ "$vok" = yes ]; then
  echo "backup-cronjob: verify ok"
else
  echo "backup-cronjob: verify FAILED (exit $vrc, status ok line: $vok)" >&2
  exit 1
fi
What it does:

It runs pgbackrest verify --output=text --verbose, prints the report into the Job log, and loops over the report with plain shell, with no grep, because the image has none. It passes only if the exit code is 0 and an exact line status: ok exists.
VERIFY_SET stays as the optional input for the test.
It also fixes a gap in the old script: with set -e, the old if form was fine, but now the verify exit code is captured explicitly so the report is printed before the Job decides.
The Job log then holds the report lines and verify ok.
The block’s checks, before it copies anything:

The file has no uncommitted changes, the verify block occurs exactly once, and the if starts exactly once.
It replaces the block between the backup command and echo "backup-cronjob: done".
The result is a valid manifest with the kinds ConfigMap,CronJob.
The extracted script passes shellcheck -s sh and sh -n.
The v3 test’s matchers find the new command, status: ok and the old verify "$@" form is gone.
A local simulation with a stand-in pgbackrest:
A healthy report with exit 0 gives verify ok, the report in the log, and exit 0.
An error report with exit 0 gives verify FAILED, exit 1, and no done.
A healthy report with a non-zero exit gives verify FAILED and exit 1.
An empty report with exit 0 (which is what the non-verbose default printed) gives verify FAILED, so silence cannot pass.
VERIFY_SET set reaches the stand-in as --set=....
If any check fails, nothing is copied.

NS=pg-ha
anypl() { local p; for p in pg-ha-0 pg-ha-1 pg-ha-2; do timeout 20 kubectl -n "$NS" exec "$p" -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list "$@" </dev/null 2>/dev/null && return 0; done; return 1; }
live_script() { kubectl -n "$NS" get configmap pgbackrest-cronjob-scripts -o json | jq -r '.data["backup-cronjob.sh"]'; }
ncmd() { live_script | grep -c -F 'verify --output=text --verbose' || true; }
nold() { live_script | grep -c -F 'verify "$@"' || true; }
echo "date: $(date -u +%FT%TZ)"
state=$(anypl -f json || true)
l=$(jq '[.[] | select(.Role == "Leader")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
s=$(jq '[.[] | select((.Role == "Replica" or .Role == "Sync Standby") and .State == "streaming")] | length' <<<"$state" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)

bo=$(nold)

# Runbook

This file holds the operating procedures written so far. Only the Restore section exists. The runbooks for a planned switchover and
a minor-version upgrade (OPS-3) are not written.

## Restore

This section covers restoring the live PostgreSQL cluster (namespace pg-ha, pgBackRest stanza pg-ha) into the fixed scratch
namespace pg-restore. Moving applications over to a restored cluster was not built or tested. What was tested is shown by the
evidence files named below, and what was not tested is listed in docs/known-limitations.md.
The design and its reasons are in docs/decisions/0006-restore-design.md.

### What a restore reads

- The repository is the in-cluster object store behind TLS. A restore takes the latest backup plus the archived WAL.
- WAL that is not archived yet is not in a restore. To include the newest writes, run `select pg_switch_wal()` on the leader and
  wait until pg_stat_archiver shows that segment as archived. archive_timeout is 0, so a quiet cluster does not archive on its own.
- Retention is retention-full=2 (type count). Only the 2 newest full backups and their incrementals are kept, and WAL older than
  the start of the oldest kept full backup is expired. A target time before that backup cannot be restored.
- To see the backups, run: `kubectl -n pg-ha exec pg-ha-2 -c postgres -- pgbackrest --stanza=pg-ha info`

### Full restore (RS-1)

1. Run `scripts/restore-full.sh` from the repository root. It stops if the namespace pg-restore already exists. Remove it first
   with scripts/restore-down.sh.
2. It creates pg-restore, copies the Secrets pg-credentials and objstore-credentials from pg-ha without printing them, creates the
   Secret objstore-ca from ca.crt only, applies the manifests in k8s/restore/ and waits up to 900 seconds.
3. A 3-pod Patroni cluster (pg-restore-0 to pg-restore-2) bootstraps with pgbackrest restore from the live repository, with
   archive_mode off. It ends with 1 leader and 2 streaming replicas.
4. Check the result with: `kubectl -n pg-restore exec pg-restore-0 -c postgres -- patronictl -c /etc/patroni/patroni.yml list`
5. The test is tests/rs-full.sh and its evidence is docs/evidence/RS-1.txt.

### Point-in-time recovery (RS-2)

Set RESTORE_TARGET to a UTC timestamp in the format the RS-2 test used:
`RESTORE_TARGET='2026-11-22 05:21:26.984816+00' scripts/restore-full.sh`
RESTORE_TYPE defaults to time and RESTORE_TARGET_ACTION defaults to promote. The script writes them into the ConfigMap
pgbackrest-env in pg-restore before the pods start. Keep the action at promote. The pgBackRest default is pause, which was not tried.
The test is tests/rs-pitr.sh and its evidence is docs/evidence/RS-2.txt. For a time target pgBackRest chooses the backup, and which
one it chose was not checked.

### Scratch restore test (RS-3)

`scripts/restore-test.sh` restores the latest backup plus the archived WAL into one plain Postgres pod, pg-restore-test, in
pg-restore (emptyDir data, no Patroni, archive_mode off). It runs a query and compares the result with a value you give it.

- RESTORE_TEST_SQL is a query that returns one value. RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT is the expected value.
- RESTORE_TEST_KEEP=1 keeps the pod after the run. RESTORE_TEST_TIMEOUT is the wait in seconds (default 600).
- The script exits non-zero if the result differs from the expected value. It removes the pod unless RESTORE_TEST_KEEP is 1.
- It prints row counts, not data. It never deletes a namespace and accepts only pg-restore.
- The pod does not read the ConfigMap pgbackrest-env, so a point-in-time target left there by RS-2 does not change this test.

An example with the table rs_scratch, which the test leaves in the live database (not run in exactly this form):
`RESTORE_TEST_SQL='select count(*) from rs_scratch' RESTORE_TEST_EXPECT=300 scripts/restore-test.sh`
The test is tests/rs-restore-test.sh and its evidence is docs/evidence/RS-3.txt, made before the test file was renamed
(ADR-0006 amendment 1).

### Removing the scratch namespace

`scripts/restore-down.sh` lists what pg-restore holds and then deletes the namespace: the pods, the PVCs, the copied Secrets, the
ConfigMaps, the Services and the Role. It refuses any other namespace and deletes nothing in pg-ha. Until it runs, pg-restore
holds copies of the live database passwords and the read-write object store key.

### What protects the live archive

- The restored cluster runs with archive_mode off (the Patroni config in k8s/restore/20-config.yaml and the restore option
  archive-mode=off), so it does not push WAL into the live repository.
- The restore tests check this. The restored instance is on a newer timeline than the newest one in the live archive, and the
  newest timeline in the live archive is unchanged afterwards (docs/evidence/RS-1.txt, docs/evidence/RS-2.txt and
  docs/evidence/RS-3.txt).
- The restored cluster has the same Patroni system identifier as the live one. Only archive_mode off keeps its WAL out of the
  live archive, so do not turn archiving on in pg-restore.

### Not tested

- Restoring an older backup. The scripts take no backup set.
- A target before the oldest kept full backup (not possible after retention), a target inside a failover, exclusive targets, and
  recovery types other than time.
- A restore when the archive lacks WAL, two restores at the same time, and production-sized data. The database was about 30 MB and
  the restore pods have a memory limit of 768Mi.
- Moving applications to a restored cluster, and the NetworkPolicies this would need (none exist).
- The full list is in docs/known-limitations.md.
EOFDOC
echo "docs/runbook.md: $(grep -c '' docs/runbook.md) lines, Restore section: $(awk '/^## Restore$/ { on = 1; next } on && /^## / { exit } on { print }' docs/runbook.md | grep -c '')"
echo "--- running tests/doc-restore.sh (read-only)"
tmp=$(mktemp)
rc=0
tests/doc-restore.sh >"$tmp" 2>&1 || rc=$?
cat "$tmp"
echo "exit=$rc"
if [ "$rc" = 0 ]; then dest=docs/evidence/DOC-restore.txt; else dest=docs/evidence/DOC-restore-run2-FAILED.txt; fi
cp "$tmp" "$dest" && rm -f "$tmp"
echo "saved: $dest"
echo "--- git status (expected 4 lines)"
git status --short
)

echo "raw lines that match the hook's pattern: $(grep -c -i -E "$pat" "$raw" || true) (replaced in the redacted copy)"

cut -f1,4,5 "$red/MANIFEST.tsv"
echo "--- git status (expected 3 lines)"
git status --short
)

Block D: push (optional, your decision, I recommend nothing)
Your handoff rule says you push when a phase is done. Phase 3 is done by your boundary, so this is the moment, and it is still your call. The repo is public, and copies that others fetch after a push cannot be recalled by a later rewrite.

What it does: it stops unless you are on main with a clean tree and a fast-forward. It scans the whole history with gitleaks, the hook’s pattern (content, messages, authors, paths, refs) and the current secret values, and pushes only if all are clean. It never uses force.

RUN IN YOUR TERMINAL (block D):

bash
(
cd ~/postgres-ha-lab || exit 1
[ "$(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD)" = main ] || { echo "STOP: not on main, nothing pushed"; exit 1; }
[ -z "$(git status --short)" ] || { echo "STOP: the tree is not clean, nothing pushed"; git status --short; exit 1; }
git fetch origin main || { echo "STOP: fetch failed, nothing pushed"; exit 1; }
git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD || { echo "STOP: origin/main is not an ancestor of HEAD, nothing pushed"; exit 1; }
echo "commits to push: $(git rev-list --count origin/main..HEAD)"
git log --oneline origin/main..HEAD
gitleaks git --no-banner --redact --log-opts="--all" . >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "STOP: gitleaks reports findings or failed, nothing pushed"; exit 1; }
echo "gitleaks over the history: no leaks"
line=$(grep -E '^pat=' .githooks/pre-commit || true)
pat=""
eval "$line"
[ -n "$pat" ] || { echo "STOP: the hook pattern could not be read, nothing pushed"; exit 1; }
revs=$(git rev-list --all)
echo "commits scanned: $(grep -c '' <<<"$revs")"
c1=$(git grep -I -l -i -E "$pat" $revs | wc -l || true)
c2=$(git log --all --format=%B | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)
c3=$(git log --all --format='%an %ae %cn %ce' | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)
c4=$(git log --all --name-only --format= | sort -u | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)
c5=$(git for-each-ref --format='%(refname)' | grep -c -i -E "$pat" || true)
echo "pattern matches: content $c1, messages $c2, authors $c3, paths $c4, refs $c5 (expected 0 each)"
[ "$((c1 + c2 + c3 + c4 + c5))" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: the hook's pattern matches in the history, nothing pushed, tell me"; exit 1; }
umask 077
sec=$(mktemp)
trap 'rm -f "$sec"' EXIT
for s in objstore-credentials objstore-admin pg-credentials; do
  kubectl -n pg-ha get secret "$s" -o json | jq -r '.data[]' | while read -r v; do printf '%s' "$v" | base64 -d; echo; done
done | awk 'length($0) >= 16' >"$sec"
[ -s "$sec" ] || { echo "STOP: the secret pattern file is empty, nothing pushed"; exit 1; }
printf 'pgbackrest\n' >"$sec.ctl"
[ "$(git grep -F -l -f "$sec.ctl" $revs | wc -l)" -gt 0 ] || { rm -f "$sec.ctl"; echo "STOP: the git grep control found nothing, nothing pushed"; exit 1; }
rm -f "$sec.ctl"
sh=$(git grep -F -l -f "$sec" $revs | wc -l || true)
echo "files in history with a current secret value: $sh (expected 0)"
[ "$sh" = 0 ] || { echo "STOP: a secret value is in the history, nothing pushed, tell me"; exit 1; }
git push origin main
git log --oneline -1 origin/main
)

WORKING LESSONS (from the phase 3 chats)
- Run a test or a check only behind a clean shellcheck, as one gate in the same command block.
- A check that cannot fail proves nothing: give every check a negative control, and assert that its own input is non-empty.
- Leak scans use grep -c or grep -l, never "producer | grep -q" under pipefail. Match whole lines when counting (a plain phrase count once matched a header line twice).
- Compute expected line counts from the text in the block; do not hard-code them. Guards stop with "nothing changed" before any copy.
- The pg image has no grep. pgbackrest verify exits 0 even when its report says status error. Read a tool's real output before you assume its exit codes.
- Several predictions were wrong in phase 3 (line counts, a status count, an exit code). Count your own predictions and mark them.
- A pasted ( ... ) block that ends at a ">" prompt did not run: I press Ctrl-C and tell you. Keep long blocks in two parts if needed.
- No commands with placeholders. Give exact commands, or say one is missing.
